Characteristic Gluing with 𝚲 \Lambda 1. Linearised Einstein equations on four-dimensional spacetimes Volume: 0 Issue: 0
Piotr T. Chruściellabel=e1]piotr.chrusciel@univie.ac.at, Wan Conglabel=e3]wan.cong@univie.ac.at

Abstract
We establish a gluing theorem for linearised vacuum gravitational fields in Bondi gauge on a class of characteristic hypersurfaces in static vacuum four-dimensional backgrounds with cosmological constant Λ ∈ ℝ \Lambda\in\mathbb{R} and arbitrary topology of the compact cross-sections of the null hypersurface. This generalises and complements, in the linearised case, the pioneering analysis of Aretakis, Czimek and Rodnianski, carried-out on light-cones in Minkowski spacetime.

1
Introduction

In their pioneering work
[
1
,
2
,
3
]
, Aretakis, Czimek and Rodnianski presented a gluing construction, along a null hypersurface, of characteristic Cauchy data for the vacuum Einstein equations, for a class of asymptotically Minkowskian data. We wish to generalise their construction to null hypersurfaces with non-spherical sections, and to allow for a cosmological constant, in spacetimes of dimension four or higher.

As a first step towards this, in this paper we consider four-dimensional vacuum Einstein equations, with a cosmological constant
\Lambda\in\mathbb{R}
, linearised at Birmingham-Kottler metrics.
Recall, now, that the analysis in
[
1
,
2
,
3
]
is based on the Christodoulou-Klainerman version of the Newman-Penrose formalism.
Recall also that there exists a well-studied Bondi-type parameterisation of the metric (see
[
4
]
and references therein), which generalises readily to any dimensions. So it appears of interest to analyse the problem in Bondi coordinates, to be able to exploit results which have already been established in the Bondi setting, with an eye-out both for possible advantages of the alternative setting
and for higher dimensions. Indeed,
while we are concerned with four-dimensional spacetimes in this work, we carry-out the higher dimensional construction in a companion paper
[
5
]
.

Interestingly enough, some more work needs to be done in other topologies and dimensions because of different properties of the differential operators involved. In fact, the analysis on null three-dimensional hypersurfaces with spherical cross-sections turns out to be somewhat simpler than the general case.
One of key new aspects of other topologies or dimensions, when compared to null hypersurfaces with two-dimensional spherical sections, is the existence of non-trivial transverse-traceless two-covariant tensors. Their existence leads to new difficulties which need to be addressed. While the collection of TT-tensors is finite-dimensional on two dimensional manifolds, these tensors carry the bulk of information about the geometry in higher dimensions.

To make things precise, we consider the linearisation of the vacuum Einstein equations at a metric

{{g}}=-\big({{\varepsilon}}-{\alpha^{2}}r^{2}{-\frac{2m}{r}}\big)du^{2}-2du\,dr+r^{2}{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}_{AB}dx^{A}dx^{B}\,,

(1)

with

\alpha\in\{0,\sqrt{{\Lambda}/3}\}\subset\mathbb{R}\cup\sqrt{-1}\mathbb{R}\,,\quad m\in\mathbb{R}\,,

where

{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}_{AB}dx^{A}dx^{B}
is a
u
and
r
-independent metric with scalar curvature
2{{\varepsilon}}
, with
{{\varepsilon}}\in\{0,\pm 1\}
.
Roughly speaking, the question addressed here is the following: given two smooth linearised solutions of the vacuum Einstein equations defined near the null hypersurfaces
\{u=0\,,\ r<r_{1}\}
and
\{u=0\,,\ r>r_{2}\}
, where
r_{2}>r_{1}
, can we find characteristic initial data on the missing region
\{u=0\,,\ r_{1}\leq r\leq r_{2}\}
which,
when evolved to a solution of the linearised Einstein equations, provide a linearised metric perturbation which coincides on
\{u=0\}
, together with
u
-derivatives up to order
k
, with the original data.
We refer to this construction as the
{C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}}
-gluing.
The resolution of this problem is presented in Theorem
4.1
, p.
4.1
below, which is the main result of this paper.
This theorem is the key step towards a nonlinear gluing
[
6
]
, where a suitable implicit function theorem is used.

An equivalent way of formulating the gluing problem, advocated in
[
1
]
, is that of connecting two sets of “sphere data” using null-hypersurface data. This perspective can also be taken in our setting, with “sphere data” replaced by suitable linearised data on codimension-two spacelike manifolds, viewed as cross-sections of a null hypersurface.

It was found by Aretakis et al., in the case
\Lambda=0
and
{{{{\varepsilon}}}}=1
, that there exists a ten-parameter family of obstructions to do such a gluing-up-to-gauge, when requiring continuity of two
u
-derivatives of the metric components along the null-hypersurface.
Our analysis shows that the analysis is affected both by the dimension, by the cosmological constant, by the topology of sections of the level sets of
u
(which we assume to be compact), by the mass, and by the number of transverse derivatives which are required to be continuous. In the spherical
four-dimensional case with
m=0
we provide an alternative proof of the corresponding result in
[
2
]
for
{C^{2}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}}
-gluing, with the same number of obstructions.

{C^{2}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}}
-gluing. The radial charges
{\overset{[a]}{Q}}{}
, a=1,2, are defined in
\eqref
24VII22.4, p.

and
\eqref

; the radially-conserved tensor fields
q_{AB}
,

{\overset{[2]}{q}}{}_{AB}
,
and
{\overset{[3,i]}{Q}}{}
are defined in
\eqref
3IX22.1, p.
101
,
\eqref
1XII22.1, p.
160
, and
\eqref
9IX22.9, p.
116
;
{{\mathfrak{g}}}
is the genus of the cross-sections of the characteristic initial data hypersurface; the superscripts
[{{\text{H}}}]
, respectively
[{{\text{TT}}}]
, denote
the
L^{2}
-orthogonal projection on the set of harmonic
1
-forms, respectively on transverse-traceless tensors. On
S^{2}
the four obstructions associated with
{\overset{[2]}{Q}}{}
correspond to spacetime translations, the three obstructions associated with
{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}
when
m\neq 0
correspond to rotations of
S^{2}
, with the further three obstructions arising when
m=0
corresponding to boosts.

lists the obstructions which arise in the linearised gluing depending upon the geometry of the cross-sections of the initial data hypersurface and the mass parameter
m
. A key role in our construction is played by the
radially-constant function
\chi

(cf.
\eqref
24IX22.2, p.
81
below), the existence of which has already been pointed-out in
[
7
]
, and the radially constant fields
q_{AB}
and
{\overset{[3,1]}{Q}}{}_{A}

(cf.
\eqref
3IX22.1, p.
101
and
\eqref
30VII23.1, p.
107
), which do not seem to have been noticed so far in the Bondi gauge,
and which seem to be related to the radially constant fields

{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
,
{\mathcal{Q}}_{3}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{4}
discovered in
[
1
]
.
We point out a slightly different interpretation of the result, namely that the gluing can be performed without obstructions after adding fields, which carry the missing radial charges and which we describe explicitly, to the data on
\{r>r_{2}\}
.
We describe the additional obstructions
that arise for
{C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}}
-gluing,
k\geq 3
, when linearising on a background with
m=0
, see Tables
4.1
, p.
4.1
, and
4.2
, p.
4.2
. We show, with a considerable amount of work, that the higher-order obstructions disappear on backgrounds with
m\neq 0
.

In their introduction, the authors of
[
1
]
discuss several applications of their construction. The results presented here lead immediately to corresponding results for the linearised fields in our setting.

This work is organised as follows: In Section
2
we introduce some of our notations. In Section
3
we analyse the linearised Einstein equations in the Bondi gauge, following
[
7
]
. As already observed in
[
1
,
2
,
3
]
, a key part of the gluing is played by the residual gauges, discussed in Section
3.2
. The
main new element, as compared to
[
7
]
, is Section
3.7
, where inductive formulae for higher-order transverse derivatives are presented. The gluing construction is carried-out in Section
4
. We present our strategy in Section
4.1
, with further details provided in the remaining sections there. In Section
6
we reformulate our gluing result as an unobstructed gluing-with-perturbation problem for the data on
\{r>r_{2}\}
.
Various technical results are presented in the appendices.

We note that the first arxiv version of this work established solvability of the linearised gluing problem through an argument which was inadequate for the full gluing problem, addressed in
[
6
]
, because of insufficient differentiability of solutions of some of the equations used. The current, alternative version resolves these issues.

Acknowledgements.
We are grateful to Finnian Gray for pointing out inaccuracies in a previous version of our manuscript. Useful discussions with Stefan Czimek are acknowledged. We thank the Beijing Institute for Mathematical Sciences and Applications for hospitality and support during part of work on this paper.

2
Notation

Let
{\mathring{\gamma}}={\mathring{\gamma}}_{AB}dx^{A}dx^{B}
be a metric on a
2
-dimensional, compact, orientable manifold
{\mathbf{S}}
, with covariant derivative
{\mathring{D}}
.
We let
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text div}_{(1)}}\,}\!
, respectively
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text div}_{(2)}}\,}\!
, denote the divergence operator on vector fields
\xi
, respectively on two-index tensor fields
h
:

{\operatorname{\mathring{\text div}_{(1)}}\,}\xi:={\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi^{A}\,,\qquad({\operatorname{\mathring{\text div}_{(2)}}\,}h)_{A}:={\mathring{D}}_{B}h^{B}{}_{A}\,.

(2)

Given a function
f
we denote by
f^{[1]}
the
L^{2}
-orthogonal projection of
f
on the constants:

f^{[1]}:=\frac{1}{|{\mathbf{S}}|_{{\mathring{\gamma}}}}\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}fd\mu_{{\mathring{\gamma}}}\,,\quad\text{where}\quad{|{\mathbf{S}}|_{{\mathring{\gamma}}}}=\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}d\mu_{{\mathring{\gamma}}}\,.

(3)

Note that
f^{[1]}
should not be confused with
f^{[=1]}
, which we use when decomposing a function or a tensor field in spherical harmonics on
S^{2}
.
We set

f^{[1^{\perp}]}:=f-f^{[1]}\,.

(4)

Let
{{\text{CKV}}}
,
respectively
{{\text{KV}}}
,
denote the space of conformal Killing vector fields on
{\mathbf{S}}
, respectively Killing vector fields. Thus (cf. Appendix
C.2
),
{{\text{CKV}}}
is six-dimensional on
S^{2}
, consists of covariantly constant vectors on
\mathbb{T}^{2}
, and is trivial on manifolds of higher genus. Given a vector field
\xi
on
{\mathbf{S}}
we denote by
(\xi^{A})^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]}
the
L^{2}
-orthogonal projection on the space
{{\text{CKV}}}
, with

(\xi^{A})^{[{{\text{CKV}}}^{\perp}]}:=\xi^{A}-(\xi^{A})^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]}\,,

with a similar notation for
(\xi^{A})^{[{{\text{KV}}}]}
and
(\xi^{A})^{[{{\text{KV}}}^{\perp}]}
.

We will denote by
{{\text{H}}}
the space of harmonic 1-forms:

{{\text{H}}}=\{\xi_{A}\,|\,{\mathring{D}}^{A}\xi_{A}=0=\epsilon^{AB}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi_{B}\}\,.

(5)

By standard results (cf., e.g.,
[
8
, Theorems 19.11 and 19.14]
or
[
9
, Theorem 18.7]
), the space
{{\text{H}}}
has dimension
2{{\mathfrak{g}}}
on cross-sections
{\mathbf{S}}
with genus
{{\mathfrak{g}}}
, in particular it is trivial on spherical sections. We will denote by
\xi_{A}^{[{{\text{H}}}]}
the
L^{2}
-orthogonal projection of
\xi_{A}
on
{{\text{H}}}
, and by
\xi_{A}^{[{{\text{H}}}^{\perp}]}
the projection on the
L^{2}
-orthogonal to
{{\text{H}}}
.

Let
{{\text{TT}}}
denote the space of transverse-traceless symmetric two tensors:

{{\text{TT}}}=\{h_{AB}\,|\,h_{[AB]}=0={\mathring{\gamma}}^{CD}h_{CD}={\mathring{D}}^{E}h_{EF}\}\,.

(6)

Then
{{\text{TT}}}
is trivial on
S^{2}
, consists of covariantly constant tensors on
\mathbb{T}^{2}
, and is
6({{\mathfrak{g}}}-1)
-dimensional on
two-dimensional manifolds of genus
{{\mathfrak{g}}}\geq 2
(cf., e.g.,
[
10
]
Theorem 8.2
and the paragraph that follows, or
[
11
, Theorem 6.1 and Corollary 6.1]
).

Given a tensor field
h=h_{AB}dx^{A}dx^{B}
we denote by
h_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}
the
L^{2}
-orthogonal projection of
h
on
{{\text{TT}}}
, and set

h_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}:=h_{AB}-h_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\,.

(7)

Clearly, for two-covariant traceless symmetric tensors on
S^{2}
it holds that
h_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}=h_{AB}
, but this is not true anymore for the remaining two-dimensional compact manifolds.

We will often follow terminology and notation from
[
1
]
. In particular, scalar functions, vector fields, and traceless two-covariant symmetric tensors on
S^{2}
will be decomposed into spherical harmonics, see Appendix
C.1
for a summary. The notation
t^{[=\ell]}
will denote the
L^{2}
-orthogonal projection of a tensor
t
on the space of
\ell
-spherical harmonics. Then

t^{[\leq\ell]}=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell}t^{[=i]}\,,\qquad t^{[>\ell]}=t-t^{[\leq\ell]}\,,

(8)

with obvious similar definition of
t^{[<\ell]}
, etc.

One of the operators appearing below is the operator

{\mathring{L}}:={\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}({\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}+2{{{{\varepsilon}}}})

(9)

acting on functions. Its kernel consists of linear combinations of
\ell=0
and
\ell=1
spherical harmonics on
S^{2}
, and of constants on the remaining compact orientable two-dimensional manifolds. Given a function
f
we will write
f^{{[\ker{\mathring{L}}]}}
for the
L^{2}
-orthogonal projection on this kernel, and
f^{{[(\ker{\mathring{L}})^{\perp}]}}
for the projection on its orthogonal.

3
Linearised characteristic constraint equations in Bondi coordinates

Let
({\mycal M},g)
be a
(3+1)
-dimensional spacetime. Locally, near a null hypersurface
for which the optical divergence scalar is non-vanishing,
we can use Bondi-type coordinates
(u,r,x^{A})
in which the metric takes the form

\displaystyle g_{\alpha\beta}dx^{\alpha}dx^{\beta}

\displaystyle=-\frac{V}{r}e^{2\beta}du^{2}-2e^{2\beta}dudr

(10)

\displaystyle\qquad+r^{2}{\gamma}_{AB}\Big(dx^{A}-U^{A}du\Big)\Big(dx^{B}-U^{B}du\Big)\,,

where

\det[\gamma_{AB}]=\det[{\mathring{{\gamma}}}_{AB}]\,,

(11)

with
{\mathring{{\gamma}}}_{AB}(x^{C})
being a metric of constant scalar curvature
2{{\varepsilon}}
. In particular,
\det[\gamma_{AB}]
is
r
and
u
-independent, which implies

\gamma^{AB}\partial_{r}\gamma_{AB}=0\,,\qquad\gamma^{AB}\partial_{u}\gamma_{AB}=0\,.

(12)

As such, the inverse metric reads

g^{\sharp}=e^{-2\beta}\frac{V}{r}\,\partial_{r}^{2}-2e^{-2\beta}\,\partial_{u}\partial_{r}-2e^{-2\beta}U^{A}\,\partial_{r}\partial_{A}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\gamma^{AB}\,\partial_{A}\partial_{B}\,.

(13)

Note that each surface
\{u=
constant
\}
is a null hypersurface with null normal proportional to
\partial_{r}
, and
r
is a parameter which varies along the null generators. Finally, the
x^{C}
’s are local coordinates on the codimension-two surfaces of constant
(u,r)
which, as
r
varies, foliate each null hypersurface of constant
u
.

The restriction of the Einstein equations (E.E.) to a null hypersurface gives a set of null constraint equations for the metric functions
(V,\beta,U^{A},\gamma_{AB})
, which lead to obstructions to the gluing of characteristic data.
In this work we will study the linearised problem around a null hypersurface in a
Birmingham-Kottler background, which includes a Minkowski, anti-de Sitter or de Sitter background.
In Bondi coordinates the background metrics can be written as

{{g}}\equiv{{g}}_{\alpha\beta}dx^{\alpha}dx^{\beta}={g_{uu}}du^{2}-2du\,dr+r^{2}{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}_{AB}dx^{A}dx^{B}\,,

(14)

with

{g_{uu}}:=-\big({{\varepsilon}}-{\alpha^{2}}r^{2}{-\frac{2m}{r}}\big)\,,\quad{{\varepsilon}}\in\{0,\pm 1\}\,,\quad\alpha\in\{0,\sqrt{{\Lambda}/3}\}\,,\quad{m\in\mathbb{R}}\,,

where

{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}_{AB}dx^{A}dx^{B}
is a
u
- and radially constant metric of scalar curvature
2{{\varepsilon}}
,
and note that
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}\cup i\mathbb{R}
: a purely imaginary value of
\alpha
is allowed to accommodate for a cosmological constant
\Lambda<0
. It holds that

g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\beta}=-2\partial_{u}\partial_{r}-{g_{uu}}(\partial_{r})^{2}+r^{-2}{\mathring{{\gamma}}}^{AB}\partial_{A}\partial_{B}\,.

Consider now a perturbation of the metric of the form

g_{\mu\nu}\rightarrow g_{\mu\nu}+\epsilon{h}_{\mu\nu}\,,

(15)

where
\epsilon
should be thought as being very small.
The conditions on the linearised fields such that the perturbed metric is still in the Bondi form to
O(\epsilon)
are,

h_{rA}=h_{rr}={\mathring{{\gamma}}}^{AB}h_{AB}=0\,.

(16)

In what follows for perturbations around a Birmingham-Kottler background, we shall sometimes find it convenient to use fields

\{\delta V,\delta\beta,\delta U_{A}:={\mathring{{\gamma}}}_{AB}\delta U^{B}\}
to denote metric perturbations. These correspond respectively to

\{\delta V+2V\delta\beta,\delta\beta,\delta U_{A}\}\equiv\{-rh_{uu},-h_{ur}/2,-h_{uA}/r^{2}\}\,.

(17)

We will also use the notation

{\check{h}}_{\mu\nu}:={h}_{\mu\nu}/r^{2}\,.

(18)

3.1
The linearised
{C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}}
-gluing problem

One of the key objects that arise in the characteristic gluing construction of
[
1
]
are the “sphere data”. Roughly speaking, these are data that are needed on a cross-section of a characteristic surface for the integration of the transport equations (see below).

Using a Bondi parameterisation of the metric, these data can be defined as follows. Let
{\mycal N}_{I}
be a null hypersurface
\{u=u_{0},r\in I\}
, where
I
is an interval in
\mathbb{R}
, and let
{\mathbf{S}}
be a cross-section of
{\mycal N}
, i.e. a two-dimensional submanifold of
{\mycal N}
meeting each null generator of
{\mathbf{S}}
precisely once.
Let
2\leq k\in\mathbb{N}
be the number of derivatives of the metric that we want to control at
{\mathbf{S}}
. Using the Bondi parameterisation of the metric, we define
linearised Bondi
cross-section data
of order
k
as the collection of fields

\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}}=(\partial_{u}^{\ell}\partial_{r}^{j}{h}_{AB}|_{{\mathbf{S}}},\,\partial_{u}^{\ell}\partial_{r}^{j}\delta\beta|_{{\mathbf{S}}},\,\partial_{u}^{\ell}\partial_{r}^{j}\delta U^{A}|_{{\mathbf{S}}},\,\partial_{u}^{\ell}\partial_{r}^{j}\delta V|_{{\mathbf{S}}})\,,

(19)

for integers
{\ell},j
such that
{\ell}+j\leq k
.
1
1

1

The data
\mathfrak{d}_{\mathbf{S}}
are closely related to the data
\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[{\mathbf{S}},k]
of
[
12
, Section 5]
. As discussed in more detail there, some of the fields in (
19
) are not independent, but this is irrelevant for our purposes.

For simplicity we assume that all the fields in (
19
)
are smooth, though a finite sufficiently large degree of differentiability would suffice for our purposes, as can be verified by chasing the number of derivatives in the relevant equations; compare Section
3.6
below.

A natural threshold for the gluing is
k=2
,
as then one expects
existence of an associated space-time solving the vacuum Einstein equations when the fields are sufficiently differentiable in directions tangent to
{\mathbf{S}}
(cf.
[
13
]
for a small data result in a different gauge; see
[
14
,
15
,
16
]
for existence without smallness restrictions under more stringent differentiability conditions).
In the linearised
{C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}}
-gluing problem we start with two sections
{\mathbf{S}}_{1}
and
{\mathbf{S}}_{2}\subset J^{+}({\mathbf{S}}_{1})
of a null hypersurface
\{u=0\}
equipped with Bondi coordinates as in (
14
), each with constant
r
,
and their linearised Bondi cross-section data of order
k
,
\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}
and

\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}
. The goal is to interpolate between
\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}
and
\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}
along a null hypersurface
{\mycal N}_{[r_{1},r_{2}]}
such that (i)
\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}
agrees with the restriction to
r_{1}
of the interpolating field along
{\mycal N}_{[r_{1},r_{2}]}
; (ii)
\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}
agrees with the restriction to
r=r_{2}
of the interpolating field; and (iii) the constructed field satisfies the linearised null constraint equations. We shall see in Section
3.3
how the linearised null constraint equations lead to obstructions to the gluing.

Since linearised Bondi data are defined up to linearised gauge transformations, we shall use these transformations to help us with the gluing.

3.2
Gauge freedom

Recall that linearised gravitational fields are defined up to a gauge transformation

h\mapsto h+{\mathcal{L}}_{\zeta}g

(20)

determined by a vector field
{\zeta}
. Once the metric perturbation has been put into Bondi gauge, there remains the freedom to make gauge transformations which preserve this gauge:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{{\zeta}}g_{{r}{r}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\,,

(21)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{{\zeta}}g_{{r}A}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\,,

(22)

\displaystyle g^{AB}{\mathcal{L}}_{{\zeta}}g_{AB}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\,,

(23)

For the metric (
14
) this is solved by (cf., e.g.,
[
7
]
)

\displaystyle{\zeta}^{{u}}(u,r,x^{A})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\xi}^{{u}}({u},x^{A})\,,

(24)

\displaystyle{\zeta}^{B}(u,r,x^{A})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\xi}^{B}({u},x^{A})-\frac{1}{{r}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\xi}^{{u}}({u},x^{A})\,,\phantom{xxxxxx}

(25)

\displaystyle{\zeta}^{{r}}(u,r,x^{A})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}{r}{\mathring{D}}_{B}{\xi}^{B}(u,x^{A})+\frac{1}{2}{\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}{\xi}^{{u}}({u},x^{A})\,,

(26)

for some fields
{\xi}^{u}({u},x^{A})
,
{\xi}^{B}({u},x^{A})
, and where
{\mathring{D}}_{A}
and
{\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}
are respectively the covariant derivative and the Laplacian operator associated with the two-dimensional metric
{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}_{AB}
appearing in (
14
).

We define

{\mathring{\mathcal{L}}}_{\zeta}

to be the Lie-derivation in the
x^{A}
-variables
with respect to the vector field
{\zeta}^{A}\partial_{A}
.

The transformation (
20
) can be viewed as a result of linearised coordinate transformation to new coordinates
\tilde{x}^{\mu}
such that

x^{\mu}=\tilde{x}^{\mu}+\epsilon\zeta^{\mu}(\tilde{x}^{\mu})\,,

(27)

where
\epsilon
is as in (
15
).
Writing
{g_{uu}}
as

{g_{uu}}=-{{\varepsilon}}+{\alpha^{2}}r^{2}+\frac{2m}{r}=:\varepsilon N^{2}\,,\ \mbox{where $\varepsilon\in\{\pm 1\}$,}

under (
27
), the linearised metric perturbation transforms as

\displaystyle{h}_{uA}\to

\displaystyle\ \tilde{{h}}_{uA}={h}_{uA}+{\mycal L}_{\zeta}g_{uA}

(28)

\displaystyle={h}_{uA}+\partial_{A}(\epsilon N^{2}\zeta^{u}-\zeta^{r})+r^{2}{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}_{AB}\partial_{u}\zeta^{B}

\displaystyle={h}_{uA}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{A}\,[\,({\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}\xi^{u}+2{{\varepsilon}}\xi^{u})-r({\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{B}-2\partial_{u}\xi^{u})]

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+r^{2}\big({{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}_{AB}\partial_{u}\xi^{B}+\big({\alpha^{2}+\frac{2m}{r^{3}}\big)}\partial_{A}\xi^{u}\big)\,,

\displaystyle{h}_{ur}\to

\displaystyle\ \tilde{{h}}_{ur}={h}_{ur}+{\mycal L}_{\zeta}g_{ur}={h}_{ur}-\partial_{u}\zeta^{u}+\epsilon N^{2}\partial_{r}\zeta^{u}-\partial_{r}\zeta^{r}

(29)

\displaystyle={h}_{ur}-\partial_{u}\xi^{u}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\xi_{A}\,,

\displaystyle{h}_{uu}\to

\displaystyle\ \tilde{h}_{uu}={h}_{uu}+{\mycal L}_{\zeta}g_{uu}={h}_{uu}+\epsilon{\zeta}^{{r}}\partial_{r}N^{2}+2\partial_{u}(\epsilon N^{2}\zeta^{u}-\zeta^{r})

(30)

\displaystyle={h}_{uu}-(2{{\varepsilon}}+{\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}})\partial_{u}\xi^{u}+r\big({\mathring{D}}_{B}\partial_{u}\xi^{B}+\big({\alpha^{2}-\frac{m}{r^{3}}\big)}{\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}\xi^{u}\big)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\big({\alpha^{2}}r^{2}+\frac{2m}{r}\big)(2\partial_{u}\xi^{u})-\big({\alpha^{2}}r^{2}-\frac{m}{r}\big){\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{B}\,,

\displaystyle{h}_{AB}\to

\displaystyle\ \tilde{h}_{AB}={h}_{AB}+{\mycal L}_{\zeta}g_{AB}={h}_{AB}+2r{\zeta}^{{r}}{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}_{AB}+r^{2}{\mathring{\mathcal{L}}}_{{\zeta}}{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}_{AB}\,,

(31)

with

\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[X_{AB}]:=\frac{1}{2}(X_{AB}+X_{BA}-{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}^{CD}X_{CD}{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}_{AB})

denoting
the traceless symmetric part of a tensor on a section
{\mathbf{S}}
.

Given
{\mathbf{S}}_{u_{0},r_{0}}
corresponding to a
\{u=u_{0},r=r_{0}\}
section of some
{\mycal N}
, equations (
28
)-(
31
) together with all their

u
- and
r
-derivatives up to order
k
define a new set of order-
k
cross-section data
on

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathbf{S}}}_{u_{0},r_{0}}:

\displaystyle=\{\tilde{u}=u_{0},\tilde{r}=r_{0}\}

\displaystyle=\{u=u_{0}+\epsilon\zeta^{u}(u_{0},r_{0},x^{A}),r=r_{0}+\epsilon\zeta^{r}(u_{0},r_{0},x^{A})\}\,,

a section lying close to the original
{\mathbf{S}}_{u_{0},r_{0}}
, in terms of the gauge fields

\{\partial^{i}_{u}\xi^{B}|_{u=u_{0}},\partial^{i}_{u}\xi^{u}|_{u=u_{0}}\}_{0\leq i\leq 3}

as well as the original metric perturbations evaluated on
\tilde{{\mathbf{S}}}_{u_{0},r_{0}}
.

Equation (
29
) shows that we can always choose
\zeta
so that

\tilde{h}_{ur}=0\,.

(32)

After having done this, we are left with a residual set of gauge transformations, defined by a
u
-parameterised family of vector fields
{\xi}^{A}(u,\cdot)
, and
\xi^{u}(u,\cdot)
, with the condition

\partial_{u}\xi^{u}(u,x^{A})=\frac{{\mathring{D}}_{B}{\xi}^{B}(u,x^{A})}{2}\,

(33)

needed to preserve the gauge
\tilde{h}_{ur}=0
.

Under the residual gauge transformations with (
33
), the transformed fields take the form

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{uA}

\displaystyle={h}_{uA}-\frac{1}{2}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}\xi^{u}+\epsilon N^{2}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi^{u}+r^{2}\partial_{u}\xi_{A}

(34)

\displaystyle={h}_{uA}-\frac{1}{2}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\,[\,({\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}\xi^{u}+2{{\varepsilon}}\xi^{u})]

\displaystyle+r^{2}\big[{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}_{AB}\partial_{u}\xi^{B}+{\big({\alpha^{2}}+\frac{2m}{r^{3}}\big)}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi^{u}\big]\,,

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{uu}

\displaystyle={h}_{uu}+r\big[{\big({\alpha^{2}}-\frac{m}{r^{3}}\big)}{\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}\xi^{u}+{\mathring{D}}_{B}\partial_{u}{\xi}^{B}\big]

(35)

\displaystyle\quad-\big({{\varepsilon}}+\frac{1}{2}{\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}-\frac{3m}{r}\big){\mathring{D}}_{B}{\xi}^{B}\,,

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{AB}

\displaystyle={h}_{AB}+2r^{2}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi_{B}]-2r\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\zeta^{u}]\,.

(36)

Let
\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}
and
\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}
be linearised Bondi cross-section data of
order
k

on
{\mathbf{S}}_{1}
and
{\mathbf{S}}_{2}
respectively. Given gauge fields

\{\partial_{u}^{i}\xi^{B}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{a}},\partial_{u}^{i}\xi^{u}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{a}}\}_{0\leq i\leq k+1\,,1\leq a\leq 2}\,,

the associated transformed Bondi cross-section data are given by (
28
)-(
31
) and their
\partial_{u}
and
\partial_{r}
derivatives. In the linearised gluing problem, we shall allow for such gauge transformations to the data; that is, we consider gluing along a null hypersurface of the transformed data
\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}_{\tilde{{\mathbf{S}}}_{1}}
and
\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}_{\tilde{{\mathbf{S}}}_{2}}
with the freedom of choosing gauge fields to achieve the gluing. We shall call this gluing-up-to-gauge.

To simplify notation we will write

\displaystyle{L_{1}}(\xi^{u})_{A}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{2}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\,[\,{\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}\xi^{u}+2{{\varepsilon}}\xi^{u}]=-{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{u}]\,,

(37)

\displaystyle C(\zeta)_{AB}

\displaystyle:=\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}\zeta_{B}]\,,

(38)

\displaystyle{L_{2}}(\xi)

\displaystyle:=-\left({{\varepsilon}}+\frac{1}{2}{\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}\right){\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{B}\,.

(39)

For further convenience we note the transformation laws, in this notation and for
i\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{uA}

\displaystyle={h}_{uA}+{L_{1}}(\xi^{u})_{A}+r^{2}\big(\partial_{u}\xi_{A}+\big({\alpha^{2}}+\frac{2m}{r^{3}}\big){\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi^{u}\big)\,,

(40)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}^{i}\tilde{h}_{uA}

\displaystyle=\partial_{u}^{i}{h}_{uA}+\frac{1}{2}{L_{1}}({\mathring{D}}_{B}\partial_{u}^{i-1}\xi^{B})_{A}

(41)

\displaystyle\quad+r^{2}\big[\partial_{u}^{i+1}\xi_{A}+\frac{1}{2}{\big({\alpha^{2}}+\frac{2m}{r^{3}}\big)}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\partial_{u}^{i-1}\xi^{B}\big]\,,

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{uu}

\displaystyle={h}_{uu}+r\big[{\big({\alpha^{2}}-\frac{m}{r^{3}}\big)}{\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}\xi^{u}+{\mathring{D}}_{B}\partial_{u}{\xi}^{B}\big]+{L_{2}}(\xi)

(42)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{3m}{r}{\mathring{D}}_{B}{\xi}^{B}\,,

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{AB}

\displaystyle={h}_{AB}+2r^{2}C(\zeta)_{AB}

(43)

\displaystyle={h}_{AB}+2r^{2}C(\xi)_{AB}-2r\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{u}]\,,

\displaystyle\partial_{u}^{i}\tilde{h}_{AB}

\displaystyle=\partial_{u}^{i}{h}_{AB}+2r^{2}C(\partial_{u}^{i}\xi)_{AB}

(44)

\displaystyle\quad-r\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}{\mathring{D}}_{C}\partial_{u}^{i-1}\xi^{C}]\,,

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{A}\tilde{h}_{uA}

\displaystyle={\mathring{D}}^{A}{h}_{uA}-\frac{1}{2}{\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}\,({\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}+2{{\varepsilon}})\xi^{u}

(45)

\displaystyle\quad+r^{2}\big[{\mathring{D}}_{A}\partial_{u}\xi^{A}+\big({\alpha^{2}}+\frac{2m}{r^{3}}\big){\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}\xi^{u}\big]\,,

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{B}\tilde{h}_{AB}

\displaystyle={\mathring{D}}^{B}{h}_{AB}+r^{2}({\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}+{{\varepsilon}})\xi_{B}-r{\mathring{D}}_{A}({\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}+2{{\varepsilon}})\xi^{u}

(46)

\displaystyle={\mathring{D}}^{B}{h}_{AB}+r^{2}({\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}+{{\varepsilon}})\xi_{A}+2r{L_{1}}(\xi^{u})_{A}\,,

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\tilde{h}_{AB}

\displaystyle={\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{h}_{AB}+r^{2}({\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}+2{{\varepsilon}}){\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi^{A}

(47)

\displaystyle\quad-r{\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}({\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}+2{{\varepsilon}})\xi^{u}

\displaystyle={\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{h}_{AB}-2r^{2}{L_{2}}(\xi)-r{\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}({\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}+2{{\varepsilon}})\xi^{u}\,.

3.3
Null constraint equations

We now turn our attention to Einstein equations,

G_{\mu\nu}:=R_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}R=8\pi T_{\mu\nu}-\Lambda g_{\mu\nu}

(48)

and their linearisation in Bondi coordinates.

3.3.1
h_{ur}

The
G_{rr}
component of the Einstein tensor, which we reproduce from
[
4
]
, reads:

\frac{r}{4}G_{rr}=\partial_{r}\beta-\frac{r}{16}{\gamma}^{AC}{\gamma}^{BD}(\partial_{r}{\gamma}_{AB})(\partial_{r}{\gamma}_{CD})\,.

(49)

Since the right-hand side of (
49
) is quadratic in
\partial_{r}\gamma_{AB}
, after linearising in vacuum we find

\partial_{r}\delta\beta=0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\delta\beta=\delta\beta(u,x^{A})\,.

(50)

Using a terminology somewhat similar to that of
[
1
]
, we thus obtain a pointwise radial conservation law for
\delta\beta
, and an apparent obstruction to gluing: two linearised fields can be glued together if and only if their Bondi functions
\delta\beta
coincide.

However, it follows from (
32
) that we can always choose a gauge so that
\delta\beta\equiv 0
.
Thus, (
50
) does not lead to an obstruction for gluing-up-to-gauge. Hence, when gluing, we will always use the gauge where
\delta\beta=0
.
As such,
in the current section we will
not
assume
\delta\beta=0

unless explicitly indicated otherwise.

3.3.2
h_{uA}

From the
G_{rA}
-component of the Einstein equations one has

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\left[r^{4}e^{-2\beta}{\gamma}_{AB}(\partial_{r}U^{B})\right]=2r^{4}\partial_{r}\Big(\frac{1}{r^{2}}{D}_{A}\beta\Big)

(51)

\displaystyle\qquad-r^{2}{\gamma}^{EF}{D}_{E}(\partial_{r}{\gamma}_{AF})+16\pi r^{2}T_{rA}\,.

The linearisation of
G_{rA}
at a Birmingham-Kottler metric reads

\displaystyle 2r^{2}\delta G_{rA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\left[r^{4}{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}_{AB}(\partial_{r}\delta U^{B})\right]-2r^{4}\partial_{r}\Big(\frac{1}{r^{2}}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta\beta\Big)

(52)

\displaystyle+r^{2}\partial_{r}\left(r^{-2}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\right)\,.

The linearised vacuum Einstein equation thus gives

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\left[r^{4}\partial_{r}(r^{-2}h_{uA})+2r^{2}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta\beta\right]=8r{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta\beta+{\mathring{D}}^{B}r^{2}\partial_{r}\left(r^{-2}h_{AB}\right)\,.

(53)

Integration of this transport equation gives us a representation formula for
\partial_{r}{h}_{uA}
:

\displaystyle\left[s^{4}\partial_{s}(s^{-2}h_{uA})+2s^{2}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta\beta\right]_{s=r_{1}}^{r}=\int_{r_{1}}^{r}8s{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta\beta+{\mathring{D}}^{B}s^{2}\partial_{s}\left(s^{-2}h_{AB}\right)\,ds\,.

(54)

In the gauge
\delta\beta=0
, and after performing an integration by parts on the right-hand side, this can be written as,

\displaystyle r^{4}\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{uA}|_{r}=r_{1}^{4}\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{uA}|_{r_{1}}+\big[{\mathring{D}}^{B}{h}_{AB}\big]_{r_{1}}^{r}-2\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\hat{\kappa}_{1}(s){\mathring{D}}^{B}{h}_{AB}\,ds

(55)

where we have defined,

\displaystyle\hat{\kappa}_{1}(s):=\frac{1}{s}\,.

(56)

Given
\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}
and
\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}
, equation (
55
) evaluated at
r=r_{2}
gives a condition for the field
{h}_{AB}(r)
, where
r\in(r_{1},r_{2})
which has to hold when constructing the solution to the gluing problem on
{\mycal N}_{[r_{1},r_{2}]}
.

Now, the cokernel of the operator
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!

{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}:\varphi_{AB}\mapsto{\mathring{D}}^{B}\varphi_{AB}

acting on traceless symmetric tensors
\varphi_{AB}
, and which appears in (
53
) in front of
h_{AB}
, is spanned by solutions of the system

\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}\pi_{B}]=0\,,

(57)

with
\pi_{A}=\pi_{A}(u,x^{B})
. The space of solutions of (
57
) is the space of conformal Killing vector fields, which we denote by
CKV
. This space is six-dimensional on
S^{2}
, and is isomorphic to the Lie algebra of the Lorentz group. On a two-dimensional torus
\mathbb{T}^{2}
, solutions of (
57
) belong to the two-dimensional space of covariantly constant vectors. Finally, the space of solutions of (
57
) on a two-dimensional negatively curved compact manifold is trivial; cf . Appendix
C.2
.

The projection of (
53
) onto
\pi_{A}
in the gauge
\delta\beta=0
gives

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\pi^{A}\left[r^{4}\partial_{r}(r^{-2}h_{uA})\right]\,d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\pi^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\big[r^{2}\partial_{r}\left(r^{-2}{h}_{AB}\right)\big]\,d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}

(58)

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}^{B}\pi^{A}]\big(r^{2}\partial_{r}\left(r^{-2}{h}_{AB}\right)\big)\,d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}

\displaystyle=0\,,

and thus the integrals

\displaystyle{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}(\pi^{A})[{\mathbf{S}}]:=\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\pi^{A}\left[r^{4}\partial_{r}(r^{-2}h_{uA})\right]\,d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}

(59)

form a family of
radially conserved charges
, with

\partial_{r}{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}=0

along any
u=
constant null hypersurfaces with the gauge choice
\delta\beta=0
.

This leads to a six-dimensional family of obstructions to gluing on
S^{2}
, two-dimensional on
\mathbb{T}^{2}
, and no obstructions on null surfaces with sections of higher-genus.

We shall denote the dependence of

{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}
on
\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}}
as
{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}={\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}}]
. Thus in the gauge
\delta\beta=0
,
to achieve gluing of
\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}
and
\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}
, it must hold that

{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}]={\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}]\,.

(60)

Indeed,
it follows from Appendix
C.3
, p.
C.3

that (
60
) is a necessary and sufficient condition for
r_{2}^{4}\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{uA}|_{{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}-r_{2}^{4}\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{uA}|_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}
to lie in the image of the operator
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!
acting on traceless symmetric tensors, or equivalently, for the existence of a solution
\tilde{\varphi}_{AB}(x^{C})
to the equation

\displaystyle r_{2}^{4}\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{uA}|_{{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}

\displaystyle=r_{1}^{4}\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{uA}|_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}-{\mathring{D}}^{B}{h}_{AB}|_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}-{\mathring{D}}^{B}\tilde{\varphi}_{AB}\,.

(61)

The gluing condition (
55
) evaluated at
r=r_{2}
can thus be achieved by interpolating
{h}_{AB}
on
{\mycal N}_{(r_{1},r_{2})}
so that

\langle{h}_{AB},\hat{\kappa}_{1}\rangle=\tilde{\varphi}_{AB}\,,

(62)

where
\tilde{\varphi}_{AB}
is the solution to (
61
), and where we write, for
f,h:(r_{1},r_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,

\langle f,h\rangle:=\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}f(s)h(s)ds\,.

Under the gauge transformation (
34
),
{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}
transforms as

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}

\displaystyle\pi^{A}\left(r^{4}\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{uA}\right)\,d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}

(63)

\displaystyle\to\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\pi^{A}r^{4}\partial_{r}\bigg({\check{h}}_{uA}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}{L_{1}}(\xi^{u})_{A}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+({{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}_{AB}\partial_{u}\xi^{B}+{\big(\alpha^{2}+\frac{2m}{r^{3}}\big)}\partial_{A}\xi^{u})\bigg)\,d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\pi^{A}\bigg(r^{4}\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{uA}+{2}{r}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\,\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{u}]-{6m\partial_{A}\xi^{u}}\bigg)\,d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\big(\pi^{A}r^{4}\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{uA}+{6m\xi^{u}}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\pi^{A}\big)\,d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}\,.

So on
S^{2}
, if
m=0

we see that
{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}
is gauge invariant, hence

{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}]={\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}]\quad\iff\quad{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}]={\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}]\,.

If
m\neq 0
,
{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}
is invariant under gauge transformations for which
{\mathring{D}}_{A}\pi^{A}
vanishes; these generate rotations of
S^{2}
.

On the remaining topologies we have
{\mathring{D}}_{A}\pi^{A}=0
, so that the charges
{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}
are gauge-invariant independently of whether or not the mass parameter
m
vanishes.

Now, let
\psi_{A}
denote (compare (
52
)),

\displaystyle\psi_{A}:=-2r^{4}\partial_{r}\Big(\frac{1}{r^{2}}{D}_{A}\delta\beta\Big)+r^{2}\partial_{r}\left(r^{-2}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\right)\,.

(64)

Integrating (
52
) in
r
twice one obtains a representation formula for
{h}_{uA}
:

\displaystyle h_{uA}(u,r,x^{B})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r^{2}{\mu}_{A}(u,x^{B})+\frac{{\lambda}_{A}(u,x^{B})}{r}

(65)

\displaystyle-r^{2}\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\psi_{A}(u,s,x^{B})\left(\frac{1}{3r^{3}}-\frac{1}{3s^{3}}\right)ds\,,

with
{\mu}_{A}
and
{\lambda}_{A}
determined by
h_{uA}(u,r_{1},x^{B})
and
\partial_{r}h_{uA}(u,r_{1},x^{B})
.

The part of (
65
) involving
{h}_{AB}
can be viewed as the following map:

\displaystyle h_{AB}

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle-r^{2}\int_{r_{1}}^{r}s^{2}\partial_{s}\left(s^{-2}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\right)\left(\frac{1}{3r^{3}}-\frac{1}{3s^{3}}\right)ds

(66)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{r^{2}}{3}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\left[\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\partial_{s}\left(s^{-2}h_{AB}\right)\left(\frac{s^{2}}{r^{3}}-\frac{1}{s}\right)ds\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{r^{2}}{3}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\Big[h_{AB}(u,s,x^{A})\left(\frac{1}{r^{3}}-\frac{1}{s^{3}}\right)\Big|_{r_{1}}^{r}\phantom{\int_{r_{1}}^{r}}

\displaystyle-\int_{r_{1}}^{r}h_{AB}\left(\frac{2}{sr^{3}}+\frac{1}{s^{4}}\right)ds\Big]\,.

When
\delta\beta\equiv 0
we thus obtain

\displaystyle h_{uA}(u,r,x^{B})

\displaystyle=r^{2}{\mu}_{A}(u,x^{B})+\frac{{\lambda}_{A}(u,x^{B})}{r}

(67)

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}(u,r_{1},x^{A})\left(\frac{r}{3}-\frac{r^{2}}{3r_{1}^{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{r^{2}}{3}\int_{r_{1}}^{r}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\left(\frac{2}{sr^{3}}+\frac{1}{s^{4}}\right)ds\,.

For future use we will track the differentiability orders of the fields involved.
Denoting the Sobolev spaces over
{\mathbf{S}}
as
H_{k_{U}}
for
\delta U^{A}\equiv-r^{-2}{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}^{AB}h_{uB}
, and
H_{k_{\gamma}}
for
\delta\gamma_{AB}\equiv\check{h}_{AB}=r^{-2}h_{AB}
,
Equation (
67
) implies

k_{\gamma}\geq k_{U}+1\,.

(68)

We emphasise that these spaces keep only track of the differentiability in directions tangent to
{\mathbf{S}}
at given
r
, with no information concerning the behaviour in the
r
-direction. But note that in our setup all fields on the interpolating initial data hypersurface are smooth in the
r
-variable.

3.3.3
{h}_{uu}

To obtain the transport equation for the function
V
occurring in the Bondi form of the metric, it turns out to be convenient to consider the expression for
2G_{ur}+2U^{A}G_{rA}-V/r\,G_{rr}
:

\displaystyle r^{2}e^{-2\beta}(2G_{ur}+2U^{A}G_{rA}-V/r\,G_{rr})=R[{\gamma}]-2{\gamma}^{AB}\Big[{D}_{A}{D}_{B}\beta

(69)

\displaystyle\qquad+({D}_{A}\beta)({D}_{B}\beta)\Big]+\frac{e^{-2\beta}}{r^{2}}{D}_{A}\Big[\partial_{r}(r^{4}U^{A})\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}r^{4}e^{-4\beta}{\gamma}_{AB}(\partial_{r}U^{A})(\partial_{r}U^{B})-2e^{-2\beta}\partial_{r}V\,,

(It follows directly from the definition of
G_{\mu\nu}
and the Bondi parametrisation of the metric that
r^{2}e^{-2\beta}(2G_{ur}+2U^{A}G_{rA}-V/r\,G_{rr})
can equivalently be written as
r^{2}g^{AB}R_{AB}
; compare Appendix
D
).

\bullet
3.1

†
†
margin:

\!\!\!\!\!\!\,\bullet
3.1:
ptc:

remove this, never needed nor used except for clarity, but has a term missing; alternatively add a commutator term

In vacuum
this can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\partial_{r}(V-\frac{r^{2}}{2}{D}_{A}U^{A})=\frac{e^{2\beta}}{2}\Big\{R[{\gamma}]-2{\gamma}^{AB}\Big[{D}_{A}{D}_{B}\beta+({D}_{A}\beta)({D}_{B}\beta)\Big]

(70)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}r^{4}e^{-4\beta}{\gamma}_{AB}(\partial_{r}U^{A})(\partial_{r}U^{B})-2\Lambda r^{2}\Big\}+r{D}_{A}U^{A}\,.

Let
{\mathring{R}}_{AB}={{\varepsilon}}{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}_{AB}
denote the Ricci tensor of the metric
{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}_{AB}
. As
h_{AB}
is
{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}
-traceless we have

\displaystyle r^{2}\delta(R[{\gamma}])|_{{\gamma}={{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}

\displaystyle=-{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}_{A}({{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}^{BC}h_{BC})+{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}-{\mathring{R}}^{AB}h_{AB}

(71)

\displaystyle={\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\,.

Linearising (
70
)

\bullet
3.2

†
†
margin:

\!\!\!\!\!\!\,\bullet
3.2:
ptc:

change crossref to (
69
) if equation removed

around a Birmingham-Kottler background thus gives

\displaystyle\partial_{r}(\delta V-\frac{r^{2}}{2}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta U^{A})=\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Big\{{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\check{h}}_{AB}-2{\gamma}^{AB}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\delta\beta\Big\}

(72)

\displaystyle\quad+r{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta U^{A}+2({{{{\varepsilon}}}}-r^{2}\Lambda)\delta\beta\,.

We note that since
\delta(G_{ur}+U^{A}G_{rA})=\delta G_{ur}
, (
72
) is equivalent to the equation
r^{2}\delta G_{ur}=r^{2}\Lambda{h}_{ur}
.

In the
\delta\beta=0
gauge, Equation (
72
) provides another family of radially conserved charges:

\displaystyle{\overset{[2]}{Q}}{}(\lambda):=\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\lambda\bigg[\delta V-\frac{r}{2}\partial_{r}\bigg(r^{2}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\delta U_{A}\bigg)\bigg]d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}\,,

(73)

where the functions
\lambda(x^{A})
are solutions of the equation

\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\lambda]=0\,.

(74)

The only solutions of this equation on a torus or on a higher genus manifold are constants. On
S^{2}
such
\lambda
’s are linear combinations of
\ell=0
or
\ell=1
spherical harmonics
[
17
]
.
We thus obtain another four-dimensional family of obstructions on
S^{2}
, and a one-dimensional family of obstructions in the remaining topologies.

The conservation equation
\partial_{r}{\overset{[2]}{Q}}{}=0
is the consequence of
an identity, already observed in
[
7
]
, of the form

\delta G_{ur}-\frac{1}{r}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\delta G_{rA}=\partial_{r}(....)\,,

(75)

which can be derived as follows:

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\bigg[\delta V

\displaystyle-\frac{r}{2}\partial_{r}\bigg(r^{2}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\delta U_{A}\bigg)\bigg]=\partial_{r}\delta V-\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{r}{2}\partial_{r}\bigg(r^{2}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\delta U_{A}\bigg)\bigg)

(76)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\partial_{r}\delta V-2r{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta U^{A}-\frac{r^{2}}{2}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\partial_{r}\delta U^{A}}_{=1/2{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\check{h}}_{AB}}

\displaystyle\qquad\underbrace{-\frac{1}{2}(r^{3}\partial_{r}^{2}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta U^{A}+4r^{2}\partial_{r}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta U^{A})}_{=r/2\partial_{r}\big({\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\check{h}}_{AB}\big)}

\displaystyle={\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\bigg[\frac{1}{2}{\check{h}}_{AB}+\frac{r}{2}\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{AB}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\bigg(r{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\check{h}}_{AB}\bigg)\,.

Hence

\partial_{r}{\overset{[2]}{Q}}{}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\lambda\partial_{r}\big(r{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\check{h}}_{AB}\big)\,d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}=0.

(77)

Under a gauge transformation the radial charge
{\overset{[2]}{Q}}{}
transforms as

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\lambda\Big[\delta V+\frac{r}{2}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\partial_{r}{h}_{uA}\Big]\,d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}

(78)

\displaystyle\to\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\lambda\Big[\delta V+r\left({{\varepsilon}}+\frac{1}{2}{\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}-\frac{3m}{r}\right)\big({\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{B}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad-r^{2}\big[{\mathring{D}}_{B}\partial_{u}\xi^{B}+{\big(\alpha^{2}-\frac{m}{r^{3}}\big)}{\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}\xi^{u}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{r}{2}\Big({\mathring{D}}^{A}\partial_{r}{h}_{uA}+2r\big[{\mathring{D}}_{B}\partial_{u}\xi^{B}+{\big(\alpha^{2}-\frac{m}{r^{3}}\big)}{\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}\xi^{u}\big]\Big)\Big]\,d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\lambda\Big[\delta V+\frac{r}{2}\partial_{r}{\mathring{D}}^{A}{h}_{uA}\Big]\,d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\lambda\Big[r\left({{\varepsilon}}+\frac{1}{2}{\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}-\frac{3m}{r}\right)\big({\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{B}\big)\Big]\,d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}\,.

Taking
{\mathring{D}}^{A}
of (
74
) gives,

{\mathring{D}}_{B}{\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}\lambda=-2{\mathring{R}}_{AB}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\lambda\,,

(79)

where
{\mathring{R}}_{AB}
is the Ricci tensor of the metric
{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}
.
Inserting this into (
78
) gives

\displaystyle{\overset{[2]}{Q}}{}\mapsto\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\lambda\Big[\delta V+\frac{r}{2}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\partial_{r}{h}_{uA}\Big]d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}-3m\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\lambda{\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{B}\,.

(80)

Therefore, a)
{\overset{[2]}{Q}}{}{}(\lambda)
is gauge invariant
when
m=0
for all
\lambda
’s satisfying (
74
), while b)
when
m\neq 0
, only
{\overset{[2]}{Q}}{}{}(\lambda^{[1]})
is gauge invariant.

As already pointed out, (
76
) takes the form of a pointwise radial conservation law:

\partial_{r}\chi=0\,,

where

\displaystyle\chi

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle-\delta V+\frac{r}{2}\partial_{r}\Big(r^{2}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\delta U_{A}\Big)+\frac{1}{2}r{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\check{h}}_{AB}

(81)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\delta V-\frac{r}{2}\partial_{r}{\mathring{D}}^{A}h_{uA}+\frac{1}{2r}{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\,.

We note that (
72
) can be used to rewrite
\chi
as (cf.
[
7
, Equation (D.4)]
)

\chi=-r^{2}\partial_{r}{h}_{uu}+{\mathring{D}}^{A}{h}_{uA}\,.

(82)

Under gauge transformations
\chi
transforms as

\chi\mapsto\chi-\frac{1}{2}\underbrace{({\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}+2{{\varepsilon}}){\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}{\xi}^{{u}}}_{={\mathring{L}}({\xi}^{u})}+3m{\mathring{D}}^{B}\xi_{B}\,.

(83)

This shows that, on
S^{2}
,
\chi^{{[(\ker{\mathring{L}})^{\perp}]}}=\chi^{[\geq 2]}

can be made to achieve any desired value by
a suitable choice of
({\xi}^{u})^{[\geq 2]}
.
For the remaining topologies this is the case for
\chi^{{[(\ker{\mathring{L}})^{\perp}]}}=\chi^{{[1^{\perp}]}}
using
({\xi}^{u})^{{[1^{\perp}]}}
.

It further follows from (
83
) that the projection
\chi^{[1]}
is gauge invariant for all topologies regardless of
m
. Moreover, when
m=0
, the projection
\chi^{[\leq 1]}
is also gauge-invariant on
S^{2}
.
These projections
are determined by the radial charge (
73
), i.e.

\displaystyle{\overset{[2]}{Q}}{}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=r\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\lambda\big[-h_{uu}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}{\mathring{D}}^{A}h_{uA}\big]d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}

(84)

\displaystyle=-r\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\lambda h_{uu}d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}+\frac{r}{2}\partial_{r}\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\lambda{\mathring{D}}^{A}h_{uA}d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}\,,

where
\lambda
is a linear combination of
\ell=0
and
\ell=1
spherical harmonics
on
S^{2}
,
and is a constant in the remaining cases,
as follows:
Recall that

\displaystyle{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}({\mathring{D}}\lambda)

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\lambda\left[r^{4}\partial_{r}(r^{-2}h_{uA})\right]\,d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}

(85)

\displaystyle=-\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\lambda\left[r^{4}\partial_{r}(r^{-2}{\mathring{D}}^{A}h_{uA})\right]\,d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}

\displaystyle=-r^{4}\partial_{r}\big(r^{-2}\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\lambda{\mathring{D}}^{A}h_{uA}\,d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}\big)\,,

see (
59
).
Integrating (
85
) over
{\mathbf{S}}
shows that there exists a function
{\overset{[1]}{{C}}}(u,x^{A})
such that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\lambda{\mathring{D}}^{A}h_{uA}\,d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}=\frac{{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}({\mathring{D}}\lambda)}{3r}+{\overset{[1]}{{C}}}r^{2}\,,

(86)

which is non-zero on
S^{2}
only.
It then follows from (
84
) that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\lambda h_{uu}d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{{\overset{[2]}{Q}}{}(\lambda)}{r}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\lambda{\mathring{D}}^{A}h_{uA}d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}

(87)

\displaystyle=-\frac{{\overset{[2]}{Q}}{}(\lambda)}{r}-\frac{{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}({\mathring{D}}\lambda)}{6r^{2}}+{\overset{[1]}{{C}}}r\,.

Hence,
whatever the topology,

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\lambda\chi d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\lambda{\mathring{D}}^{A}{h}_{uA}d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}-r^{2}\partial_{r}\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\lambda{h}_{uu}d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}=-{\overset{[2]}{Q}}{}(\lambda)\,.

(88)

Writing
H_{k_{V}}
for the Sobolev space of the
\delta V
’s, (
72
) above implies

k_{\gamma}\geq k_{V}+2
and
k_{U}\geq k_{V}+1
.

(89)

3.3.4
\partial_{u}{h}_{AB}

We continue with
\partial_{u}{h}_{AB}
, as determined from
[
4
, Equation (32)]
:

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\Big[r\partial_{u}{\gamma}_{AB}-\frac{1}{2}V\partial_{r}{\gamma}_{AB}-\frac{1}{2r}V{\gamma}_{AB}\Big]

(90)

\displaystyle\quad=-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}(V/r){\gamma}_{AB}-\frac{1}{r}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\Big[e^{2\beta}R[\gamma]_{AB}-2e^{\beta}{D}_{A}{D}_{B}e^{\beta}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+{\gamma}_{CA}{D}_{B}[\partial_{r}(r^{2}U^{C})]-\frac{1}{2}r^{4}e^{-2\beta}{\gamma}_{AC}{\gamma}_{BD}(\partial_{r}U^{C})(\partial_{r}U^{D})

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{r^{2}}{2}(\partial_{r}{\gamma}_{AB})({D}_{C}U^{C})+r^{2}U^{C}{D}_{C}(\partial_{r}{\gamma}_{AB})

\displaystyle\quad\quad-r^{2}(\partial_{r}{\gamma}_{AC}){\gamma}_{BE}({D}^{C}U^{E}-{D}^{E}U^{C})+\Lambda e^{2\beta}g_{AB}-8\pi e^{2\beta}T_{AB}\Big]\,.

The linearisation of (
90
) around a Birmingham-Kottler background
in vacuum
reads, keeping in mind that
\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[R[\gamma]_{AB}]=0

in dimension two,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[\delta G_{AB}]

(91)

\displaystyle=\partial_{r}\Big[r\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{AB}-\frac{1}{2}V\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{AB}-\frac{1}{2r}V{\check{h}}_{AB}-r\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\big[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\check{h}}_{uB}\big]\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\underbrace{+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}(V/r)}_{mr^{-2}-\alpha^{2}r}{\check{h}}_{AB}-r^{-1}\Big(2{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\delta\beta+r\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\big[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\check{h}}_{uB}\big]\Big)\,.

Integrating this equation gives

\displaystyle s\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{AB}\Big|_{r_{1}}^{r}

\displaystyle=\Big[\frac{1}{2}V\partial_{s}{\check{h}}_{AB}+\frac{V}{2s}{\check{h}}_{AB}+s\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\big[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\check{h}}_{uB}\big]\Big]_{r_{1}}^{r}

(92)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\Big[-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{s}(V/s){\check{h}}_{AB}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+1/s\Big(2{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\delta\beta+s\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\big[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\check{h}}_{uB})\big]\Big)\Big]ds\,.

Denoting
H_{k_{\partial_{u}\gamma}}\ni\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{AB}
, (
92
) implies

k_{\beta}\geq k_{\partial_{u}\gamma}+2
and
k_{U}\geq k_{\partial_{u}\gamma}+1
.

(93)

When
\delta\beta=0
,
using (
67
) in the last term of (
92
)
leads to

\displaystyle\partial_{u}{h}_{AB}

\displaystyle=r\frac{\partial_{u}{h}_{AB}|_{r_{1}}}{r_{1}}-\frac{r}{2}\Big[V\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{AB}+\frac{1}{s}V{\check{h}}_{AB}

(94)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\Big(\frac{1}{s^{2}}-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\Big)TS\Big[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}^{C}{h}_{BC}\big]\Big]\Big|_{s=r_{1}}

\displaystyle+2r\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\Big\{\frac{r_{1}^{2}(r_{1}^{2}-r^{2})}{4r^{2}}\partial_{s}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\check{h}}_{uB}|_{r_{1}}-r_{1}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\check{h}}_{uB}|_{r_{1}}

\displaystyle+r{\mathring{D}}_{A}\Big[r^{2}{\mu}_{B}(u,x^{C})+\frac{{\lambda}_{B}(u,x^{C})}{r}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{r^{2}}{3}{\mathring{D}}^{C}{h}_{BC}(u,r_{1},x^{A})\left(\frac{1}{r_{1}^{3}}-\frac{1}{r^{3}}\right)\Big]\Big\}

\displaystyle+r\Bigg[\frac{V}{2r}\Big[r\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{AB}+{\check{h}}_{AB}\Big]

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\Big[\big(\frac{{\alpha^{2}}}{s}-\frac{m}{s^{4}}\big){h}_{AB}+\Big(\frac{1}{3sr^{2}}+\frac{2r}{3s^{4}}\Big)\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\big[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}^{C}{h}_{BC}\big]\Big]ds\Bigg]\,.

Recall that

V=r{{\varepsilon}}-{\alpha^{2}}r^{3}{-2m}\,.

Let us write
{\mathrm{b.d.}|_{r_{1}}}
for terms known from “boundary data at
r_{1}
”. We rewrite (
94
) as

\displaystyle\partial_{u}

\displaystyle h_{AB}={\mathrm{b.d.}|_{r_{1}}}+r\Bigg[\frac{V}{2r}\Big[r\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{AB}+{\check{h}}_{AB}\Big]+\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\Big[\big(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{s}-\frac{m}{s^{4}}\big){h}_{AB}

(95)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\big(\frac{1}{3sr^{2}}+\frac{2r}{3s^{4}}\big)\underbrace{\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\big[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}^{C}{h}_{BC}\big]}_{=:Ph_{AB}}\Big]ds\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{r({{\varepsilon}}r-{\alpha^{2}}r^{3}-2m)}{2}\Big[\partial_{r}(r^{-2}h_{AB})+\frac{1}{r^{3}}h_{AB}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\Big[\underbrace{(\frac{{\alpha^{2}}r}{s}-\frac{mr}{s^{4}})}_{\overset{(1,0)}{\psi}}{h}_{AB}+\underbrace{\big(\frac{1}{3sr}+\frac{2r^{2}}{3s^{4}}\big)}_{\overset{(1,1)}{\psi}(s,r)}Ph_{AB}\big]\Big]ds+{\mathrm{b.d.}|_{r_{1}}}\,.

For further use it is convenient to separate the terms involving
\alpha
and
m
from the remaining ones:

\displaystyle\partial_{u}

\displaystyle h_{AB}=\frac{{{\varepsilon}}}{2}\Big[\partial_{r}h_{AB}-\frac{1}{r}h_{AB}\Big]+\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\bigg(\frac{1}{3sr}+\frac{2r^{2}}{3s^{4}}\bigg)Ph_{AB}\,ds

(96)

\displaystyle-(\frac{{\alpha^{2}}r^{2}}{2}+\frac{m}{r})\Big[\partial_{r}h_{AB}-\frac{1}{r}h_{AB}\Big]+\int_{r_{1}}^{r}(\frac{{\alpha^{2}}r}{s}-\frac{mr}{s^{4}}){h}_{AB}\,ds+{\mathrm{b.d.}|_{r_{1}}}\,.

3.4
A pointwise radial conservation law

In this section we show that the equation

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\big(\frac{1}{r}\delta G_{AB}+{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta G_{rB}\big)=0

(97)

can be written as a radial conservation law,
\partial_{r}(....)=0

when
m=0=\alpha=\delta\beta
, where
P
is as in
(
95
):

\displaystyle Ph_{AB}:=\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}^{C}h_{BC}]\,.

(98)

We further show that the equation obtained by taking
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!
of (
97
),

{\mathring{D}}^{A}\big[\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\big(\frac{1}{r}\delta G_{AB}+{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta G_{rB}\big)\big]=0

(99)

can likewise be written as a radial conservation law
when
m=0=\delta\beta
, for any
\alpha
.
This is likely to be related to the contracted Bianchi identity discussed in Section
3.5
below, but if and how is not clear.

Indeed, when
\delta\beta=0
, taking
\frac{1}{2r^{2}}\times C
of
(
53
) gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2r^{2}}\partial_{r}\left[r^{4}\partial_{r}(r^{-2}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{B}h_{uA}])\right]-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\left(r^{-2}P{h}_{AB}\right)=0\,.

(100)

Subtracting (
100
) from (
91
) leads to

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\Big[\underbrace{r\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{AB}-\frac{V}{2}\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{AB}-\frac{V}{2r}{\check{h}}_{AB}-\frac{\partial_{r}\big(r^{2}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{h}_{uB}]\big)}{2r^{2}}+\frac{P{\check{h}}_{AB}}{2}}_{{:=q_{AB}}}\Big]

(101)

\displaystyle\quad=\big(\frac{{\alpha^{2}}}{r}-\frac{m}{r^{4}}\big){h}_{AB}\,.

Hence
q_{AB}
is radially conserved when
\alpha=m=0
.

Under a gauge transformation
q_{AB}
transforms as

\displaystyle q_{AB}\mapsto q_{AB}-\Big[

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}{\mathring{D}}_{C}\xi^{C}]-(P-{{\varepsilon}}+\alpha^{2}r^{2}-\frac{2m}{r})C(\xi)_{AB}\big)

(102)

\displaystyle-(2\alpha r+\frac{m}{r^{2}})\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{u}]\Big]\,.

Since
C(X)^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}=0

for any vector field
X^{A}
(cf. Proposition
C.3
, Appendix
C.2
below),
the field
q^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}_{AB}
is gauge-independent and, when
\alpha=0=m
, gives a
2
-dimensional family of radially conserved charges on
\mathbb{T}^{2}
, and a
6({{\mathfrak{g}}}-1)
-dimensional family of such charges on sections with genus
{{\mathfrak{g}}}\geq 2
.

Next, taking the divergence of (
101
) and using (
53
) we find

\displaystyle\partial_{r}({\mathring{D}}^{B}q_{AB})=-\frac{{\alpha^{2}}}{2}\partial_{r}\left[r^{4}\partial_{r}(r^{-2}h_{uA})-{\mathring{D}}^{B}{h}_{AB}\right]-\frac{m}{r^{4}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\,.

(103)

Setting
\delta\beta=0
in (
53
) we have

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\bigg(3{\check{h}}_{uA}+r\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{uA}-r^{-3}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\bigg)=r^{-4}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\,.

(104)

Equation (
104
)
allows us to rewrite (
103
) as:

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\Big[{\mathring{D}}^{B}q_{AB}+\frac{{\alpha^{2}}}{2}\left(r^{4}\partial_{r}({\check{h}}_{uA})-{\mathring{D}}^{B}{h}_{AB}\right)

(105)

\displaystyle\qquad+m\big(3{\check{h}}_{uA}+r\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{uA}-r^{-3}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\big)\Big]=0\,.

We define

\displaystyle\frac{{\overset{[3,1]}{Q}_{A}}{}}{2}:=

\displaystyle r{\mathring{D}}^{B}\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{AB}-\frac{1}{2}V\partial_{r}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\check{h}}_{AB}-\frac{1}{2r}V{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\check{h}}_{AB}

(106)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2r^{2}}\partial_{r}\big(r^{2}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{h}_{uB}]\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}{\mathring{D}}^{B}P{\check{h}}_{AB}+\frac{{\alpha^{2}}}{2}\big(r^{4}\partial_{r}(r^{-2}h_{uA})-{\mathring{D}}^{B}{h}_{AB}\big)

\displaystyle+m(3{\check{h}}_{uA}+r\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{uA}-r^{-3}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB})\,,

with
{\overset{[3,1]}{Q}_{A}}{}
being
r-
independent by
(
105
),
where the notation
{\overset{[3,1]}{Q}_{A}}{}
should be clear from (
116
) below. Equivalently, the field

\displaystyle{\overset{[3,1]}{Q}_{A}}{}=

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{B}\Big[2r\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{AB}-V\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{AB}-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\partial_{r}\big(r^{4}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\check{h}}_{uB}]\big)

(107)

\displaystyle\qquad+\big(P-{{\varepsilon}}\big){\check{h}}_{AB}\Big]+{{\alpha^{2}}r^{4}}\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{uA}+2m(3{\check{h}}_{uA}+r\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{uA})

is radially conserved.

To continue, for
\pi^{A}\in{{\text{CKV}}}
and
i\geq 0
we set

{\overset{[4,i]}{Q}}{}(\pi^{A}):=\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\pi^{A}\bigg(3\partial_{u}^{i}{\check{h}}_{uA}+r\partial_{r}\partial_{u}^{i}{\check{h}}_{uA}\bigg)d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}\,.

(108)

It follows from the
u
-derivatives of (
104
) that these are radially conserved charges.
Furthermore it holds that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\pi^{A}{\overset{[3,1]}{Q}_{A}}{}\,d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\pi^{A}(\alpha^{2}r^{4}\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{uA}+2m(3{\check{h}}_{uA}+r\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{uA}))\,d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}

(109)

\displaystyle={\alpha^{2}}{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}(\pi^{A})+2m{\overset{[4,0]}{Q}}{}(\pi^{A})\,;

recall that
{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}(\pi^{A})
has been defined in (
59
).
Thus
{\overset{[3,1]}{Q}}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]}
is zero if
m=0=\alpha
, and is determined by
{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}
and
{\overset{[4,0]}{Q}}{}
otherwise.

Under a gauge transformation
{\overset{[3,1]}{Q}_{A}}{}
transforms as

\displaystyle{\overset{[3,1]}{Q}_{A}}{}\mapsto

\displaystyle{\overset{[3,1]}{Q}_{A}}{}+2\underbrace{{\mathring{D}}^{B}\Big\{-\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}{\mathring{D}}_{C}\xi^{C}]+\big(P-{{\varepsilon}}\big)\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi_{B}]\Big\}}_{=(\widehat{\operatorname{{L}}}\xi)_{A}}

(110)

\displaystyle+6m\partial_{u}\xi_{A}\,,

where the operator
\widehat{\operatorname{{L}}}
can be written as

\widehat{\operatorname{{L}}}=-{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}C\operatorname{{L}}\,,\quad\operatorname{{L}}:={\mathring{D}}\,{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(1)}}\,}-{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}C+{{\varepsilon}}\,.

(111)

It follows from Proposition
C.6
, Appendix
C.4
, that the gauge transformations
(
110
) associated with
\xi_{A}
act transitively on
{\overset{[3,1]}{Q}}{}_{A}^{{[({{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}})^{\perp}]}}
.

In the case
m\neq 0
, the gauge-transformations associated with
\partial_{u}\xi_{A}
clearly act transitively on the collection of all radial charges
{\overset{[3,1]}{Q}}{}_{A}
.

On
S^{2}
we have
{{\text{H}}}=\{0\}
, and
when
m=0
but
\alpha\neq 0
we conclude that there the integrals

{\overset{[3,1]}{Q}}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]}={\overset{[3,1]}{Q}}{}_{A}^{[<2]}={\overset{[3,1]}{Q}}{}_{A}^{[=1]}\,,

provide
a 6-dimensional family of gauge-invariant radially-conserved charges.

On
\mathbb{T}^{2}
we have (compare (
371
)-(
372
) below)

\displaystyle\widehat{\operatorname{{L}}}(\xi)_{A}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}{\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}({\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}^{C}\xi_{C}-{\frac{1}{2}}{\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}\xi_{A})\,,

(112)

\displaystyle\operatorname{{L}}(\xi)_{A}

\displaystyle={\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}^{C}\xi_{C}-\frac{1}{2}{\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}\xi_{A}\,,

(113)

with kernels and cokernels spanned by covariantly constant vectors. So
{{\text{CKV}}}={{\text{KV}}}={{\text{H}}}
, and
when
m=0
it follows that on a torus the gauge transformations (
110
) act transitively on
{\overset{[3,1]}{Q}}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{KV}}}^{\perp}]}
,
and that
{\overset{[3,1]}{Q}}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{KV}}}]}
gives a 2-dimensional family of gauge-invariant radially-conserved charges.

On negatively curved two dimensional manifolds with genus
{{\mathfrak{g}}}
we have
{{\text{CKV}}}=\{0\}
so that
{{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}}={{\text{H}}}
and, again
when
m=0
,
{\overset{[3,1]}{Q}_{A}}{}
leads to a
2{{\mathfrak{g}}}
-dimensional family of gauge-invariant radially-conserved charges
{\overset{[3,1]}{Q}}{}^{[{{\text{H}}}]}
.

Summarising:
we can always choose
{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}_{A}

so that

{\overset{[3,1]}{Q}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}]^{[({{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}})^{\perp}]}={\overset{[3,1]}{Q}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}]^{[({{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}})^{\perp}]}

(114)

holds.
When
m=0
the equality

{\overset{[3,1]}{Q}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}]^{[{{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}}]}={\overset{[3,1]}{Q}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}]^{[{{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}}]}

(115)

provides an obstruction to gluing;
when
m\neq 0
it can be enforced by an appropriate choice of

\partial_{u}\xi_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}}]}
.
On
S^{2}
and on
\mathbb{T}^{2}
the condition (
115
) is trivially satisfied when
m=\alpha=0
, and reduces to the requirement of conservation of
{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}

and
{\overset{[4,0]}{Q}}{}
if
m\alpha\neq 0
.

It should be clear from the above that if we set, for
i\geq 1
,

\displaystyle{\overset{[3,i+1]}{Q}_{A}}{}:=

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{B}\Big[2r\partial_{u}^{i+1}{\check{h}}_{AB}-V\partial_{r}(\partial_{u}^{i}{\check{h}}_{AB})-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\partial_{r}\big(r^{4}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}\partial_{u}^{i}{\check{h}}_{uB}]\big)

(116)

\displaystyle\quad+\big(P-{{\varepsilon}}\big)\partial_{u}^{i}{\check{h}}_{AB}\Big]+\alpha^{2}r^{4}\partial_{r}\partial_{u}^{i}{\check{h}}_{uA}

\displaystyle\quad+2m(3\partial_{u}^{i}{\check{h}}_{uA}+r\partial_{r}\partial_{u}^{i}{\check{h}}_{uA})\,,

then we have:

Lemma
3.1

Suppose that for
i\geq 0

the
i
’th
u
-derivative of (
53
) and (
91
) with
\delta\beta\equiv 0
hold. Then

\partial_{r}{\overset{[3,i+1]}{Q}_{A}}{}=0\,.

\qed

Similarly to (
109
), for conformal Killing vectors
\pi^{A}

we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\pi^{A}{\overset{[3,i]}{Q}_{A}}{}\,d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}

(117)

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\pi^{A}\big({{\alpha^{2}}r^{4}\partial_{r}\partial_{u}^{i-1}{\check{h}}_{uA}+2m(3\partial_{u}^{i-1}{\check{h}}_{uA}+r\partial_{r}\partial_{u}^{i-1}{\check{h}}_{uA})}\big)\,d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}

\displaystyle=:{\alpha^{2}}{\overset{[1,i-i]}{Q}}{}(\pi^{A})+2m{\overset{[4,i-1]}{Q}}{}(\pi^{A})\,,

so that the left-hand side obviously vanishes if
\alpha=0=m
.
In fact, for
i\geq 2
there is no obstruction regardless of
\alpha
and
m
,
as our arguments below show that the right-hand side of the last equation is continuous at
r_{2}
when the Einstein equations together with a sufficient number of their
u
-derivatives hold on
{\mycal N}
.

Under gauge transformations, it follows from (
110
) that

\displaystyle{\overset{[3,i+1]}{Q}_{A}}{}\mapsto

\displaystyle{\overset{[3,i+1]}{Q}_{A}}{}+2(\widehat{\operatorname{{L}}}\partial_{u}^{i}\xi)_{A}+6m\partial_{u}^{i+1}\xi_{A}\,.

(118)

3.5
The remaining Einstein equations

Let us start by recalling that the Einstein equations

\mycal E_{\mu\nu}:=G_{\mu\nu}+\Lambda g_{\mu\nu}-8\pi T_{\mu\nu}

can be split as

\displaystyle\mycal E^{u}{}_{\mu}=0\,,\qquad\mycal E_{AB}-\frac{1}{2}g^{CD}\mycal E_{CD}g_{AB}=0\,,

(119)

\displaystyle g^{CD}\mycal E_{CD}=0\,,

(120)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}(r^{2}e^{2\beta}\mycal E^{r}{}_{u})=0\,,\qquad\partial_{r}(r^{2}e^{2\beta}\mycal E^{r}{}_{A})=0\,,

(121)

and the following holds (cf., e.g.,
[
4
, Section 3]
): Suppose that (
119
) holds on a null hypersurface
{\mycal N}
and that

\partial_{u}\mycal E^{u}{}_{\mu}|_{{\mycal N}}=0\,.

(122)

Then a) (
120
) is satisfied automatically on
{\mycal N}
, and b) the equations
\mycal E^{r}{}_{u}|_{{\mycal N}}=\mycal E^{r}{}_{A}|_{{\mycal N}}=0
will hold if they are satisfied at one single value of
r
. This follows from the observation that, in Bondi coordinates, we have the identity

\nabla_{\mu}\mycal E^{\mu}{}_{\nu}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{|\det g|}}\partial_{\mu}(\sqrt{|\det g|}\mycal E^{\mu}{}_{\nu})+\frac{1}{2}\mycal E_{\mu\sigma}\partial_{\nu}g^{\mu\sigma}\,.

(123)

In the current context this implies, using
\partial_{\nu}g^{u\mu}=0=\partial_{u}g^{\mu\sigma}
and the divergence identity,

0=\frac{1}{\sqrt{|\det g|}}\partial_{\mu}(\sqrt{|\det g|}\delta\mycal E^{\mu}{}_{\nu})+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mu,\sigma\neq u}\delta\mycal E_{\mu\sigma}\underbrace{\partial_{\nu}g^{\mu\sigma}}_{0\ \text{if}\ \nu=u}\,.

(124)

Since
\delta\mycal E^{u}{}_{\mu}=-\delta\mycal E_{r\mu}
, when the main equations (
119
) are satisfied
(
124
) becomes

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{|\det g|}}\partial_{\mu}(\sqrt{|\det g|}\delta\mycal E^{\mu}{}_{\nu})+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mu,\sigma\not\in\{u,r\}}\delta\mycal E_{\mu\sigma}\partial_{\nu}g^{\mu\sigma}

(125)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{|\det g|}}\partial_{\mu}(\sqrt{|\det g|}\delta\mycal E^{\mu}{}_{\nu})+\frac{1}{2}\delta\mycal E_{AB}\underbrace{\partial_{\nu}g^{AB}}_{0\ \text{if}\ \nu=u}\,.

In what follows we assume

\delta\mycal E^{u}{}_{\mu}|_{\mycal N}=0=\partial_{u}\delta\mycal E^{u}{}_{\mu}|_{\mycal N}\,.

(126)

We review the standard argument, which is a somewhat simplified version of what needs to be done in our gluing.
Setting
\nu=r
in (
125
) one obtains immediately

\displaystyle 0=-\frac{1}{{r}}g^{AB}\delta\mycal E_{AB}|_{\mycal N}\,,

(127)

hence the linearisation of (
120
) holds on
{\mycal S}
. So the linearised version of the second equation in (
119
) is equivalent to

\delta\mycal E_{AB}|_{\mycal N}=0
. Then
\delta\mycal E^{A}{}_{B}|_{\mycal N}=g^{AC}\delta\mycal E_{CB}|_{\mycal N}=0
, and (
125
) with
\nu=A
becomes

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\partial_{r}(r^{2}\delta\mycal E^{r}{}_{A})|_{\mycal N}\,,

(128)

as desired. So, if
\mycal E^{r}{}_{A}
vanishes for some
r
on
{\mycal N}
, it will vanish throughout
{\mycal N}
. Now,
(
125
) with
\nu=u
reduces to

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\partial_{r}(r^{2}\delta\mycal E^{r}{}_{u})|_{\mycal N}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\partial_{A}(r^{2}\delta\mycal E^{A}{}_{u})|_{\mycal N}\,.

(129)

and what has been said about
\delta\mycal E^{A}{}_{u}|_{\mycal N}
gives the
result.

The above means that there is no need to integrate in
r
these Einstein equations which have not been discussed so far, namely
g^{AB}\mycal E_{AB}=0
,
\mycal E_{uA}=0
and
\mycal E_{uu}=0
, when (
126
) holds. Indeed, once the already analysed equations (
119
) are solved, together with their first
u
-derivatives,
the whole set of Einstein equations will be solved by ensuring that
\mycal E^{r}{}_{A}=0=\mycal E^{r}{}_{u}
holds at one value of
r
; this is equivalent to ensuring
\mycal E_{uA}=0=\mycal E_{uu}
at one value of
r
.

The same scheme applies to the set of equations obtained by further differentiating the Einstein equation in
u
an arbitrary number of times.

3.5.1
\partial_{u}\partial_{r}{h}_{uA}

The equations
\mycal E_{uA}=0
are too long to be usefully displayed here.
Their linearisation
\delta\mycal E_{uA}\equiv-\delta\mycal E^{r}{}_{A}+(\epsilon-{\alpha^{2}}r^{2}-\frac{2m}{r})\delta\mycal E_{rA}
in vacuum reads

\displaystyle 0=2\delta\mycal E_{uA}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\Bigg[{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{h}_{uB}-{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\mathring{D}}_{B}{h}_{uA}+\partial_{u}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{h}_{AB}

(130)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-r^{2}\bigg(\left({{\varepsilon}}-r^{2}\alpha^{2}-\frac{2m}{r}\right)\partial_{r}^{2}h_{uA}+(2\alpha^{2}+\frac{4m}{r^{3}})h_{uA}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-r^{2}\partial_{r}\partial_{u}\left(\frac{h_{uA}}{r^{2}}\right)+\partial_{r}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{h}_{uu}\bigg)\Bigg]\,.

This equation is satisfied both by
\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}
and
\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}
in vacuum.

Assuming
\delta G_{rA}=0
, using the transport equation (
53
)
to eliminate
\partial_{r}^{2}{\check{h}}_{uA}
and
the identity (
82
) to eliminate
\partial_{r}{h}_{uu}
, we can rewrite (
130
) as

\displaystyle-r^{4}\partial_{r}\partial_{u}\left(\frac{h_{uA}}{r^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle={\mathring{D}}^{B}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{h}_{uB}-{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\mathring{D}}_{B}{h}_{uA}+\partial_{u}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{h}_{AB}

(131)

\displaystyle\quad-r^{2}\bigg(\left({{\varepsilon}}-r^{2}\alpha^{2}-\frac{2m}{r}\right)\partial_{r}^{2}h_{uA}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+(2\alpha^{2}+\frac{4m}{r^{3}})h_{uA}+\partial_{r}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{h}_{uu}\bigg)

\displaystyle={\mathring{D}}^{B}\Big[-2\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{B}{h}_{uA}]+\partial_{u}{h}_{AB}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+(\alpha^{2}r^{2}-{{\varepsilon}}+\frac{2m}{r})r^{2}\partial_{r}\left(r^{-2}h_{AB}\right)\Big]+{\mathring{D}}_{A}\chi\,.

Using the fact that
\partial_{r}\chi=0
we obtain, for any
\pi^{A}(x^{B})
satisfying
\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}\pi_{B}]=0
,

\partial_{r}\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\pi^{A}r^{4}\partial_{u}\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{uA}d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}\equiv\partial_{r}{\overset{[1,1]}{Q}}{}(\pi^{A})=0\,,

(132)

where we recall from (
117
) that for
0\leq i\in\mathbb{N}
,

{\overset{[1,i]}{Q}}{}(\pi^{A}):=\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\pi^{A}r^{4}\partial^{i}_{u}\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{uA}d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}\,.

(133)

Clearly, by
u
-differentiating (
131
), we conclude that
\partial^{i}_{u}\delta\mycal E_{uA}=0
implies

\partial_{r}{\overset{[1,i+1]}{Q}}{}(\pi^{A})=0\,.

(134)

for
i\geq 0
.

Denoting
H_{k_{\partial_{u}U}}\ni\partial_{u}\delta U^{A}
, (
130
) implies

k_{U}\geq k_{\partial_{u}U}+2
,
k_{\partial_{u}\gamma}\geq k_{\partial_{u}U}+1
and
k_{V}\geq k_{\partial_{u}U}+1
.

(135)

3.5.2
\partial_{u}{h}_{uu}

The equation
\mycal E_{uu}=0
is likewise too long to be usefully displayed here.
Its linearised version is shorter and, in vacuum, can be rewritten as an equation for the transverse derivative
\partial_{u}(rh_{uu}-{\mathring{D}}^{A}{h}_{uA})
:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=2\delta\mycal E_{uu}

(136)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\Big[2\Big(\partial_{u}+\big({\alpha^{2}r^{2}-{{\varepsilon}}+\frac{2m}{r}}\big)\partial_{r}+\frac{3m}{r^{2}}-\frac{{{\varepsilon}}}{r}\Big){\mathring{D}}^{A}{h}_{uA}

\displaystyle\quad-{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{h}_{uu}-\big({\alpha^{2}r^{2}-{{\varepsilon}}+\frac{2m}{r}}\big)\Big(\frac{{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{h}_{AB}}{r^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad-2r\partial_{u}{h}_{uu}-2\big({\alpha^{2}r^{2}-{{\varepsilon}}+\frac{2m}{r}}\big)\partial_{r}(r{h}_{uu})\Big]

This must be satisfied by
\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}
and
\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}
when the linearised vacuum Einstein equations hold.

Denoting
H_{k_{\partial_{u}V}}\ni\partial_{u}\delta V
, (
136
) implies

k_{U}\geq k_{\partial_{u}V}+1
,
k_{V}\geq k_{\partial_{u}V}+2
and
k_{\gamma}\geq k_{\partial_{u}V}+2
.

(137)

3.6
Regularity

The regularity analysis carried-out so far is summarised by the following inequalities for the regularity of the metric components:

\displaystyle h_{uA}\text{ equation}:\quad

\displaystyle k_{\beta}\geq k_{U}+1\,,\quad k_{\gamma}\geq k_{U}+1\,,

(138)

\displaystyle h_{uu}\text{ equation}:\quad

\displaystyle k_{\gamma}\geq k_{V}+2\,,\quad k_{U}\geq k_{V}+1\,,

(139)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}h_{AB}\text{ equation}:\quad

\displaystyle k_{\beta}\geq k_{\partial_{u}\gamma}+2\,,\quad k_{U}\geq k_{\partial_{u}\gamma}+1\,,

(140)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\partial_{r}h_{uA}\text{ equation}:\quad

\displaystyle k_{U}\geq k_{\partial_{u}U}+2\,,\quad k_{V}\geq k_{\partial_{u}U}+1\,,

(141)

\displaystyle k_{\partial_{u}\gamma}\geq k_{\partial_{u}U}+1\,,

\displaystyle\partial_{u}h_{uu}\text{ equation}:\quad

\displaystyle k_{U}\geq k_{\partial_{u}V}+1\,,\quad k_{V}\geq k_{\partial_{u}V}+2\,,

(142)

\displaystyle k_{\gamma}\geq k_{\partial_{u}V}+2\,.

A consistent scheme for the linearised equations will thus be obtained if we choose any field
h_{AB}
such that
h_{AB}(r,\cdot)\in{H_{k_{\gamma}}({\mathbf{S}})}
, for all
r\in[r_{1},r_{2}]
, with
k_{\gamma}\geq 4
and

\displaystyle k_{\beta}

\displaystyle=k_{\gamma}\,,\quad k_{U}=k_{\gamma}-1\,,\quad k_{V}=k_{\gamma}-2\,,\quad k_{\partial_{u}U}=k_{\gamma}-3\,,

(143)

\displaystyle k_{\partial_{u}V}

\displaystyle=k_{\gamma}-4\,,\quad k_{\partial_{u}\gamma}=k_{\gamma}-2\,.

(144)

Note that the question of regularity of
r
-derivatives of
\gamma
has been swept under the rug using integration by parts. This question will need to be addressed when dealing with the nonlinear problem.

The regularity properties of the metric will be compatible with gauge transformations (
28
)-(
31
) if we assume, using obvious notation,

\displaystyle h_{uA}\text{ equation}:\quad

\displaystyle k_{\xi^{u}}\geq k_{U}+3\,,\quad k_{\partial_{u}\xi^{u}}\geq k_{U}+1\,,

(145)

\displaystyle k_{\xi^{A}}\geq k_{U}+2\,,

\displaystyle h_{ur}\text{ equation}:\quad

\displaystyle k_{\partial_{u}\xi^{u}}\geq k_{\beta}\,,\quad k_{\xi^{A}}\geq k_{\beta}+1\,,

(146)

\displaystyle h_{uu}\text{ equation}:\quad

\displaystyle k_{\partial_{u}\xi^{u}}\geq k_{V}+2\,,\quad k_{\partial_{u}\xi^{B}}\geq k_{V}+1\,,

(147)

\displaystyle k_{\xi^{u}}\geq k_{V}+2\,,\quad k_{\xi^{B}}\geq k_{V}+1\,,

\displaystyle h_{AB}\text{ equation}:\quad

\displaystyle k_{\xi^{A}}\geq k_{\gamma}+1\,,\quad k_{\xi^{u}}\geq k_{\gamma}+2\,,

(148)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}h_{AB}\text{ equation}:\quad

\displaystyle k_{\partial_{u}\xi^{A}}\geq k_{\partial_{u}\gamma}+1\,,\quad k_{\partial_{u}\xi^{u}}\geq k_{\partial_{u}\gamma}+2\,.

(149)

A scheme consistent with (
143
)-(
149
) results by choosing

k_{\xi^{u}}=k_{\gamma}+2\,,\quad k_{\partial_{u}\xi^{u}}=k_{\gamma}\,,\quad k_{\xi^{A}}=k_{\gamma}+1\,,\quad k_{\partial_{u}\xi^{A}}=k_{\gamma}-1\,.

(150)

3.7
Further
u
-derivatives

The representation formula for higher
u
-derivatives of the linearised metric components can be obtained by taking the
u
-derivatives of the existing equations. This gives, for
i\geq 0
, representation formulae of the form

\displaystyle\partial_{u}^{i}{h}_{AB}

\displaystyle=\overset{(i)}{\Psi}_{AB}(u,r,x^{A})+\sum_{0\leq j+k\leq i,k\neq i}\overset{(i,j,k)}{\psi}(r)\partial_{r}^{j}P^{k}{h}_{AB}

(151)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\sum_{j=0}^{i}\overset{(i,j)}{{\psi}}(s,r)P^{j}{h}_{AB}\,ds\,,

\displaystyle\partial_{u}^{i}{\check{h}}_{uA}

\displaystyle=\overset{(i)}{X}_{A}(u,r,x^{A})+{\mathring{D}}^{B}\Big[\sum_{0\leq j+k\leq i,k\neq i}\overset{(i,j,k)}{\chi}(r)\partial_{r}^{j}P^{k}{h}_{AB}

(152)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\sum_{j=0}^{i}\overset{(i,j)}{\chi}(s,r)P^{j}{h}_{AB}\,ds\Big]\,,

where
\overset{(i)}{X}
and
\overset{(i)}{\Psi}
depend only on data at
r_{1}
;
recall that
P
denotes the operator

Ph_{AB}=\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[D_{A}D^{C}h_{BC}]\,.

(153)

The above is proved by induction (see Appendix
B
), which is initialised with
i=0
as follows:

1.

Order zero for (
151
) is trivial, with

\displaystyle\overset{(0,0,0)}{\psi}(r)=1\,,\quad\overset{(0)}{\Psi}_{AB}(u,r,x^{A})=0=\overset{(0,0)}{{\psi}}(s,r)\,.\quad

(154)

We note that order one for (
151
) is obtained from
(
95
), with

\displaystyle\displaystyle\overset{(1,0,0)}{\psi}(r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{{{\varepsilon}}}{2r}+\frac{\alpha^{2}r}{2}+\frac{m}{r^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle\overset{(1,1,0)}{\psi}(r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left({{\varepsilon}}-{\alpha^{2}}r^{2}-\frac{2m}{r}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\overset{(1,0,1)}{\psi}(r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\,,\quad\overset{(1,1)}{{\psi}}(s,r)=\frac{2r^{2}}{3s^{4}}+\frac{1}{3sr}\,,

\displaystyle\overset{(1,0)}{\psi}(s,r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{{\alpha^{2}}r}{s}-\frac{mr^{2}}{s^{4}}\,.\quad

2.

Order zero for (
152
) follows from (
67
), where
\mu
and
\lambda
are determined from
h_{uA}|_{r_{1}}
and
\partial_{r}h_{uA}|_{r_{1}}
, with

\displaystyle\overset{(0,0,0)}{\chi}(r)=0\,,\quad\overset{(0,0)}{\chi}(s,r)=\frac{1}{3}\left(\frac{2}{sr^{3}}+\frac{1}{s^{4}}\right)\,.

(155)

We note that the terms involving
\overset{(i,j,k)}{\psi}
and
\overset{(i,j,k)}{\chi}
are innocuous at
r=r_{2}
, as they are determined by known boundary data at
r_{2}
