. However, they are essential for the induction procedure for
r\neq r_{2}
, as they contribute to the key terms
\overset{(i,j)}{\psi}
and
\overset{(i,j)}{\chi}
in the iteration.
This implies in particular that the explicit form of

\overset{(i,j,k)}{\psi}

etc. with the highest index
i=\ell
is
not
needed when gluing at order
\ell
.

Again by induction (cf. Appendix
B
), one shows the following:

1.

All the integral kernels in (
151
)-(
152
), depending upon
r
and
s
, are polynomials in
s^{-1}
with coefficients depending upon
r
;

2.

when
m=0
,
\overset{(i,0)}{\psi}
is proportional to
{\alpha^{2}}s^{-1}
.

3.

The highest power of
1/s
in
\overset{(1,j)}{\psi}
is
s^{-4}
.

4.

The highest power of
1/s
in
\overset{(i,j)}{\psi}
with
1\leq j\leq i

is
s^{-(i+3)}

when
m=0
, and this power is not larger than
s^{-2i+j-{3}}
when
m\neq 0
; cf. Lemma
B.1
, Appendix
B.2
.

5.

It holds that

\overset{(i+1,i+1)}{{\psi}}(s,r)=\int_{s}^{r}\overset{(i,i)}{{\psi}}(y,r)\overset{(1,1)}{{\psi}}(s,y)\,dy\,,

with

\overset{(1,1)}{{\psi}}(s,r)=\frac{2r^{2}}{3s^{4}}+\frac{1}{3rs}\,,

independently of
m
.

6.

The highest power of
1/s
in
\overset{(i,j)}{\chi}
with
0\leq j\leq i

is
s^{-(i+4)}
when
m=0
, and this power is not larger than
s^{-2i+j-4}

when
m\neq 0
.

In what follows we will often use the notation

\displaystyle\hat{\kappa}_{i}(s):=\frac{1}{s^{i}}\,.

(156)

We have collected the explicit formulae for all the integral kernels appearing in (
151
)-(
152
), and needed for
{C^{2}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}}
-gluing, in Appendix
B
.

3.7.1
The transverse-traceless part

For most of our further purposes, the essential role is played by the integral kernels
\overset{(i,j)}{\chi}
and
\overset{(i,j)}{\psi}
appearing in (
151
)-(
152
). However, it turns out that when
m=0
the
TT
-part of
\partial_{u}^{i}h_{AB}
leads to obstructions to gluing, in which case the boundary terms in (
151
) become significant. This forces us to revisit the induction, as follows:

We first consider the
L^{2}
-projection of (
94
) on
TT
, with
m=0
:

\displaystyle\partial_{u}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}

\displaystyle=r\underbrace{\bigg[\frac{\partial_{u}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{r_{1}}}{r_{1}}-\frac{1}{2}({{{{\varepsilon}}}}-\alpha^{2}r_{1}^{2})\bigg(\frac{1}{r_{1}}\partial_{r}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{r_{1}}-\frac{1}{r_{1}^{2}}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{r_{1}}\bigg)\bigg]}_{q_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{r_{1}}}

(157)

\displaystyle+\frac{r}{2}({{{{\varepsilon}}}}-\alpha^{2}r^{2})\bigg(\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}-\frac{1}{r^{2}}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\bigg)+{\alpha^{2}r}\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\frac{1}{s}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\,ds\,;

equivalently,

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{1}{r}\partial_{u}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}-\frac{1}{2}({{{{\varepsilon}}}}-\alpha^{2}r^{2})\bigg(\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}-\frac{1}{r^{2}}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\bigg)}_{q_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{r}}=

(158)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\underbrace{\bigg[\frac{\partial_{u}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{r_{1}}}{r_{1}}-\frac{1}{2}({{{{\varepsilon}}}}-\alpha^{2}r_{1}^{2})\bigg(\frac{1}{r_{1}}\partial_{r}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{r_{1}}-\frac{1}{r_{1}^{2}}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{r_{1}}\bigg)\bigg]}_{q_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{r_{1}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+{\alpha^{2}}\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\frac{1}{s}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\,ds\,.

This can of course also be derived directly from (
101
), but note that this calculation makes it clear how the tensor field
q_{AB}
appears in the formalism.

It follows that when
\alpha=0=m
, the field
\overset{}{q}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}
provides a 2-dimensional family of gauge-independent radially conserved charges on
\mathbb{T}^{2}
, and a
6({{\mathfrak{g}}}-1)
-dimensional family of such charges on sections with genus
{{\mathfrak{g}}}\geq 2
.

When
\alpha\neq 0
but
m
remains zero,
taking
u
-derivatives of (
158
) leads to

\displaystyle\overset{[p+1]}{q}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\Big|_{r_{1}}^{r}

\displaystyle=\alpha^{2}\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\hat{\kappa}_{1}(s)\partial^{p}_{u}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\,ds\,,

(159)

where, for
i\geq 1
,

\overset{[i]}{q}_{AB}:=\frac{1}{r}\partial_{u}^{i}{h}_{AB}-\frac{1}{2}({{{{\varepsilon}}}}-\alpha^{2}r^{2})\big(\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}\partial_{u}^{i-1}{h}_{AB}-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\partial_{u}^{i-1}{h}_{AB}\big)\,.

(160)

Making use again of (
157
) we find

\displaystyle\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\hat{\kappa}_{1}(s)\partial_{u}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\,ds

(161)

\displaystyle=\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\bigg[q_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{r_{1}}+\frac{1}{2}({{{{\varepsilon}}}}-\alpha^{2}s^{2})\big(\frac{1}{s}\partial_{s}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{s}-\frac{1}{s^{2}}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{s}\big)\bigg]ds

\displaystyle\quad+{\alpha^{2}}\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\int_{r_{1}}^{s}\bigg(\frac{1}{y}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{y}\bigg)dy\,ds

\displaystyle=\bigg[s\ q_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{r_{1}}+\frac{1}{2s}({{{{\varepsilon}}}}-\alpha^{2}s^{2}){h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{s}\bigg]_{r_{1}}^{r}+{\alpha^{2}r}\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\hat{\kappa}_{1}(s){h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\ ds\,.

It follows by induction that

\displaystyle\partial^{p}_{u}\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\hat{\kappa}_{1}(s){h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\,ds

(162)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}(\alpha^{2}r)^{k}\partial_{u}^{p-1-k}\bigg[s\ q_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{r_{1}}+\frac{1}{2s}({{{{\varepsilon}}}}-\alpha^{2}s^{2}){h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{s}\bigg]_{r_{1}}^{r}

\displaystyle\quad+(\alpha^{2}r)^{p}\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\hat{\kappa}_{1}(s){h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\,ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}(\alpha^{2}r)^{k}\bigg[s\ {\overset{[p-k]}{q}}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{r_{1}}+\frac{1}{2s}({{{{\varepsilon}}}}-\alpha^{2}s^{2})\partial_{u}^{p-1-k}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{s}\bigg]_{r_{1}}^{r}

\displaystyle\quad+(\alpha^{2}r)^{p}\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\hat{\kappa}_{1}(s){h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\,ds\,.

This allows us to rewrite (
159
) as

\displaystyle\overset{[p+1]}{q}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\Big|_{r_{1}}^{r}

(163)

\displaystyle=\alpha^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}(\alpha^{2}r)^{k}\bigg[s\ {\overset{[p-k]}{q}}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{r_{1}}+\frac{1}{2s}({{{{\varepsilon}}}}-\alpha^{2}s^{2})\partial_{u}^{p-1-k}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{s}\bigg]_{r_{1}}^{r}

\displaystyle\quad+\alpha^{2(p+1)}r^{p}\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\hat{\kappa}_{1}(s){h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\,ds\,.

4
Gluing up to gauge

We now present a scheme for matching, up-to residual gauge, the linearised fields

\{{h}_{\mu\nu},\partial_{u}{h}_{\mu\nu}\,\ldots\,\partial_{u}^{k}{h}_{\mu\nu}\}

(164)

in Bondi gauge, with
2\leq k<\infty
.
We will assume, for simplicity, that each of the fields
\partial_{u}^{i}{h}_{\mu\nu}\big|_{\{u=0\}}
,
0\leq i\leq k
, is smooth. The collection of fields of this differentiability class will be denoted by
{C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}}
.

Let
0\leq r_{0}<r_{1}<r_{2}<r_{3}\in\mathbb{R}
.
Consider two sets of vacuum linearised gravitational fields in Bondi gauge,
of
{C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}}
-differentiability class,
defined in spacetime neighbourhoods of
{\mycal N}_{(r_{0},r_{1}]}
and
{\mycal N}_{[r_{2},r_{3})}
. Let us denote by
{\mathbf{S}}_{1}
the section of
{\mycal N}_{(r_{0},r_{1}]}
at
r=r_{1}
. The linearised gravitational field near
{\mycal N}_{(r_{0},r_{1}]}

induces a set of Bondi cross-section data on
{\mathbf{S}}_{1}
, which we denote
as
\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}
. Similarly, we denote by
{\mathbf{S}}_{2}
the section of
{\mycal N}_{[r_{2},r_{3})}
at
r=r_{2}
and the induced gluing data by
\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}
. Let us also denote by
\tilde{{\mathbf{S}}}_{1}
(resp.
\tilde{{\mathbf{S}}}_{2}
) the codimension-two section obtained by
gauge-transforming
{\mathbf{S}}_{1}
(resp.
{\mathbf{S}}_{2}
) using arbitrary gauge fields
{\overset{(1)}{{\xi}}}{}^{\mu}
(resp.
{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{\mu}
), the associated gluing data by
\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}_{\tilde{{\mathbf{S}}}_{1}}
(resp.
\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}_{\tilde{{\mathbf{S}}}_{2}}
) and the outgoing null hypersurface on which it lies by
{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{(r_{0},r_{1}]}
(resp.

{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{2},r_{3})}
).

Of course, in the linearised gluing the initial hypersurface
{\mycal N}_{(r_{0},r_{3})}
does not change, thus
{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{(r_{0},r_{3})}={\mycal N}_{(r_{0},r_{3})}
as a set, but the Bondi coordinates on either
{\mycal N}_{(r_{0},r_{1}]}
or on
{\mycal N}_{[r_{2},r_{3})}
need to be “infinitesimally deformed” both in transverse and in tangential directions. We use the symbol
{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}
to emphasise the infinitesimal adjustment of Bondi coordinates, as an adjustment of
{\mycal N}_{(r_{0},r_{1}]}
or
{\mycal N}_{[r_{2},r_{3})}
is generically needed when passing to the nonlinear gluing both in our case and in
[
3
]
.

The goal
is to glue
\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}_{\tilde{{\mathbf{S}}}_{1}}
and
\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}_{\tilde{{\mathbf{S}}}_{2}}

along
{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{1},r_{2}]}
so that the resulting linearised field on
{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{(r_{0},r_{3})}
provide smooth characteristic data for Einstein equations
together with a matching of
k
transverse derivatives.
Indeed, we claim:

Theorem
4.1

A
{C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}}
-linearised vacuum data set on
{\mycal N}_{(r_{0},r_{1}]}
can be smoothly glued to another such set on
{\mycal N}_{[r_{2},r_{3})}
if and only if the obstructions listed in Tables
4.1
-
4.2
are satisfied.

The rest of this section is devoted to the proof of this theorem.

Let
{v}_{AB}
be any symmetric traceless tensor field defined on a neighbourhood of
{\mycal N}_{[r_{1},r_{2}]}
which interpolates between the original fields
{h}_{AB}|_{{\mycal N}_{(r_{0},r_{1}]}}
and
{h}_{AB}|_{{\mycal N}_{[r_{2},r_{3})}}
,
so that the resulting field on
{\mycal N}_{(r_{0},r_{3})}
is as differentiable as the original fields.
When attempting a
{C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}}
-gluing, we can add to
{v}_{AB}
a field
{w}_{AB}|_{[r_{1},r_{2}]}

which vanishes smoothly
(i.e. together with
r
-derivatives of all orders)
at the end cross-sections
\{r_{1}\}\times{\mathbf{S}}
and
\{r_{2}\}\times{\mathbf{S}}
without affecting the gluing of
h_{AB}
.
To take into account the gauge freedom, let
\phi(r)\geq 0
be a smooth function which equals
1
near
r=r_{1}
and equals
0
near
r=r_{2}
. Let
{\overset{(1)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u}
and
{\overset{(1)}{{\xi}}_{A}}
be gauge fields used to gauge the metric around
{\mycal N}_{(r_{0},r_{1}]}
, and let
{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u}
and
{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}_{A}}
be gauge fields used to gauge the metric around
{\mycal N}_{[r_{2},r_{3})}
. For
r_{1}\leq r\leq r_{2}
we set

\tilde{h}_{AB}={v}_{AB}+{w}_{AB}+\phi r^{2}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{\mathcal{L}}}_{{\overset{(1)}{{\zeta}}}}{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}_{AB}]+(1-\phi)r^{2}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{\mathcal{L}}}_{{\overset{(2)}{{\zeta}}}}{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}_{AB}]\,.

(165)

(Recall that
{\zeta}^{A}=\xi^{A}-{\mathring{D}}^{A}\xi^{u}/r
, cf. (
25
).)

In the gluing problem, the gauge fields evaluated on
\tilde{{\mathbf{S}}}_{1,2}
and the field
{w}_{AB}
on
{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{(r_{1},r_{2})}
are
free fields
which can be chosen arbitrarily. Our aim in what follows is to show how to choose these fields to solve the transport equations of Section
3.3
-
3.7
to achieve gluing-up-to-gauge.
When extending fields across
r_{1}
by solving the transport equations, we will always choose initial data at
r_{1}
which guarantee smoothness of the fields there.

For the
{C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}}
-gluing we will need smooth functions

\kappa_{i}:(r_{1},r_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}\,,\quad i\in\{0,\ldots,{k_{[m]}}+4\}\,,

where
k_{[m]}=k

when
m=0
and
k_{[m]}=2k

when
m\neq 0
, satisfying

\displaystyle\displaystyle\langle\kappa_{i},\hat{\kappa}_{j}\rangle\equiv\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\kappa_{i}(s)\hat{\kappa}_{j}(s)\,ds=0\quad\mbox{for $i>j$}\,,

(166)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\langle\kappa_{i},\hat{\kappa}_{i}\rangle=1\,,

(167)

and vanishing near the end points
r\in\{r_{1},r_{2}\}
, which is possible since the
\hat{\kappa}_{i}
’s are linearly independent;
see Appendix
A
.

The fields
{w}_{AB}
of (
165
) will be taken of the following form: for
s\in[r_{1},r_{2}]
,

\displaystyle{w}_{AB}(s)=\sum_{i=1}^{{k_{[m]}}+4}\kappa_{i}(s){\overset{[i]}{\varphi}}_{AB}\,.

(168)

We define

{\overset{[j]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}:=\langle{w}_{AB},\hat{\kappa}_{j}\rangle\,.

(169)

We will show how to construct the fields
\{{\overset{[j]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}\}_{j=1}^{k_{[m]}+4}
. In view of (
166
)-(
167
) we then have

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}{\overset{[1]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}\\
{\overset{[2]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}\\
\vdots\\
{\overset{[k_{[m]}+4]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}\\
\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}1&0&\cdots&0\\
\langle\kappa_{1},\hat{\kappa}_{2}\rangle&1&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&&\ddots&\vdots\\
\langle\kappa_{1},\hat{\kappa}_{k_{[m]}+4}\rangle&\langle\kappa_{2},\hat{\kappa}_{k_{[m]}+4}\rangle&\cdots&1\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}{\overset{[1]}{\varphi}}_{AB}\\
{\overset{[2]}{\varphi}}_{AB}\\
\vdots\\
{\overset{[k_{[m]}+4]}{\varphi}}_{AB}\\
\end{array}\right)\,,

(170)

which allows one to determine
\{{\overset{[j]}{\varphi}}_{AB}\}_{j=1}^{k_{[m]}+4}
in terms of
\{{\overset{[j]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}\}_{j=1}^{k_{[m]}+4}
in the obvious way.

4.1
Strategy

A collection of fields
\{\partial_{u}^{i}h_{\mu\nu}\}_{0\leq i\leq k}
on a null hypersurface
{\mycal N}
will be called
characteristic
{C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}}
-data for linearised vacuum Einstein equations on
{\mycal N}
, or simply
{C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}}
-data
, if the fields
\partial_{u}^{i}h_{\mu\nu}
are smooth on
{\mycal N}
and satisfy on
{\mycal N}

the equations which are obtained by differentiating the linearised vacuum Einstein equations in
u
up to
k
-times, and in which no-more than
k
derivatives of the
h_{\mu\nu}
’s with respect to
u
occur. In Bondi gauge this means that the equations
\partial_{u}^{i}\mycal E_{\mu\nu}=0
should hold with
0\leq i\leq k-1
, and that in addition we also have
\partial_{u}^{k}\mycal E_{rA}=0=\partial_{u}^{k}\mycal E_{rr}=\partial_{u}^{k}\mycal E_{ur}
.

We will say that
{C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}}
-data are smooth if the
\partial_{u}^{i}h_{\mu\nu}
’s are smooth on
{\mycal N}
.

We note that the linearised Einstein equations are invariant under linearised gauge transformations. In our scheme we will perform gauge transformations which will be needed to ensure the continuity of the fields, but which will have no influence on the question whether or not the linearised Einstein equations hold.

A set of
{C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}}
-data can be obtained by restricting a smooth solution of linearised vacuum equations, and its transverse derivatives, to a null hypersurface.
The converse is also true for null hypersurfaces with boundary, e.g.
{\mycal N}_{[r_{0},r_{1})}
or
{\mycal N}_{[r_{0},r_{1}]}
, in the following sense: any such data set arises by restriction of (many) solutions of vacuum Einstein equations to
{\mycal N}
. This can be realised by solving a characteristic Cauchy problem with two null hypersurfaces intersecting transversally at
\{r=r_{0}\}
, and requires providing data on both hypersurfaces. We note that losses of differentiability are unavoidable in the characteristic Cauchy problem when the data are not smooth:
solutions constructed from characteristic initial data which are of
C^{k}
-differentiability class will typically be of differentiability class
C^{k-k_{0}}
, for some
k_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
which typically depends upon
k
.
Compare
[
13
,
14
]
.

Our gluing procedure for such fields rests on the following elementary result. Let

a<b<c
, and let us for simplicity assume that all fields

\partial_{u}^{i}h_{\mu\nu}
,
i\in\mathbb{N}
, on
{\mycal N}_{(a,b]}
and
{\mycal N}_{[b,c)}
are smooth in all variables, up-to-and-including the common boundary at
b
; a similar result for finitely-differentiable fields, with distinct finite losses of differentiability for distinct fields, can be established using the results of Section
3.6
, and is left as an exercise to a concerned reader.

Lemma
4.2

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
Two
{C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}}

data sets in Bondi gauge on
{\mycal N}_{(a,b]}
and
{\mycal N}_{[b,c)}
, with
h_{AB}
extending smoothly across
\{r=b\}
, extend to smooth
{C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}}
data on
{\mycal N}_{(a,c)}
if and only if the fields

1.

\partial_{u}^{i}h_{ur}
,
\partial_{u}^{i}h_{uA}
,
\partial_{u}^{i}h_{AB}
, with
0\leq i\leq k
, as well as

2.

\partial_{r}h_{uA}
and

h_{uu}

extend by continuity at
\{r=b\}
to continuous fields.

\proof

The necessity is obvious. The sufficiency
follows from the equations in Sections
3.3
-
3.5
, together with their
u
-derivatives, as follows:

Suppose that
\delta\beta
extends by continuity at
b
, then (
50
) shows that
\delta\beta
extends to a smooth function. Next, (
53
) shows that continuity of

\partial_{r}(r^{-2}h_{uA})
at
b
guarantees a smooth extension of
\partial_{r}(r^{-2}h_{uA})
.
But then, by another integration, continuity of
h_{uA}
at
b
guarantees smooth extendability. One can now use (
72
) and (
91
) to similarly show that
continuity, at
b
, of
\delta V
and
\partial_{u}h_{AB}
leads to smooth extensions of these fields.
In particular
\partial_{u}h_{AB}
is now smooth on
{\mycal N}_{(a,c)}
, and one can apply the argument just given to the equations obtained by
u
-differentiating the vacuum Einstein equations to obtain smoothness on
{\mycal N}_{(a,c)}
of
\partial_{u}h_{\mu\nu}
and
\partial^{2}_{u}h_{AB}
.

Iterating this argument a finite number of times establishes the result.

\Box

As such, Lemma (
4.2
) will apply directly at
r=r_{2}
, once we have shown that all desired equations hold for
r\in(r_{0},r_{2})
. However, the argument that we are about to present is more complicated because, within our construction, for
r\in[r_{1},r_{2}]
we can only solve some of the Einstein equations. Fortunately the conditions of the Lemma are not independent, and the crux of the argument is to isolate and enforce the independent ones in a hierarchical way, proving as we progress both the continuity of the fields listed in the Lemma, and the satisfaction of the linearised Einstein equations, as well as their
u
-derivatives, on
{\mycal N}_{(r_{0},r_{2}]}
.

Given
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
k\geq 2
, in order to carry out a
{C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}}
-gluing the smooth solution on
{\mycal N}_{(r_{0},r_{1}]}
is extended to one on
{\mycal N}_{(r_{0},r_{2}]}
using a smooth interpolating field
{v}_{AB}
as in (
168
) and smooth gauge fields
{\overset{(1)}{{\zeta}}}
and
{\overset{(2)}{{\zeta}}}
as in (
165
), with the
u
-derivatives extended using the equations in Section (
3.7
). This guarantees that some of the Einstein equations are satisfied. It now remains to show that we can choose
{v}
,
{\overset{(1)}{{\zeta}}}
and
{\overset{(2)}{{\zeta}}}
to satisfy the remaining conditions of Lemma
4.2
together with the Einstein equations on
{\mycal N}_{(r_{0},r_{2}]}
. This can be done in three steps:

1.

The requirement of continuity of the fields
\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{\mu\nu}
for
0\leq p\leq k
at
\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}
imposes conditions on the given data
\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}
and
\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}
, as well as on the gauge fields
{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}
and
{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u}
and the gluing fields
{\overset{[p]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}
. We summarise these conditions here
(cf. Tables
4.1
-
4.2
), with further details presented in the next section:

), involves

,

)

,

,

r_{2}
when

.

\tilde{h}_{\mu\nu}
are the gauge-transformed fields

using the gauge fields
{\overset{(1)}{{\xi}}}{}

for

and

Table 4.2:
Fields used to ensure the continuity at
r_{2}
when
m\neq 0
. The notation, and the last two lines, are as in Table
4.1
. “KV of
{\mathbf{S}}
” stands for “Killing vector of
({\mathbf{S}},{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}})
”.

i.

\tilde{h}_{uu}
: Continuity of
\tilde{h}_{uu}
at
\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}
requires

\displaystyle\chi[\mathfrak{d}_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}]=\chi[\mathfrak{d}_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}]\,.

(171)

The continuity of
\chi^{{[\ker{\mathring{L}}]}}
at
r_{2}
requires the radial-charge matching-condition

{\overset{[2]}{Q}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}](\lambda)={\overset{[2]}{Q}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}](\lambda)\,,

(172)

for all
\lambda
with vanishing Hessian,
where
{\mathring{L}}
has been defined in (
9
).

When
m=0
, this condition is a gauge-invariant obstruction to gluing. The continuity of the remaining part
\chi^{{[(\ker{\mathring{L}})^{\perp}]}}
can be achieved using the gauge field

({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u})^{{[(\ker{\mathring{L}})^{\perp}]}}
(see (
83
)).

When
m\neq 0
, the gauge-invariant obstruction is (
172
) with constant
\lambda
. The remaining projection of
\chi
can be made continuous at
r_{2}

using the same gauge field as when
m=0
, together with
proper
conformal Killing vectors
{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]}
(which are zero unless we are on
S^{2}
).

ii.

\partial_{r}\tilde{h}_{uA}
: Continuity of
\partial_{r}\tilde{h}_{uA}
at
\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}

requires the radial-charge
matching condition

{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}]={\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}]\,,

(173)

as well as a suitable choice of the field
{\overset{[1]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}
. This condition is equivalent to the obstruction

{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}]={\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}]\,,

(174)

except if
{\mathbf{S}}=S^{2}
and
m\neq 0
. In this last case we use the gauge field
({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u})^{[=1]}
to get rid of the obstructions associated with proper conformal Killing vectors in (
174
).

iii.

\tilde{h}_{uA}
: Continuity of
\tilde{h}_{uA}
at
\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}
is achieved by a suitable choice of
{\overset{[4]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}_{AB}
and of

\partial_{u}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]}
.

iv.

\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{AB}
for
1\leq p\leq k
: This is addressed in Sections
4.2.2
,
4.2.6
and
4.3.2
. The continuity of
\partial^{p}_{u}\tilde{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}
requires

\displaystyle{\overset{[3,p]}{Q}_{A}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}]={\overset{[3,p]}{Q}_{A}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}]\,.

(175)

In the case
m=0
, the gauge fields
\partial_{u}^{p-1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}^{[({{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}})^{\perp}]}
can be used to achieve the matching of
({\overset{[3,p]}{Q}}{})^{[({{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}})^{\perp}]}
.
({\overset{[3,p]}{Q}}{})^{[{{\text{H}}}]}
provide obstructions for gluing on negatively curved manifolds of
{{\mathfrak{g}}}\geq 2
.
The requirement of continuity of
\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}
, which is non-trivial only on
\mathbb{T}^{2}
and manifolds of higher genus, is subjected to the obstruction (
283
), p.
283
, namely

\displaystyle\partial^{p}_{u}

\displaystyle q_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}-\partial^{p}_{u}q_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}=\alpha^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}(\alpha^{2}r_{2})^{k}\bigg[s\ {\overset{[p-k]}{q}}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{r_{1}}

(176)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2s}({{{{\varepsilon}}}}-\alpha^{2}s^{2})\partial_{u}^{p-1-k}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{s}\bigg]_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+(\alpha^{2}r_{2})^{p}(q_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}-q_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{1}})\,,

which simplifies considerably when
\alpha=0
.

In the case
m\neq 0
, the collection

\{\partial_{u}^{p}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}_{A}^{[({{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}})^{\perp}]}\}_{p=0}^{k}
of gauge fields can be used to match
\{{\overset{[3,p]}{Q}}{}^{[({{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}})^{\perp}]}\}_{p=1}^{k}
. The gauge fields
\partial_{u}^{p}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{H}}}]}
can be used to achieve the matching of
{\overset{[3,p]}{Q}}{}^{[{{\text{H}}}]}
.
The requirement of continuity of
\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}
is ensured by a suitable choice of

\overset{({p},0)}{\psi}_{2p+2}(r_{2}){\overset{[2p+2]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}_{AB}+\overset{({p},0)}{\psi}_{2p+1}(r_{2}){\overset{[2p+1]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}_{AB}\,.

(177)

v.

\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{uA}
for
1\leq p\leq k
: In the case
m=0
, the continuity of
\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{uA}
at
\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}
determines
{\overset{[p+4]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}
and
\partial_{u}^{p+1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]}
,
with additional obstructions coming from the kernel of the operator
\sum_{j=0}^{p}\overset{(p,j)}{\chi}_{p+4}P^{j}
. We provide an analysis of this kernel in Appendix
C.1
.

In the case
m\neq 0
, the continuity of

\{\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{uA}\}_{p=1}^{k}
at
\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}
is obtained by solving a system of equations for the collections

\{{\overset{[p]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}\}_{p=5}^{2k+4}

and
\{\partial_{u}^{p+1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]}\}_{p=1}^{k}
.

2.

Once the gauge fields and the fields
{\overset{[p]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}
with
1\leq p\leq k+4
in the case
m=0
, and with
1\leq p\leq 2k+4
in the case
m\neq 0
, have been determined, we construct the fields
\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{\mu\nu}
on
{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{1},r_{2})}
by setting
\tilde{h}_{AB}
according to (
165
) and using this to solve the transport equations of Section
3.3
-
3.7
:

i.

\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{ur}
for
0\leq p\leq k
: We set
\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{ur}|_{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}\equiv 0
, which guarantees both smoothness of
\tilde{h}_{ur}
and the validity of the equations, for all
i
,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E_{rr}|_{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}\equiv-\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E^{u}{}_{r}|_{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}\equiv\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E^{uu}|_{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}\,.

(178)

ii.

\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{uA}
for
0\leq p\leq k
: Using the representation formulae (
152
), with all
h_{\mu\nu}
’s there replaced by
\tilde{h}_{\mu\nu}
’s. This guarantees that on
{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}
we have

\partial_{u}^{p}\delta\mycal E_{rA}|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}\equiv-\partial_{u}^{p}\delta\mycal E^{u}{}_{A}|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}=0\,.

(179)

It follows that

\partial^{p}_{u}\delta\mycal E^{A}{}_{B}|_{{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}}=g^{AC}\partial^{p}_{u}\delta\mycal E_{CB}|_{{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}}\,.

(180)

The divergence identity

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mu}\delta\mycal E^{\mu}{}_{A}

(181)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r^{-2}\partial_{r}(r^{2}\delta\mycal E^{r}{}_{A})+\partial_{u}\delta\mycal E^{u}{}_{A}+{\mathring{D}}_{B}\delta\mycal E^{B}{}_{A}\,,

together with its
u
-derivatives, shows that we also have
\forall\ 0\leq i\leq k-1
,

\Big(r^{-2}\partial_{r}(r^{2}\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E^{r}{}_{A})+{\mathring{D}}_{B}\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E^{B}{}_{A}\Big)\Big|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}=0\,.

(182)

iii.

\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{uu}
for
0\leq p\leq k
: We impose
\partial_{r}\overset{[p]}{\chi}|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{1},r_{2})}}=0
with the initial conditions
\overset{[p]}{\chi}|_{r_{1}}=\overset{[p]}{\chi}[\mathfrak{d}_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}]
, together with the value of
\partial_{u}^{p-1}\tilde{h}_{uA}|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{1},r_{2})}}
determined in (ii) above. This ensures

\partial^{p}_{u}\delta\mycal E_{ru}|_{{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}}-\frac{1}{2r}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\partial^{p}_{u}\delta\mycal E_{rA}|_{{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}}=0\,.

(183)

Together with (
179
), Equation (
183
) ensures

\partial_{u}^{p}\delta{\mycal E}_{ru}|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}\equiv-\partial_{u}^{p}\delta\mycal E^{u}{}_{u}|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}=0\,.

(184)

iv.

\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{AB}
for
1\leq p\leq k
:
We use the representation formulae (
151
), with all
h_{\mu\nu}
’s replaced by
\tilde{h}_{\mu\nu}
’s. This ensures that

\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\big(\delta\partial^{p-1}_{u}\mycal E_{AB}\big)\big|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}=0\,.

(185)

The
u
differentiated divergence identity (
124
) with
\nu=r
reads

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\equiv\partial^{p}_{u}\delta\mycal E^{u}{}_{r}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\partial_{r}(r^{2}\delta\partial^{p-1}_{u}\mycal E^{r}{}_{r})

(186)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\sqrt{|\det{\mathring{\gamma}}|}}\partial_{A}(\sqrt{|\det{\mathring{\gamma}}|}\delta\partial^{p-1}_{u}\mycal E^{A}{}_{r})

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{r}g^{AB}\delta\partial^{p-1}_{u}\mycal E_{AB}\,,

so that, in view of (
179
) and (
184
), we have now

\forall\ 0\leq i\leq k\qquad 0=\frac{1}{r}g^{AB}\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E_{AB}\big|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}\,.

(187)

Together with (
185
), it follows that

\forall\ 0\leq i\leq k-1\qquad\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E_{AB}\big|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}=0\,.

(188)

Equation (
182
) then gives,
\forall\ 0\leq i\leq k-1
,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=r^{-2}\partial_{r}(r^{2}\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E^{r}{}_{A})|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}

(189)

\displaystyle=-r^{-2}\partial_{r}(r^{2}\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E_{uA})|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}\,,

where we have used

\displaystyle\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E^{r}{}_{A}|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}

\displaystyle=-g_{uu}\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E_{rA}|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}-\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E_{uA}|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}

\displaystyle=-\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E_{uA}|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}\,;

note that the last equality is justified by (
179
). Continuity at
r_{1}
, where all the fields
\partial_{u}^{i}\mycal E_{\mu\nu}
,
i\in\mathbb{N}
, vanish when the data there arise from a smooth solution of linearised Einstein equations, together with (
189
) implies that

\forall\ 0\leq i\leq k-1\quad\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E^{r}{}_{A}|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}=0=\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E_{uA}|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}\,.

(190)

Meanwhile, the divergence identity for the Einstein tensor with a free lower index
u
now reduces to
\forall\ 0\leq i\leq k-1
,

0\equiv\partial_{u}^{i}\nabla_{\mu}\delta\mycal E^{\mu}{}_{u}\big|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}=r^{-2}\partial_{r}(r^{2}\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E^{r}{}_{u})\big|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}\,.

(191)

Continuity and vanishing at
r_{1}

together with (
178
)
and (
184
)
implies that
\forall\ 0\leq i\leq k-1

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E_{uu}\big|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}=-\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E^{r}{}_{u}\big|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}

(192)

\displaystyle=\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E^{rr}\big|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}\,.

3.

The construction above guarantees the continuity of
\tilde{h}_{uu}
,
\partial_{u}\tilde{h}_{AB}
,
\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{uA}
with
0\leq p\leq k
, and
\partial_{u}^{i}\tilde{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}
with

at
r_{2}
. Continuity of the fields
\partial_{r}\partial^{p}_{u}\tilde{h}_{uA}
and
\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{uu}
for

and
\partial^{i}_{u}\tilde{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}
for
2\leq i\leq k
at
r_{2}
follows now
by induction: The explicit form (
130
) of the equation
\delta\mycal E_{uA}=0
together with the continuity of
\tilde{h}_{uu}
,
\tilde{h}_{uA}
and
\partial_{u}\tilde{h}_{AB}
at
