ensures the continuity of
\partial_{r}\partial_{u}\tilde{h}_{uA}
at
r_{2}
.
This guarantees continuity of
{\overset{[1,1]}{Q}}{}
and
{\overset{[4,1]}{Q}}{}
, hence of
{\overset{[3,2]}{Q}}{}^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]}
in view of (
117
) , namely

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\pi^{A}{\overset{[3,k]}{Q}_{A}}{}\,d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}

\displaystyle={\alpha^{2}}{\overset{[1,k-1]}{Q}}{}(\pi^{A})+2m{\overset{[4,k-1]}{Q}}{}(\pi^{A})\,,

(193)

with
k=2
.

This, together with point
1
.iv
ensures continuity of
{\overset{[3,2]}{Q}_{A}}{}
at
r_{2}
, which in turn renders
\partial^{2}_{u}\tilde{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}
continuous there.

Meanwhile the explicit form (
136
) of
\delta\mycal E_{uu}=0
together with smoothness at
r_{2}
of
\tilde{h}_{uu}
,
\tilde{h}_{uA}
and
\partial_{u}\tilde{h}_{AB}
, leads to continuity of
\partial_{u}\tilde{h}_{uu}
at
r_{2}
.

Now, suppose that the continuity of the fields
\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{uu}
,
\partial_{r}\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{uA}
and
\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}_{AB}
has been achieved up to
p=k-1
.
It follows that we have
\partial^{k-2}_{u}\delta\mycal E_{uA}|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}}=0
and thus
\partial_{r}{\overset{[1,k-1]}{Q}}{}(\pi^{A})|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}}=0=\partial_{r}{\overset{[4,k-1]}{Q}}{}(\pi^{A})|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}}
(compare (
134
)). Further, (
193
) implies that the
({\overset{[3,k]}{Q}}{})^{[{{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}}]}
-part of the radial charges on
S^{2}
and
\mathbb{T}^{2}
are continuous. Meanwhile, recall that continuity of the radial charge
({\overset{[3,k]}{Q}}{})^{[({{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}})^{\perp}]}
was ensured using the gauge field
\partial_{u}^{k-1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}_{A}
, while on higher-genus sections continuity of the charge
({\overset{[3,k]}{Q}}{})^{[{{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}}]}=({\overset{[3,k]}{Q}}{})^{[{{\text{H}}}]}
is achieved by
\partial_{u}^{k}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{H}}}]}
when
m\neq 0
and is an obstruction whose continuity has to be assumed when
m=0
. We have now the continuity of
({\overset{[3,k]}{Q}}{})=({\overset{[3,k]}{Q}}{})^{[({{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}})^{\perp}]}+({\overset{[3,k]}{Q}}{})^{[({{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}})]}
, which ensures the continuity of
\partial^{k}_{u}\tilde{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}
at
r_{2}
.

Next, by differentiation of (
130
) we obtain the explicit form of (
190
) with
i=k-1

\displaystyle r^{2}\partial_{r}\left(\frac{\partial_{u}^{k}h_{uA}}{r^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\Big[{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\partial_{u}^{k-1}{h}_{uB}-{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\partial_{u}^{k-1}{h}_{uA}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+\partial_{u}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\partial_{u}^{k-1}{h}_{AB}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\left({{\varepsilon}}-r^{2}\alpha^{2}-\frac{2m}{r}\right)\partial_{r}^{2}\partial_{u}^{k-1}h_{uA}

\displaystyle\quad+(2\alpha^{2}+\frac{4m}{r^{3}})\partial_{u}^{k-1}h_{uA}+\partial_{r}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\partial_{u}^{k-1}h}_{uu}\,.

This equation, together with the continuity of

\partial^{k-1}_{u}\tilde{h}_{uu}
,

\partial_{u}^{k-1}\tilde{h}_{uA}
and
\partial^{k}_{u}\tilde{h}_{AB}
,
ensures the continuity of
\partial_{r}\partial_{u}^{k}\tilde{h}_{uA}
at
r_{2}
.

Finally, the explicit form of (
192
) with
i=k-1
,
i.e.

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\partial_{u}^{k-1}\delta\mycal E_{uu}\big|_{{\mycal N}_{[r_{1},r_{2})}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\Big\{2\Big[\partial_{u}^{k}+\big({\alpha^{2}r^{2}-{{\varepsilon}}+\frac{2m}{r}}\big)\partial_{r}+\frac{3m}{r^{2}}-\frac{{{\varepsilon}}}{r}\Big]{\mathring{D}}^{A}{h}_{uA}

\displaystyle\phantom{\frac{1}{r^{2}}\Big[}-{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\partial_{u}^{k-1}{h}_{uu}-({\alpha^{2}r^{2}-{{\varepsilon}}+\frac{2m}{r}}\big)\big(\frac{{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\partial_{u}^{k-1}{h}_{AB}}{r^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\phantom{\frac{1}{r^{2}}\Big[}-2r\partial_{u}^{k}{h}_{uu}-2({\alpha^{2}r^{2}-{{\varepsilon}}+\frac{2m}{r}}\big)\partial_{r}(r\partial_{u}^{k-1}{h}_{uu})\Big\}\,,

together with smoothness at
r_{2}
of
\partial_{u}^{k-1}\tilde{h}_{uu}
,
\partial_{u}^{k-1}\tilde{h}_{uA}
and
\partial^{k-1}_{u}\tilde{h}_{AB}
, ensures the continuity of
\partial^{k}_{u}\tilde{h}_{uu}
at
r_{2}
.

We now pass to a more detailed presentation of some of the arguments above.

4.2
Continuity at
r_{2}

4.2.1
Gluing of
\delta\beta

The sets of gauge functions
\partial_{u}^{i}{\overset{(1)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u}|_{\tilde{{\mathbf{S}}}_{1}}
and
\partial_{u}^{i}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u}|_{\tilde{{\mathbf{S}}}_{2}}

with
i\leq k+1
allow
us to transform
\partial_{u}^{j}\delta\tilde{\beta}
for
j\leq k
to zero on
\tilde{{\mathbf{S}}}_{1}
and
\tilde{{\mathbf{S}}}_{2}
,
and hence, by invoking the
ur-
component of the linearised Einstein equation (
50
), on the whole
{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{1},r_{2}]}
. In what follows, we assume that this gauge choice has been made, and set
\partial_{u}^{j}\delta\tilde{\beta}=0
for
j\leq k
everywhere.

Furthermore, to simplify notation we omit the “
|_{\tilde{{\mathbf{S}}}_{j}}
” on all gauge fields, with the understanding that all
{\overset{(1)}{{\xi}}}{}
fields, and their
u
-derivatives, are evaluated on
\tilde{{\mathbf{S}}}_{1}
, while all
{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}
fields, and their
u
-derivatives, are evaluated on
\tilde{{\mathbf{S}}}_{2}
, unless indicated otherwise.

4.2.2
Freezing part of the gauge

First, recall that the radial charge
{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}
is gauge invariant except in the case
m\neq 0
on
S^{2}
. In this case, we can use the gauge field
({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u})^{[=1]}
for the matching of
{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}(\pi_{A})
when the conformal Killing field
\pi_{A}
is such that
{\mathring{D}}^{A}\pi_{A}\neq 0
, i.e. a proper conformal Killing vector field. According to (
63
), this is achieved by choosing

({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u})^{[=1]}
so that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}\pi^{A}(r_{2}^{4}\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{uA}\big|_{r_{2}}-6m{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u})d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}=\int_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}\pi^{A}(r_{1}^{4}\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{uA}|_{r_{1}})d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}\,.

(194)

However, for Killing vector fields the terms explicitly involving
m
integrate-out to zero, and we obtain an obstruction to gluing.

Next, we determine the gauge fields needed to ensure continuity of
\chi
.
For this, we evaluate the function

\chi
of (
81
) at
{\mathbf{S}}_{1}
:

\chi[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}]=\Big(-\delta V+\frac{r}{2}\partial_{r}\big(r^{2}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\delta U_{A}\big)+\frac{1}{2}r{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\check{h}}_{AB}\Big)\Big|_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}\,,

(195)

and use the transformation law (
83
), i.e.

\chi\mapsto\chi-\frac{1}{2}({\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}+2{{\varepsilon}}){\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}{\xi}^{{u}}+3m{\mathring{D}}^{B}\xi_{B}\,,

(196)

to do the gluing.
As already pointed out, and clearly seen from this equation, the integral average of
\chi
over
{\mathbf{S}}
, i.e.
{\overset{[2]}{Q}}{}{(\lambda=1)}
, is gauge-invariant regardless of topology or of the value
m
. Now,

when
m=0
,
we can find a function

{({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u})^{{[(\ker{\mathring{L}})^{\perp}]}}}
so that

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\mathring{L}{({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u})^{{[(\ker{\mathring{L}})^{\perp}]}}}

\displaystyle\equiv-\frac{1}{2}({\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}+2{{\varepsilon}}){\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}{({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u})^{{[(\ker{\mathring{L}})^{\perp}]}}}

(197)

\displaystyle=\chi^{{[(\ker{\mathring{L}})^{\perp}]}}[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}]-\chi^{{[(\ker{\mathring{L}})^{\perp}]}}[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}]\,.

The smooth fields
({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u})^{{[\ker{\mathring{L}}]}}
are left arbitrary at this stage.

When
m\neq 0
,
to ensure the desired gluing we use the field
({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u})^{{[(\ker{\mathring{L}})^{\perp}]}}
determined in (
197
) together with the equation

(3m{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{A})^{{[\ker{\mathring{L}}]}}=\chi^{{{[\ker{\mathring{L}}]}}}[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}]-\chi^{{{[\ker{\mathring{L}}]}}}[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}]\,.

(198)

On
S^{2}
this determines the proper-conformal-Killing-vectors part of
{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]}
, but does not provide a useful equation for the remaining topologies as there are no proper conformal Killing vectors there.

Next, we
determine the gauge fields
\partial^{i}_{u}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}
for
0\leq i\leq k
as follows:
We evaluate the
radially-constant covector field

{\overset{[3,i+1]}{Q}_{A}}{}
of (
116
) at
{\mathbf{S}}_{1}
:

\displaystyle{\overset{[3,i+1]}{Q}_{A}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}]=

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{B}\Big(2r\partial_{u}^{i+1}{\check{h}}_{AB}-V\partial_{r}(\partial_{u}^{i}{\check{h}}_{AB})

(199)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\partial_{r}\big(r^{4}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}\partial_{u}^{i}{\check{h}}_{uB}]\big)+(P-{{\varepsilon}})\partial_{u}^{i}{\check{h}}_{AB}\Big)\Big|_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}

\displaystyle+\big({{\alpha^{2}}r^{4}}\partial_{r}\partial_{u}^{i}{\check{h}}_{uA}+{2m(3\partial_{u}^{i}{\check{h}}_{uA}+r\partial_{r}\partial_{u}^{i}{\check{h}}_{uA})}\big)\big|_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}\,.

The idea now is to use the transformation law (
118
) to find gauge-vector fields such that

\displaystyle 2(\widehat{\operatorname{{L}}}\partial_{u}^{i}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{[({{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}})^{\perp}]})_{A}+6m\partial_{u}^{i+1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}_{A}

(200)

\displaystyle\qquad=\underbrace{({\overset{[3,i+1]}{Q}_{A}}{})[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}]-({\overset{[3,i+1]}{Q}_{A}}{})[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}]}_{=:\Delta{\overset{[3,i+1]}{Q}_{A}}{}}\,.

We start by noting that the image of
\widehat{\operatorname{{L}}}
is orthogonal to
{{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}}
; (cf. Proposition
C.6
, p.
C.6
).
(The spaces
H
and
CKV
can be viewed as being orthogonal to each other, in that
H
a) is trivial on
S^{2}
; b) can be set to
\{0\}
on
\mathbb{T}^{2}
being a subset of
CKV
; c) is non-trivial on the remaining topologies but
CKV
is trivial there.)

Note next that for
\pi^{A}\in{{\text{CKV}}}
we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\pi^{A}{\overset{[3,i]}{Q}_{A}}{}\,d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}

(201)

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\pi^{A}(\alpha^{2}r^{4}\partial_{r}\partial_{u}^{i-1}{\check{h}}_{uA}+2m(3\partial_{u}^{i-1}{\check{h}}_{uA}+r\partial_{r}\partial_{u}^{i-1}{\check{h}}_{uA}))\,d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}

\displaystyle={\alpha^{2}}{\overset{[1,i-1]}{Q}}{}(\pi^{A})+2m{\overset{[4,i-1]}{Q}}{}(\pi^{A})\,.

Thus the continuity of
{\overset{[3,i]}{Q}}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]}
will be guaranteed by the continuity of
{\overset{[1,i-1]}{Q}}{}
and
{\overset{[4,i-1]}{Q}}{}
, with the latter two being
addressed in point
3
, p.
3
.
Hence we ignore for the moment the condition
\Delta{\overset{[3,i]}{Q}}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]}=0

and consider the system of equations

\displaystyle\displaystyle\small 2(\widehat{\operatorname{{L}}}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{[({{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}})^{\perp}]})_{A}+6m\partial_{u}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}_{A}^{[({{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}})^{\perp}]}

\displaystyle=\big(\Delta{\overset{[3,1]}{Q}_{A}}{}\big)^{[({{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}})^{\perp}]}\,,

(202)

\displaystyle 6m\partial_{u}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{H}}}]}

\displaystyle=\big(\Delta{\overset{[3,1]}{Q}_{A}}{}\big)^{[{{\text{H}}}]}\,,

(203)

\displaystyle\ \,\vdots

\displaystyle 2(\widehat{\operatorname{{L}}}\partial_{u}^{k-2}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{[({{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}})^{\perp}]})_{A}+6m\partial_{u}^{k-1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}_{A}^{[({{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}})^{\perp}]}

\displaystyle=\big(\Delta{\overset{[3,k-1]}{Q}_{A}}{}\big)^{[({{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}})^{\perp}]}\,,

(204)

\displaystyle 6m\partial_{u}^{k-1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{H}}}]}

\displaystyle=\big(\Delta{\overset{[3,k-1]}{Q}_{A}}{}\big)^{[{{\text{H}}}]}\,,

(205)

\displaystyle 2(\widehat{\operatorname{{L}}}\partial_{u}^{k-1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{[({{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}})^{\perp}]})_{A}+6m\partial_{u}^{k}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}_{A}^{[({{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}})^{\perp}]}

\displaystyle=\big(\Delta{\overset{[3,k]}{Q}_{A}}{}\big)^{[({{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}})^{\perp}]}\,,

(206)

\displaystyle 6m\partial_{u}^{k}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{H}}}]}

\displaystyle=\big(\Delta{\overset{[3,k]}{Q}_{A}}{}\big)^{[{{\text{H}}}]}\,.

(207)

In the case
m\neq 0
we set, e.g.,

\partial_{u}^{k}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}_{A}^{[({{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}})^{\perp}]}=0\,,

(208)

and we can solve (
206
)-(
207
) to guarantee continuity
of
{\overset{[3,k]}{Q}}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}}}^{\perp}]}
on
\mathbb{T}^{2}
and
S^{2}
, and
{\overset{[3,k]}{Q}}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{H}}}]}
on other topologies.
The solution of (
206
) can then be inserted into (
204
) to obtain a solution of (
204
)-(
205
). Continuing in this way, after a finite number of steps we obtain a solution of the system
(
202
)-(
207
).

Clearly the argument breaks down

when
m=0
, in which case we can still solve (
200
)
for
\partial_{u}^{i}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{[({{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}})^{\perp}]}_{A}
, but the equations

\big(\Delta{\overset{[3,i]}{Q}_{A}}{}\big)^{[{{\text{H}}}]}=0\,,\quad i=0,\ldots,k\,,

provide obstructions to gluing.

4.2.3
Continuity of
\tilde{h}_{uu}

It follows from the pointwise radial conservation of the function
\chi
defined in (
81
) that the gluing of
\tilde{h}_{uu}
requires

\chi[\mathfrak{d}_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}]=\chi[\mathfrak{d}_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}]\,.

(209)

This is achieved by the condition
{\overset{[2]}{Q}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}]={\overset{[2]}{Q}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}]
together with
formulae (
197
)-(
198
) for the projected
gauge fields
({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u})^{{[(\ker{\mathring{L}})^{\perp}]}}
and

{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]}
.

4.2.4
Continuity of
\partial_{r}\tilde{h}_{uA}

Taking into account the allowed gauge perturbations to Bondi data, the gluing of
\partial_{r}\tilde{{h}}_{uA}
requires
\tilde{h}_{AB}
to satisfy on
{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{(r_{1},r_{2})}
,

\displaystyle r_{2}^{4}\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{uA}|_{\tilde{{\mathbf{S}}}_{2}}

\displaystyle=2r_{2}{L_{1}}({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u})_{A}+2r_{2}^{2}{\mathring{D}}^{B}C({\overset{(2)}{{\zeta}}})_{AB}{-6m{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u}}

(210)

\displaystyle\quad+\Phi_{A}(x^{C})+{\mathring{D}}^{B}{h}_{AB}|_{\tilde{{\mathbf{S}}}_{2}}-2\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\hat{\kappa}_{1}(s){\mathring{D}}^{B}\tilde{h}_{AB}\,ds\,.

We have

{\mathring{D}}_{A}({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u})^{[1]}=0\,,\qquad{L_{1}}\big(({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u})^{{[\ker{\mathring{L}}]}}\big)=0\,,\qquad C({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]})_{AB}=0\,,

so that the gauge-part of the right-hand side of (
210
) involving
{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u}
, except for the term explicitly involving
m
, depends only on

({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u})^{{[(\ker{\mathring{L}})^{\perp}]}}

and has already been determined in terms of
the given data by (
197
).
When
m\neq 0
and
{\mathbf{S}}\approx S^{2}

the remaining part, which contributes to
-6m{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u}
,
is already known from (
194
).
For the remaining topologies the kernel of
\mathring{L}

consists of constants, which do not contribute to the right-hand side. Thus in all cases, the terms in (
210
) involving
{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u}
are
either vanishing
or already determined.

To clarify the freedom left, let us rewrite (
210
) as an equation for
{\overset{[1]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}\equiv\langle\hat{\kappa}_{1},{w}_{AB}\rangle
, where
{w}_{AB}
is as in (
165
):

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\overset{[1]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}=\

\displaystyle{\tilde{\Phi}}_{A}(x^{C})+{\mathring{D}}^{B}\Big[r_{2}^{2}C({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{{[{{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}]}})_{AB}

(211)

\displaystyle\quad-2\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\hat{\kappa}_{1}(s)(1-\phi)s^{2}C({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{{[{{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}]}}(s))_{AB}\,ds\Big]\,,

where the already known fields such as
{\check{h}}_{uA}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}
,
{v}_{AB}
and
({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u})^{{[(\ker{\mathring{L}})^{\perp}]}}
,
as well as the gauge fields
{\overset{(1)}{{\xi}}}{}^{A}
and
{\overset{(1)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u}
have been collected into the term
\tilde{\Phi}_{A}
.

Now, the divergence operator on traceless symmetric two-tensors in two dimensions is elliptic; it has a cokernel spanned on conformal Killing vectors; on
S^{2}
it has no kernel
(see Appendix
C.3
).
It follows that (
211
)
determines a unique tensor field
{\overset{[1]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}
on
S^{2}
provided that the source term
\tilde{\Phi}
is
L^{2}
-orthogonal to the cokernel. This orthogonality is guaranteed by the condition
{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}]={\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}]
and either the gauge invariance of
{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}

in the case
m=0
, or by a suitable choice of
the gauge field
({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u})^{[=1]}
if
m\neq 0
.
In other words, if the radial charge
{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}
of the linearised field on
{\mycal N}|_{(r_{0},r_{1}]}
coincides with that of the linearised field on
{\mycal N}|_{[r_{2},r_{3})}
, the field
{\overset{[1]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}
satisfying (
211
) exists, and is uniquely determined in terms of the given data and the gauge field

{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}]}
.

By a similar analysis for the remaining topologies, (
211
) determines
{\overset{[1]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}_{AB}
uniquely in terms of the given data and the gauge field
{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}^{[{{{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}}]}

provided that the radial charges
{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}
at
r=r_{1}
and
r=r_{2}
coincide.

4.2.5
Continuity of
\tilde{h}_{uA}

Taking into account the allowed gauge perturbations of Bondi data, it follows from (
34
)
and (
67
) that the continuity of
\tilde{{h}}_{uA}
at
r_{2}
can be achieved by choosing
{\overset{[4]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}^{{[{{\text{TT}^{\perp}}}]}}
so that

\displaystyle{h}_{uA}|_{\tilde{{\mathbf{S}}}_{2}}

\displaystyle+{L_{1}}({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u})_{A}+r_{2}^{2}\big[\partial_{u}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}_{A}}+(\alpha^{2}+\frac{2m}{r^{3}_{2}}){\mathring{D}}_{A}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u}\big]

(212)

\displaystyle=X_{A}(x^{C})+\frac{1}{3}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\tilde{{h}}_{AB}\left(\frac{2\hat{\kappa}_{1}(s)}{r_{2}}+\hat{\kappa}_{4}(s)r_{2}^{2}\right)ds\,,

where
X_{A}
depends only on data at
r_{1}
. More explicitly:

\displaystyle\frac{r_{2}^{2}}{3}

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\overset{[4]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}^{{[{{\text{TT}^{\perp}}}]}}\equiv\frac{r_{2}^{2}}{3}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\langle\hat{\kappa}_{4},{w}_{AB}\rangle

(213)

\displaystyle=r_{2}^{2}\partial_{u}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}_{A}}-\tilde{X}_{A}(x^{C})-\frac{2}{3r_{2}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\overset{[1]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}+(\alpha^{2}+\frac{2m}{r^{3}})r_{2}^{2}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{2}{3}\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\left(\frac{2\hat{\kappa}_{1}(s)}{r_{2}}+\hat{\kappa}_{4}(s)r_{2}^{2}\right)(1-\phi)s^{2}{\mathring{D}}^{B}C({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{[{{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}]})_{AB}\,ds

\displaystyle=r_{2}^{2}\partial_{u}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}_{A}}-\tilde{X}_{A}(x^{C})-\frac{2r_{2}}{3}{\mathring{D}}^{B}C({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{[{{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}]})_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{2}{3r_{2}}\tilde{\Phi}_{A}+(\alpha^{2}+\frac{2m}{r^{3}})r_{2}^{2}{\mathring{D}}_{A}({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u})^{{[\ker{\mathring{L}}]}}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{2r_{2}^{2}}{3}\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\hat{\kappa}_{4}(s)(1-\phi)s^{2}{\mathring{D}}^{B}C({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{[{{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}]})_{AB}\,ds\,,

where once again the already known fields such as

{h}_{uA}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}
,
{v}_{AB}
and

({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u})^{{[(\ker{\mathring{L}})^{\perp}]}}

as well as the gauge fields
{\overset{(1)}{{\xi}}}{}^{A}
and
{\overset{(1)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u}
have been collected into the term
\tilde{X}_{A}
.
Since
\ker(C)=\text{im}({\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!)^{\perp}
, we can use the freedom in choosing the smooth fields

\big(\partial_{u}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}+(\alpha^{2}+\frac{2m}{r^{3}}){\mathring{D}}_{A}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u})^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]}

(214)

to arrange that the right-hand side of (
213
) lies in the image of
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}
.
(Note that the first term in (
214
) has not been determined so far, while the remaining ones have only been determined if
m\neq 0
.)
It follows that (
213
) can be solved uniquely for both
{\overset{[4]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}_{AB}
and
\partial_{u}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]}
in terms of
\partial_{u}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}]}

and
{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}]}
when
\alpha=0=m
. For
\alpha,m\neq 0
there remains some freedom
in the gauge field
({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}^{u})^{{[\ker{\mathring{L}}]}}
, made clear by (
214
). On sections with higher genus, it follows from the surjectivity of
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!
(Lemma
C.4
, Appendix
C.3
) that (
213
) determines
{\overset{[4]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}_{AB}
uniquely in terms of
\partial_{u}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}
.

A conserved, gauge dependent, radial charge involving
h_{uA}
and
\partial_{r}h_{uA}
is derived in Appendix
E.1
.

4.2.6
Continuity of
\partial_{u}\tilde{h}_{AB}

The case
m=0
:

It follows from the pointwise radial conservation of
{\overset{[3,1]}{Q}_{A}}{}
that the gluing of
\partial_{u}\tilde{h}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}_{AB}
requires

{\overset{[3,1]}{Q}_{A}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}]={\overset{[3,1]}{Q}_{A}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}]\,.

(215)

This is achieved on
S^{2}
by the condition
{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}]={\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}]
together with the expressions (
200
) with
i=0
for the gauge field
{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}^{[{{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}]}_{A}
.

For the remaining topologies
we use (
101
) to obtain an equation for
q_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}
:

\displaystyle\partial_{r}q_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}=\partial_{r}\Big[r\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}-\frac{1}{2}V\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}-\frac{1}{2r}V{\check{h}}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\Big]=\frac{{\alpha^{2}}}{r}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\,.

(216)

Integrating, we obtain

\displaystyle q_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}-q_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}

\displaystyle=\alpha^{2}\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\hat{\kappa}_{1}(s)\tilde{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\,ds

\displaystyle=\alpha^{2}{\overset{[1]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}+\alpha^{2}\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\hat{\kappa}_{1}(s){v}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\,ds\,.

This provides an equation for
{\overset{[1]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}
when
\alpha\neq 0
:

\displaystyle\alpha^{2}{\overset{[1]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}

\displaystyle=q_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}-q_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}-\alpha^{2}\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\hat{\kappa}_{1}(s){v}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\,ds\,.

(217)

When
\alpha=0
,
\partial_{u}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}
is part of the radially conserved charge
q_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}
of (
101
). In this case, the continuity of
\partial_{u}\tilde{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}
at
r_{2}
imposes the radial conservation law

\displaystyle q_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}]=q_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}]\,.

(218)

The case
m\neq 0
:

Taking into account the allowed gauge perturbations of Bondi data, it follows from (
95
) that we need to satisfy the equation

\displaystyle\partial_{u}{h}_{AB}|_{\tilde{{\mathbf{S}}}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\overset{(1)}{\tilde{\Psi}}_{AB}(r_{2},x^{A})-2r_{2}^{2}C(\partial_{u}{\overset{(2)}{{\zeta}}})_{AB}

\displaystyle\qquad+r_{2}\left({{\varepsilon}}-{\alpha^{2}}r_{2}^{2}-\frac{2m}{r_{2}}\right)C({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{})_{AB}

\displaystyle\qquad+(\alpha^{2}r_{2}+\frac{1}{3r_{2}}P){\overset{[1]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}-(mr_{2}{-}\frac{2r^{2}}{3}P){\overset{[4]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}

\displaystyle\qquad+2\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}(\alpha^{2}r_{2}+\frac{1}{3r_{2}}P)(1-\phi)\kappa_{1}(s)s^{2}C({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{})_{AB}

\displaystyle\qquad-2\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}(mr_{2}{-}\frac{2r^{2}}{3}P)\kappa_{4}(s)s^{2}C({\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{})_{AB}\,.

Since
C(\xi)^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}=0
for any vector field
\xi
, and similarly the image of
P
is orthogonal to
TT
,
continuity of
\partial_{u}\tilde{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}
at
\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}
requires
{\overset{[1]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}
and
{\overset{[4]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}
to satisfy

\displaystyle\partial_{u}{h}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}_{AB}|_{\tilde{{\mathbf{S}}}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\overset{(1)}{\tilde{\Psi}}{{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}}_{AB}(r_{2},x^{A})+\alpha^{2}r_{2}{\overset{[1]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}_{AB}-mr_{2}{\overset{[4]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}_{AB}\,,

(219)

which can be achieved by setting, for example,
{\overset{[1]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}_{AB}=0
and solving (
219
) for
{\overset{[4]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}_{AB}
.

4.3
Higher derivatives

Recall from Section
3.7
that the terms
\overset{(i,j)}{\chi}(s,r)
are linear combinations of
\hat{\kappa}_{j}(s)
’s with
1\leq j\leq i_{[m]}
,
j\neq 2,3
, with
i_{[m]}=i+4
when
m=0
and
i_{[m]}=2i+4
when
m\neq 0
,
where
i_{[m]}
is not necessarily optimal unless
i=0
; see Appendix
B
. We shall henceforth write them as

\overset{(i,j)}{\chi}(s,r)=\sum_{\ell=1}^{i_{[m]}}\overset{(i,j)}{\chi}_{\ell}(r)\,\hat{\kappa}_{\ell}(s)\,,\quad\text{with}\ \,\overset{(i,j)}{\chi}_{2}=0=\overset{(i,j)}{\chi}_{3}\,.

(220)

Similarly we write, for
i\geq 1
,

\overset{(i,j)}{\psi}(s,r)=\sum_{\ell=1}^{i_{[m]}-1}\overset{(i,j)}{\psi}_{\ell}(r)\,\hat{\kappa}_{\ell}(s)\,,\quad\text{with}\ \,\overset{(i,j)}{\psi}_{2}=0=\overset{(i,j)}{\psi}_{3}\,,

(221)

where again the upper bound
i_{[m]}-1
is not necessarily optimal unless
i=1
.

4.3.1
Continuity of
\partial_{u}^{i}\tilde{h}_{uA}

While this is not needed for the current argument, we note that a conserved, gauge dependent, radial charge involving
\partial_{u}h_{uA}
is derived in Appendix
E.2
.

To continue, let

k
be the order at which we want to perform the gluing, i.e. the number of
u
-derivatives of
h_{\mu\nu}
which we want to be continuous, and let
1\leq{p}\leq k
.

The case
m=0
:

After performing a gauge transformation, Equation (
152
) at order
i={p}
together with (
41
) provides a gluing equation of the form,

\displaystyle\partial_{u}^{{p}}{\check{h}}_{uA}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2r_{2}^{2}}{L_{1}}({\mathring{D}}^{C}\partial_{u}^{{p}-1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{C})-\Big(\partial_{u}^{{p}+1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{2}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}^{C}\partial_{u}^{{p}-1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{C}\Big)

\displaystyle+\overset{({p})}{X}_{A}+\sum_{0\leq j+\ell\leq{p},\ell\neq{p}}\overset{({p},j,\ell)}{\chi}(r)\partial_{r}^{j}{\mathring{D}}^{B}P^{\ell}{h}_{AB}

\displaystyle+\sum_{0\leq j+\ell\leq{p},\ell\neq{p}}\overset{({p},j,\ell)}{\chi}(r)\partial_{r}^{j}{\mathring{D}}^{B}P^{\ell}\Big(2r^{2}C({\overset{(2)}{{\zeta}}})_{AB}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{j=0}^{{p}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\overset{({p},j)}{\chi}(s,r_{2})P^{j}\tilde{h}_{AB}(s)\,ds

which equals

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle-\partial_{u}^{{p}+1}{{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]}}+\overset{({p})}{\tilde{X}}_{A}+\sum_{j=0}^{{p}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{{p}+4}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\overset{({p},j)}{\chi}_{\ell}(r_{2})\hat{\kappa}_{\ell}(s)P^{j}{w}_{AB}(s)\,ds\,;\\
\displaystyle-\partial_{u}^{{p}+1}{{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}}+\overset{({p})}{\tilde{X}}_{A}+\sum_{j=0}^{{p}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{{p}+4}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\overset{({p},j)}{\chi}_{\ell}(r_{2})\hat{\kappa}_{\ell}(s)P^{j}{w}_{AB}(s)\,ds,\end{array}\right.

with the first case arising when
p\leq k-2
and the second for
p\in\{k-1,k\}
,
and which we rewrite as

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle-\partial_{u}^{{p}+1}{{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]}}+\overset{({p})}{\tilde{X}}_{A}+\sum_{j=0}^{{p}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{{p}+4}\overset{({p},j)}{\chi}_{\ell}(r_{2}){\mathring{D}}^{B}P^{j}{\overset{[\ell]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}_{AB},&\hbox{${p}\leq k-2$;}\\
\displaystyle-\partial_{u}^{{p}+1}{{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}}+\overset{({p})}{\tilde{X}}_{A}+\sum_{j=0}^{{p}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{{p}+4}\overset{({p},j)}{\chi}_{\ell}(r_{2}){\mathring{D}}^{B}P^{j}{\overset{[\ell]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}_{AB},&\hbox{${p}=k-1,k$.}\end{array}\right.

Here we used the fact that
P|_{{{\text{TT}}}}=0
, that the fields
\partial_{u}^{\ell}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{C}^{{[{{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}]}}
with
\ell\leq k-1
are already known from Section
4.2.2
, and we included them, together with all
other already known fields, in
\overset{({p})}{\tilde{X}}_{A}=\overset{({p})}{\tilde{X}}_{A}(r,x^{A})
.
Recall that
{\overset{[1]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}
has been determined in Section
4.2.4
,
{\overset{[4]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}
in (
213
), and we further set

{\overset{[2]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}={\overset{[3]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}=0\,.

For the sake of induction, suppose that the fields
{\overset{[\ell]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}
with
4\leq\ell\leq{p}+3
are known.
Together with
\overset{({p})}{\tilde{X}}_{A}
and
\partial_{u}^{p}{\check{h}}_{uA}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}
we collect them into a term
\overset{({p})}{\hat{\tilde{X}}}_{A}
, so that the requirement that
\partial_{u}^{{p}}\tilde{h}_{uA}
be continuous at
r_{2}
results in an equation of the form

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{B}\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{{p}}\overset{({p},j)}{\chi}_{{p}+4}(r_{2})P^{j}{\overset{[{p}+4]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}_{AB}\Big)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\partial_{u}^{{p}+1}{{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]}}-\overset{({p})}{\hat{\tilde{X}}}_{A},&\hbox{${p}\leq k-2$;}\\
\displaystyle\partial_{u}^{{p}+1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}_{A}}-\overset{({p})}{\hat{\tilde{X}}}_{A},&\hbox{${p}=k-1,k$.}\end{array}\right.

Now, the operator at the left-hand side of this equation,
namely

{\overset{(p)}{{\chi}}}{}:={\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\circ\sum_{j=0}^{{p}}\overset{({p},j)}{\chi}_{{p}+4}(r_{2})P^{j}{\overset{[{p}+4]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}_{AB}\,,

(226)

is elliptic,
of order
2p+1
, and
has a non-trivial cokernel on
S^{2}
and
\mathbb{T}^{2}
.
(For example, the cokernel on
S^{2}
is the space of spherical harmonic vectors with index
1\leq\ell\leq{p}+1
, see Appendix
C.5.2
; it is
30
-dimensional when
{p}=2
and
48
-dimensional when
{p}=3
.)
Recall that the fields
\partial_{u}^{p+1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]}
,
i\geq 2
, have not been determined so far. We can use this freedom to arrange that
\partial_{u}^{{p}+1}{{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]}}-\overset{({p})}{\hat{\tilde{X}}}_{A}
is in the image of
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!
.
For all
p\leq k
we can therefore find a unique
{\overset{[{p}+4]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}^{[{({\ker{\overset{(p)}{{\chi}}}{}})^{\perp}}]}_{AB}
and
\partial_{u}^{{p}+1}{{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]}}

satisfying

{\overset{(p)}{{\chi}}}{}({\overset{[{p}+4]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}^{[{({\ker{\overset{(p)}{{\chi}}}{}})^{\perp}}]})=\partial_{u}^{{p}+1}{{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]}}-\overset{({p})}{\hat{\tilde{X}}}{}^{[{\text{im}{\overset{(p)}{{\chi}}}{}}]}\,.

(227)

For
p\leq k-1
the
{({\text{im}{\overset{(p)}{{\chi}}}{}})^{\perp}}
-part of
\overset{({p})}{\hat{\tilde{X}}}_{A}
constitutes thus a further obstruction to the solvability of (
4.3.1
), as it is not clear whether or not the right-hand side is orthogonal to the cokernel.

However, if
p=k
or
k+1
, since the fields
\partial_{u}^{k}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}_{A}}
and
\partial_{u}^{k+1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}_{A}}
are unconstrained so far, we can use (
4.3.1
) to choose these fields so that continuity of
\partial_{u}^{k-1}\tilde{h}_{uA}
and
\partial_{u}^{k}\tilde{h}_{uA}
at
r_{2}
holds:

{({\text{im}{\overset{(p)}{{\chi}}}{}})^{\perp}}\ni\partial_{u}^{{p}+1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}_{A}:=\overset{({p})}{\hat{\tilde{X}}}{}_{A}^{[{({\text{im}{\overset{(p)}{{\chi}}}{}})^{\perp}}]}\,.

(228)

The case
m\neq 0
:

After performing a gauge transformation, Equation (
152
) at order
i={p}
together with (
41
) provides a gluing equation of the form,

\displaystyle\partial_{u}^{{p}}{\check{h}}_{uA}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2r_{2}^{2}}{L_{1}}({\mathring{D}}^{C}\partial_{u}^{{p}-1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{C})_{A}-\partial_{u}^{{p}+1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}

(229)

\displaystyle\quad-\big(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{2}+\frac{m}{r_{2}^{3}}\big){\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}^{C}\partial_{u}^{{p}-1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{C}+\overset{({p})}{X}_{A}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{0\leq j+\ell\leq{p},\ell\neq{p}}\overset{({p},j,\ell)}{\chi}(r_{2})\partial_{r}^{j}{\mathring{D}}^{B}P^{\ell}{h}_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{0\leq j+\ell\leq{p},\ell\neq{p}}\overset{({p},j,\ell)}{\chi}(r_{2})\partial_{r}^{j}{\mathring{D}}^{B}P^{\ell}\Big(2r_{2}^{2}C({\overset{(2)}{{\zeta}}})_{AB}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j=0}^{{p}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\overset{({p},j)}{\chi}(s,r_{2})P^{j}\tilde{h}_{AB}(s)\,ds

\displaystyle=-\partial_{u}^{{p}+1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}+\overset{({p})}{\tilde{X}}_{A}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j=0}^{{p}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{{2p}+4}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\overset{({p},j)}{\chi}_{\ell}(r_{2})\hat{\kappa}_{\ell}(s)P^{j}{w}_{AB}(s)\,ds

\displaystyle=-\partial_{u}^{{p}+1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}+\overset{({p})}{\tilde{X}}_{A}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{{2p}+4}\underbrace{\sum_{j=0}^{{p}}\overset{({p},j)}{\chi}_{\ell}(r_{2}){\mathring{D}}^{B}P^{j}}_{=:L_{p,\ell}}{\overset{[\ell]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}\,,

where we have collected the fields
\partial_{u}^{j}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}
for
j\leq p
, together with all other already known fields, into
\overset{({p})}{\tilde{X}}_{A}
.

For
k\geq 1
we let

\Phi_{k}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}{\overset{[5]}{\hat{\varphi}}}\\
\vdots\\
{\overset{[4+k]}{\hat{\varphi}}}\\
\end{array}\right)\,,\qquad\Psi_{k}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}{\overset{[5+k]}{\hat{\varphi}}}\\
\vdots\\
{\overset{[4+2k]}{\hat{\varphi}}}\\
\end{array}\right)\,,

and

X_{k}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{uA}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}+\partial_{u}^{2}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}-\overset{({1})}{\tilde{X}}_{A}\\
\vdots\\
\partial_{u}^{{k}}{\check{h}}_{uA}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}+\partial_{u}^{{k}+1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}-\overset{({k})}{\tilde{X}}_{A}\\
\end{array}\right)\,.

The system of equations (
229
) with
1\leq p\leq k

takes the form

{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\circ L_{k}\Phi_{k}+{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\circ M_{k}\Psi_{k}=X_{k}\,,

(230)

where

L_{k}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}L_{1,5}&\ldots&L_{1,k+4}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
L_{k,5}&\ldots&L_{k,k+4}\\
\end{array}\right)\,,\quad M_{k}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}L_{1,k+5}&\ldots&L_{1,2k+4}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
L_{k,k+5}&\ldots&L_{k,2k+4}\\
\end{array}\right)\,.

(231)

Recall that the fields
\partial_{u}^{i}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]}
,
i\geq 2
, have not been determined so far. We can use this freedom to arrange that
\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{uA}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}+\partial_{u}^{2}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}-\overset{({1})}{\tilde{X}}_{A}
is in the image of
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!
. Inserting the result into
\overset{({2})}{\tilde{X}}_{A}
we can arrange

\partial^{2}_{u}{\check{h}}_{uA}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}+\partial_{u}^{3}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}-\overset{({2})}{\tilde{X}}_{A}

also lies in the image of
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!
. Continuing in this way we obtain that all the entries in
X_{k}
are in the image of
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!
.

We continue by noting that the operator
L_{k}
is elliptic in the sense of Agmon, Douglis and Nirenberg (cf., e.g.,
[
18
]
, in particular see the estimates of Theorem C there).
Indeed, in each row of
L_{k}
the operators
L_{i,j}
are of order less than
2i
, except for the operator
L_{i,i}
lying on the diagonal, which is elliptic and precisely of order
2i
. This shows that the Agmon-Douglis-Nirenberg condition on the orders
s_{i}+t_{j}
of
L_{i,j}
holds by setting
s_{i}=2i
and
t_{j}=0
;
ellipticity readily follows.

It is instructive to consider explicitly the case
k=2
:

\displaystyle L_{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{1}{4}(P+2{{{{\varepsilon}}}})&-\frac{3m}{2}\\
\frac{15m\alpha^{2}}{8}-\frac{3{m}}{8r_{2}^{2}}(P+2{{{{\varepsilon}}}})&\frac{9{m}^{2}}{4r_{2}^{2}}+\frac{1}{10}(P+2{{{{\varepsilon}}}})(P+5{{{{\varepsilon}}}})\\
\end{array}\right)\,,

\displaystyle M_{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&0\\
-\frac{7m}{4}(P+\frac{11{{\varepsilon}}}{3})&\frac{75{m^{2}}}{8}\\
\end{array}\right)\,,

so that (
230
) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cc }L_{1,5}(P)&-a\\
L_{2,5}(P)&L_{2,6}(P)\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}{\overset{[5]}{\hat{\varphi}}}\\
{\overset{[6]}{\hat{\varphi}}}\\
\end{array}\right)+\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&0\\
L_{2,7}(P)&c\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}{\overset{[7]}{\hat{\varphi}}}\\
{\overset{[8]}{\hat{\varphi}}}\\
\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=({\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!)^{-1}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}x_{1}\\
x_{2}\\
\end{array}\right)\,,

with
ac\neq 0
,
where the
L_{i,j}(P)
’s are polynomials in
P
,
and where
({\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!)^{-1}
is the inverse of
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!
viewed as a map from
(\ker{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!)^{\perp}
to

\text{im}\,{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}=(\ker C)^{\perp}
.
It should be clear from this equation that for generic
m
the operator
L_{2}
is invertible from
(\ker{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!)^{\perp}
to
(\ker C)^{\perp}
,
so that for such
m
’s we can solve the problem by setting

{\overset{[7]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}={\overset{[8]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}\equiv 0

and solving (
4.3.1
) for

{\overset{[5]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}
and
{\overset{[6]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}
.
However, this genericity condition is not needed; indeed, for any
m\neq 0
we can proceed as follows: From the first equation of
(
4.3.1
) we find

{\overset{[6]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}=a^{-1}\big(L_{1,5}(P){\overset{[5]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}-({\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!)^{-1}x_{1}\big)\,.

(246)

Inserting into the second equation we obtain

\underbrace{L_{2,6}(P)L_{1,5}(P)}_{=:\mathfrak{L}_{2}}{\overset{[5]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}+aL_{1,2}(P){\overset{[5]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}=a\big(({\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!)^{-1}x_{2}-L_{2,7}(P){\overset{[7]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}-c{\overset{[8]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}\big)\,.

(247)

We can choose smooth fields
{\overset{[7]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}
and
{\overset{[8]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}
so that

L_{2,7}(P){\overset{[7]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}+c{\overset{[8]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}=\big(({\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!)^{-1}x_{2}\big)^{[\text{im}(\mathfrak{L}_{2})^{\perp}]}\in(\text{im}\mathfrak{L}_{2})^{\perp}

(e.g., by setting
{\overset{[7]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}=0
, but other choices might be more convenient); note that
(\text{im}\mathfrak{L}_{2})^{\perp}
is finite dimensional and is spanned by smooth functions by ellipticity of
\mathfrak{L}_{2}^{\dagger}
.
We then solve the elliptic equation

\mathfrak{L}_{2}{\overset{[5]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}+aL_{1,2}(P){\overset{[5]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}=a\big(({\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!)^{-1}x_{2}\big)^{[\text{im}\mathfrak{L}_{2}]}\,,

(248)

with a unique
{\overset{[5]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}\in(\ker\mathfrak{L}_{2})^{\perp}
. Inserting into (
246
) we have thus obtained a desired solution of

L_{k}\Phi_{k}+M_{k}\Psi_{k}=({\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!)^{-1}X_{k}\,,

(249)

with
k=2
.

Let us pass now to general
k
’s.
We note first that ellipticity and self-adjointness of
P
implies existence of a complete set of smooth,
pairwise
L^{2}
-orthogonal, eigenfunctions
\phi_{\ell}
with a corresponding discrete set of eigenvalues
\lambda_{\ell}\to_{\ell\to\infty}\infty
.
We can therefore write

\Phi_{k}=\sum_{\ell}\Phi_{k,\ell}\phi_{\ell}\,,\quad\Psi_{k}=\sum_{\ell}\Psi_{k,\ell}\phi_{\ell}\,,\quad({\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!)^{-1}X_{k}=\sum_{\ell}X_{k,\ell}\phi_{\ell}\,.

(250)

Equation (
249
) can be solved mode-by-mode:

L_{k}\Phi_{k,\ell}+M_{k}\Psi_{k,\ell}=X_{k,\ell}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad L_{k}|_{P\mapsto\lambda_{\ell}}\Phi_{k,\ell}+M_{k}|_{P\mapsto\lambda_{\ell}}\Psi_{k,\ell}=X_{k,\ell}\,,

(251)

where
P\mapsto\lambda_{\ell}
means that every occurrence of
P
should be replaced by
\lambda_{\ell}
.

Now,
\det L_{k}|_{P\mapsto\lambda_{\ell}}
is a polynomial in
\lambda_{\ell}
.
Keeping in mind that in each line of the matrix
L_{k}
the highest power of
P
is on the diagonal, we see that
\det L_{k}|_{P\mapsto\lambda_{\ell}}
is non-zero for
\ell
large enough, and therefore there exists
N(k)
such that we can find a unique solution of (
251
) with
\Psi_{k,\ell}=0
for all
\ell>N(k)
. It remains to show that (
251
) can be solved in the finite dimensional space of
\Phi_{k}
’s and
\Psi_{k}
’s of the form

\Phi_{k}=\sum_{\ell\leq N(k)}\Phi_{k,\ell}\phi_{\ell}\,,\quad\Psi_{k}=\sum_{\ell\leq N(k)}\Psi_{k,\ell}\phi_{\ell}\,,\quad({\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!)^{-1}X_{k}=\sum_{\ell\leq N(k)}X_{k,\ell}\phi_{\ell}\,.

(252)

This is equivalent to the requirement that all the linear maps obtained by juxtaposing
L_{k}|_{P\mapsto\lambda_{\ell}}
and
M_{k}|_{P\mapsto\lambda_{\ell}}
with
\ell<N(k)
are surjective. (Note, by the way, that we have already established surjectivity for
\ell\geq N(k)
.) This, in turn, is equivalent to the fact that the adjoints of these linear maps have no kernel.

Let us denote by
(L_{k}\,M_{k})
the relevant matrices. For simplicity write
L_{i,j}
for
L_{i,j}(P)|_{P\mapsto\lambda_{\ell}}
.
It follows from Appendix
B.2

that
(L_{k}\,M_{k})
is of the form

\displaystyle\small\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\begin{array}[]{|cc|}\hline\cr L_{1,4+1}&L_{1,4+2}\\
L_{2,4+1}&L_{2,4+2}\\
\vdots&\vdots\\
\vdots&\vdots\\
\vdots&\vdots\\
L_{k,4+1}&L_{k,4+2}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}&\hskip-5.69046pt\ldots&\begin{array}[]{|cc|}\hline\cr 0&0\\
\vdots&\vdots\\
0&0\\
L_{k-j,3+2(k-j)}&L_{k-j,4+2(k-j)}\\
\vdots&\vdots\\
L_{k,3+2(k-j)}&L_{k,4+2(k-j)}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}&\hskip-5.69046pt\ldots&\begin{array}[]{|cc|}\hline\cr 0&0\\
\vdots&\vdots\\
\cdot&\cdot\\
\vdots&\vdots\\
0&0\\
L_{k,3+2k}&L_{k,4+2k}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\end{array}\right)\,,

where the
(i,j)
-th entry is
L_{i,4+j}
. Note that each of the two-columns pairs, as grouped above, has a specific vanishing-block structure. This gives the following adjoint matrix:

\displaystyle\small\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\begin{array}[]{|cccccc|}\hline\cr L_{1,4+1}&L_{2,4+1}&\dots\dots&\ldots\dots&\dots\hskip 9.24994pt\dots\hskip 9.24994pt\dots\dots&\,L_{k,4+1}\\
\framebox{ $L_{1,4+2}$ }&L_{2,4+2}&\dots\dots&\ldots\dots&\dots\hskip 9.24994pt\dots\hskip 9.24994pt\dots\dots&\,L_{k,4+2}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\\
\begin{array}[]{cccccc}&&&\vdots&&\end{array}\\
\begin{array}[]{|cccccc|}\hline\cr\phantom{L_{k,k}}\,0&\phantom{L_{k,k}}\hskip 9.24994pt0\dots&0&L_{k-j,4+2(k-j)-1}&\dots\hskip 9.24994pt\dots\dots&L_{k,4+2(k-j)-1}\\
\phantom{L_{k,k}}\,0&\phantom{L_{k,k}}\hskip 9.24994pt0\dots&0&\framebox{$L_{k-j,4+2(k-j)}$}&\dots\hskip 9.24994pt\dots\dots&L_{k,4+2(k-j)}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\\
\begin{array}[]{cccccc}&&&\vdots&&\end{array}\\
\begin{array}[]{|cccccc|}\hline\cr\phantom{L_{k,k}}\,0&\phantom{L_{k,k}}\hskip 9.24994pt0\dots&\dots\dots&\ldots\hskip 9.24994pt\dots 0&\,L_{k-1,4+2k-3}&L_{k,4+2k-3}\\
\phantom{L_{k,k}}\,0&\phantom{L_{k,k}}\hskip 9.24994pt0\dots&\dots\dots&\ldots\hskip 9.24994pt\dots 0&\,\framebox{$L_{k-1,4+2k}$}&L_{k,4+2k-2}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\\
\\
\begin{array}[]{|cccccc|}\hline\cr\phantom{L_{k,k}}\,0&\phantom{L_{k,k}}\hskip 9.24994pt0\dots&\dots\dots&\ldots\hskip 9.24994pt\dots 0&0&\phantom{L_{k,k}\hskip 9.24994pt}L_{k,4+2k-1}\\
\phantom{L_{k,k}}\,0&\phantom{L_{k,k}}\hskip 9.24994pt0\dots&\dots\dots&\ldots\hskip 9.24994pt\dots 0&0&\phantom{L_{k,k}\hskip 9.24994pt}\framebox{$L_{k,4+2k}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\end{array}\right)\,.

Equation (
344
) shows
that the entries (boxed in the above)
L_{i,4+2i}
are non-zero when
m\neq 0
.
This easily implies that that the matrix (
4.3.1
) has maximal rank, and thus has trivial kernel.

We have therefore proved:

Theorem
4.3

The system (
229
) with
p\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
can be solved by a choice of gluing fields and gauge fields for any finite
k
. Its solutions are determined by an elliptic system, uniquely up to a finite number of eigenfunctions of
P
.

4.3.2
Continuity of
\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{AB}
,
p\geq 2

The case
m=0
:

It follows from the pointwise radial conservation law of
{\overset{[3,p]}{Q}}{}
(cf. (
116
)) that the continuity of
\partial^{p}_{u}\tilde{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}
at
r_{2}
requires

{\overset{[3,p]}{Q}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}]={\overset{[3,p]}{Q}}{}[\mathfrak{d}_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}]\,.

(282)

The gauge field
\partial_{u}^{p-1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}^{[({{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}})^{\perp}]}
is used to achieve the matching of

({\overset{[3,p]}{Q}}{})^{[({{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}})^{\perp}]}
according to (
200
).

On
S^{2}
, this ensures the continuity of
\partial^{p}_{u}\tilde{h}_{AB}
at
r_{2}
since then
\partial^{p}_{u}\tilde{h}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}_{AB}=\partial^{p}_{u}{\tilde{h}}_{AB}
.

For the remaining topologies we return to (
163
).
Taking into account the gauge invariance of
{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}
, Equation (
163
)
provides a necessary and sufficient condition for the continuity of
\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}
at
r_{2}
according to:

\displaystyle\partial^{p}_{u}q_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}-\partial^{p}_{u}q_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}

(283)

\displaystyle=\alpha^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}(\alpha^{2}r_{2})^{k}\bigg[s\ {\overset{[p-k]}{q}}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{r_{1}}+\frac{1}{2s}({{{{\varepsilon}}}}-\alpha^{2}s^{2})\partial_{u}^{p-1-k}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{s}\bigg]_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\alpha^{2(p+1)}r_{2}^{p}\bigg[\,{\overset{[1]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}+\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\hat{\kappa}_{1}(s){v}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\,ds\,\bigg]

\displaystyle=\alpha^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}(\alpha^{2}r_{2})^{k}\bigg[s\ {\overset{[p-k]}{q}}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{r_{1}}+\frac{1}{2s}({{{{\varepsilon}}}}-\alpha^{2}s^{2})\partial_{u}^{p-1-k}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{s}\bigg]_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+(\alpha^{2}r_{2})^{p}(q_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}-q_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{1}})\,,

where we used the formula (
217
) for
{\overset{[1]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}
in the last step. Equation (
283
) provides a further obstruction to be satisfied by the data. When
\alpha=0
, the condition reduces to

\forall\ 0\leq p\leq k{-1}\qquad\overset{[p+1]}{q}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}]=\overset{[p+1]}{q}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}[\mathfrak{d}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}]\,.

(284)

The case
m\neq 0
:

The continuity at
r_{2}
of the
{{\text{TT}^{\perp}}}
-part of
\partial^{p}_{u}h_{AB}
has already been addressed in Section
4.2.2
.
Taking into account the allowed gauge perturbations of the linearised gravitational field, it follows from (
151
) that
it remains to satisfy the equation

\displaystyle\partial^{p}_{u}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}|_{\tilde{{\mathbf{S}}}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\Big(\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\Psi}}_{AB}(r_{2},x^{A})-2r_{2}^{2}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}\partial_{u}^{p}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}_{B}}]\Big)^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}

(285)

\displaystyle\quad+\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{2}\sum_{\ell=2p+1}^{2p+2}\overset{({p},j)}{\psi}_{\ell}(r_{2})P^{j}{\overset{[\ell]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}\Big)^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\,.

Here, for the sake of induction, we treated the fields
\partial_{u}^{j}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}
for
0\leq j\leq p-1
and
{\overset{[\ell]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}
for
1\leq\ell\leq 2p
as known, and collected them together with the remaining known fields into the term
\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\Psi}}_{AB}(r_{2},x^{A})
.
This equation is non-trivial only for
\mathbb{T}^{2}
and for cross-sections
{\mathbf{S}}
of higher genus,
can be solved using a linear combination of
{\overset{[2p+2]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}^{{[{{\text{TT}}}]}}
and
{\overset{[2p+1]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}^{{[{{\text{TT}}}]}}
:

\displaystyle\partial^{p}_{u}{h}^{{[{{\text{TT}}}]}}_{AB}|_{\tilde{{\mathbf{S}}}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\Psi}}{}^{{[{{\text{TT}}}]}}_{AB}(r_{2},x^{A})+\overset{({p},0)}{\psi}_{2p+2}(r_{2}){\overset{[2p+2]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}^{{[{{\text{TT}}}]}}_{AB}+\overset{({p},0)}{\psi}_{2p+1}(r_{2}){\overset{[2p+1]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}^{{[{{\text{TT}}}]}}_{AB}\,.

(286)

5
An isomorphism theorem

In Section
3.6
we have verified that a consistent scheme for the linearised equations is obtained

\framebox{ if we assume that $h_{AB}(r,\cdot)\in{H_{k_{\gamma}}({\mathbf{S}})}$, for all $r\in[r_{1},r_{2}]$, with $k_{\gamma}\geq 4$}

and

\displaystyle k_{\beta}

\displaystyle=k_{\gamma}\,,\quad k_{U}=k_{\gamma}-1\,,\quad k_{V}=k_{\gamma}-2\,,\quad k_{\partial_{u}U}=k_{\gamma}-3\,,

(287)

\displaystyle k_{\partial_{u}V}

\displaystyle=k_{\gamma}-4\,,\quad k_{\partial_{u}\gamma}=k_{\gamma}-2\,.

as well as

k_{\xi^{u}}=k_{\gamma}+2\,,\quad k_{\partial_{u}\xi^{u}}=k_{\gamma}\,,\quad k_{\xi^{A}}=k_{\gamma}+1\,,\quad k_{\partial_{u}\xi^{A}}=k_{\gamma}-1\,.

(288)

We wish to check that this is consistent with the equations satisfied by the gluing functions, and that the gluing equations provide surjections in the relevant spaces, with splitting kernels, so that the implicit function theorem can be applied to the full nonlinear problem.

More precisely,
we assume that the gluing fields take the form as in (
165
) and (
168
). Assuming that the fields at
r_{1}
and
r_{2}
satisfy (
287
), and that all
r
-derivatives of the interpolating field
{v}_{AB}
are in
H_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{k_{\gamma}}}\equiv H_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{k_{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{S}})
, we want to show that the gluing fields
{\overset{[i]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}_{AB}
will be in
H_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{k_{\gamma}}}
, and that the gauge functions will satisfy (
288
).

Note that within the scheme presented above, the
r
-derivatives of all fields preserve their Sobolev class.

Whether for the sake of induction when
m=0
, or for the analysis of the elliptic system (
230
) when
m\neq 0
,
we assume that each
u
-derivative of all fields obtained in a previous induction step has a loss of no more than two degrees of differentiability:

k_{\partial_{u}^{i}U}=k_{\gamma}-1-2i\,,\quad k_{\partial^{i}_{u}V}=k_{\gamma}-2i\,,\quad k_{\partial^{i}_{u}\gamma}=k_{\gamma}-2i\,,

(289)

while for the gauge fields it holds that

k_{\partial^{i}_{u}\xi^{u}}=k_{\gamma}+2-2i\,,\quad k_{\partial^{i}_{u}\xi^{A}}=k_{\gamma}+1-2i\,.

(290)

5.1

\partial_{u}^{i}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}\in H_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{k_{\gamma}}}-2i+1}\,,\quad i\geq 0\,,

When

,
the solution of the set of equations (

, is again of the desired differentiability class when the condition (

As the next step, consider the field
{\overset{[1]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}\equiv\langle\hat{\kappa}_{1},{w}_{AB}\rangle
solving (

), cf. also (

).
Here a note on the differentiability class of the boundary term appearing in (
211
),
or in the equations that will be referred to in what follows, is in order. We have already checked that the differentiability classes in (
289
)-(
290
) are consistent with all the equations. The boundary terms in all integrated equations are obtained by integration by parts in the
r
-variable,
which does not change the differentiability in the
{\mathbf{S}}
-directions.
The boundary terms for the gauge equations only involve the known boundary data, which have the needed regularity by hypothesis.
The boundary terms for the gluing fields are either determined by the known boundary data, or from the gauge fields.

When
m=0
, the hierarchical structure of our proof implies that the boundary terms arising at each further step of our analysis will have the right differentiability properties for the induction argument.
When
m\neq 0
the solutions are obtained by a global system of equations, with boundary terms determined by the known boundary data.

In any case,
one can directly chase through the derivatives of the already known fields which appear in (

)-(
211
),
and use that on two-dimensional manifolds the operator
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!
is elliptic, and that the field
{\overset{[1]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}

solving (

)
is smooth, to obtain that

{\overset{[1]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}_{AB}\in H_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{k_{\gamma}}}}\,.

as needed.

When
m=0
,

), where

. By the arguments just given, or by a direct inductive calculation, the boundary terms
\overset{({p})}{\hat{\tilde{X}}}_{A}
are in

.
For

, where
k
is the number of derivatives that we wish to glue, the operator recovers the number of derivatives lost by the right-hand side, which results in a field

{\overset{[{p}+4]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}_{AB}\in H_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{k_{\gamma}}}}\,.

(292)

The case
m\neq 0
is taken care of by Theorem
4.3
.

Keeping in mind that all the operators considered in the analysis of the linearised equations have splitting kernels, we have proved:

Theorem
5.1

The linearised gluing map, assigning to the collection

\{\mbox{data at $r_{1}$, the gluing fields, and the gauge fields}\}

the data at
r_{2}
, as described above, is continuous and surjective in the Sobolev spaces defined in (
289
)-(
290
), with splitting kernel, modulo the obstructions listed in Tables
4.1
, p.
4.1
and
4.2
, p.
4.2
.

6
Unobstructed gluing to perturbed data

Given that there exist obstructions to glue two arbitrary characteristic data sets of order
k
, the question arises whether something can be done about that. Since we are dealing with linear equations, the simplest solution is to add to the data another data set with charges chosen to compensate for the obstructions. This requires families of data sets with a sufficient number of radial charges to cover all obstructions.

Now, a static family of such data sets can be obtained by differentiating the Birmingham-Kottler metrics with respect to mass:

\frac{d}{dm}\Big[\big({{\varepsilon}}-{\alpha^{2}}r^{2}{-\frac{2m}{r}}\big)du^{2}-2du\,dr+r^{2}{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}_{AB}dx^{A}dx^{B}\Big]=-\frac{2}{r}du^{2}\,.

(293)

These metric perturbations can be used to compensate for the missing charge
{\overset{[2]}{Q}}{}(\lambda)
with
\lambda=1
.

Another such family is obtained by differentiating (
1
) with respect to a parameter along a curve of metrics

\lambda\mapsto{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}_{AB}(\lambda)
with constant scalar curvature:

\frac{d}{d\lambda}\Big[\big({{\varepsilon}}-{\alpha^{2}}r^{2}{-\frac{2m}{r}}\big)du^{2}-2du\,dr+r^{2}{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}_{AB}dx^{A}dx^{B}\Big]=r^{2}\frac{d{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}_{AB}}{d\lambda}dx^{A}dx^{B}\,.

(294)

By
[
19
, Theorem 8.15]

every
TT
-tensor, say
{{\mathring{m}}}_{AB}
, is tangent to such a curve, and thus metric perturbations of the form

r^{2}{{\mathring{m}}}_{AB}dx^{A}dx^{B}\,,\ \mbox{with}\ {\mathring{D}}_{A}{{\mathring{m}}}^{AB}=0={{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}_{AB}{{\mathring{m}}}^{AB}

(295)

provide the missing radial charges
{\overset{[i]}{q}}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}
.

Yet another, time-independent, family is provided by differentiating the Kerr-(Anti) de Sitter metrics with respect to the angular-momentum. (Since there is no explicit formula for these metrics in Bondi coordinates, the associated linearised metrics can only be obtained by an indirect calculation.)
When calculated at the (A)dS solution (with
m\neq 0
), this leads to the metric perturbation (
296
) below, where
{\mathring{\lambda}}^{A}\partial_{A}
is a
u
-independent Killing vector on
S^{2}
, with the remaining fields there vanishing.
2
2

2

We are grateful to Finn Gray for checking this.

It turns out that we can obtain a family of metric perturbations compensating for all radial charges needed for
{C^{2}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}}
-gluing by setting

\displaystyle\mathring{h}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{{\mathring{\mu}}(u,x^{C})}{r}-\frac{{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{\lambda}}_{A}(u,x^{C})}{2r^{2}}+\frac{1}{2r}{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathring{s}_{AB}(u,x^{C})\Big)du^{2}

(296)

\displaystyle+\Big(\frac{{\mathring{\lambda}}_{A}(u,x^{C})}{r}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathring{s}_{AB}(u,x^{C})\Big)dx^{A}du

\displaystyle+(r\mathring{s}_{AB}(u,x^{C})+r^{2}{{\mathring{m}}}_{AB}(u,x^{C}))dx^{A}dx^{B}\,,

with symmetric
{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}
-traceless tensors
\mathring{s}_{AB}
and
{{\mathring{m}}}_{AB}
. In addition, anticipating the fact that
{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathring{s}_{AB}
plays a role in adjusting
({\overset{[3,i]}{Q}}{})^{[{{\text{H}}}]}
,
we impose

{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathring{s}_{AB}(u,x^{A})=0\,.

(297)

After using

{\mathring{D}}_{A}\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}\psi^{A}=(\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}+{{{{\varepsilon}}}}){\mathring{D}}_{A}\psi^{A}\,,

(298)

the linearised Einstein equations will hold if and only if

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{{\mathring{m}}}_{AB}=0\,,\quad{\alpha^{2}\mathring{s}_{AB}-\partial_{u}{{\mathring{m}}}_{AB}=0}\,,\quad 3m{\mathring{D}}^{A}\mathring{\lambda}_{A}=0\,,

(299)

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\big[{\mathring{D}}_{A}\mathring{\lambda}_{B}\big]+m\mathring{s}_{AB}=0\,,

(300)

\displaystyle 3\alpha^{2}{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{\lambda}}_{A}+2\partial_{u}{\mathring{\mu}}=0\,,\quad 3\partial_{u}{\mathring{\lambda}}_{A}-{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{\mu}}+\frac{1}{2}(\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}-{{{{\varepsilon}}}}){\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathring{s}_{AB}=0\,,

(301)

\displaystyle 2{{{{\varepsilon}}}}\mathring{\lambda}_{A}+{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathring{\lambda}_{B}-{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\mathring{\lambda}_{B}+\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}\mathring{\lambda}_{A}+2m{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathring{s}_{AB}=0\,,

(302)

\displaystyle{{{{\varepsilon}}}}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\mathring{\lambda}_{A}+\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\mathring{\lambda}_{A}=0\,.

(303)

For completeness we listed above all conditions obtained from the linearised Einstein equations, cf. Sections
3.3
and
3.5
, but we note that (
299
)-(
301
) suffice.
Indeed, taking
2\times{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!
of (

) gives equation (
302
), while equation (
303
) can be obtained by taking
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(1)}}\,}\!
of (
302
) and by making use of (
299
).

Equation (
299
) implies that
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(1)}}\,}\mathring{\lambda}
has to vanish when
m\neq 0
, and
\partial_{u}{{\mathring{m}}}_{AB}
has to vanish when
\alpha=0
or when we are on
S^{2}
. In addition, it follows from (
300
) that
\mathring{\lambda}_{A}
has to vanish when
m=0
and
{{{{\varepsilon}}}}=-1
.

Equations (
301
) together with their
u
-differentiated versions show that

\displaystyle 2\partial_{u}^{2}\mathring{\mu}

\displaystyle=-3\alpha^{2}\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}\mathring{\mu}\,,

(304)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}^{2}\mathring{\lambda}_{A}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\alpha^{2}}{2}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathring{\lambda}_{B}-\frac{1}{6}(\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}-{{{{\varepsilon}}}}){\mathring{D}}^{B}\partial_{u}\mathring{s}_{AB}\,.

(305)

When
m\neq 0
we can use (
300
) to rewrite the last equation as

\displaystyle\partial_{u}^{2}\mathring{\lambda}_{A}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\alpha^{2}}{2}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathring{\lambda}_{B}+\frac{1}{6m}(\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}-{{{{\varepsilon}}}}){\mathring{D}}^{B}\partial_{u}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\big[{\mathring{D}}_{A}\mathring{\lambda}_{B}\big]\,.

(306)

So, when

, Equations (

) and (

) provide evolution equations for
\mathring{\mu}
and
\mathring{\lambda}_{A}
, solutions of which determine the time-evolution of the remaining fields.

To continue, we note
that equation (
302
) can be rewritten as,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}+{{{{\varepsilon}}}})\mathring{\lambda}_{A}+m{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathring{s}_{AB}=0\,.

(307)

Next, the second equation in (

), together with (

), implies that

\displaystyle(\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}+{{{{\varepsilon}}}}){\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{\mu}}

\displaystyle=3(\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}+{{{{\varepsilon}}}})\partial_{u}{\mathring{\lambda}}_{A}+\frac{1}{2}(\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}+{{{{\varepsilon}}}})(\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}-{{{{\varepsilon}}}}){\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathring{s}_{AB}

\displaystyle=-6m(\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}+{{{{\varepsilon}}}}){\mathring{D}}^{B}\partial_{u}\mathring{s}_{AB}+\frac{1}{2}(\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}+{{{{\varepsilon}}}})(\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}-{{{{\varepsilon}}}}){\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathring{s}_{AB}\,.

Taking
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(1)}}\,}\!
of this and making use of
{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathring{s}_{AB}=0=\partial_{u}{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathring{s}_{AB}
gives

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{A}(\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}+{{{{\varepsilon}}}}){\mathring{D}}_{A}\mathring{\mu}

\displaystyle=\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}(\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}+2{{{{\varepsilon}}}})\mathring{\mu}=0\,.

In particular, when we are not on
S^{2}
,
the “mass aspect function”
\mathring{\mu}
must be
x^{A}
-independent, while
on
S^{2}
it is a linear combination of
\ell=0
and
\ell=1
spherical harmonic. It follows that

\partial_{u}^{2}{\mathring{\mu}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}3\alpha^{2}\mathring{\mu},&\hbox{${\mathbf{S}}=S^{2}$ and $\mathring{\mu}$ has no $\ell=0$ harmonics;}\\
0,&\hbox{${\mathbf{S}}\neq S^{2}$, or ${\mathbf{S}}=S^{2}$ and $\mathring{\mu}$ has no $\ell=1$ harmonics.}\end{array}\right.

Next, when
m=0
the space of
\mathring{\lambda}
’s satisfying
(
300
) is six-dimensional on
S^{2}
and two-dimensional on
\mathbb{T}^{2}
; for negatively curved
{\mathbf{S}}
one finds
\mathring{\lambda}_{A}\equiv 0
.

The tensor field
{\mathring{h}}_{AB}
carries the full set of conserved radial charges needed for
{C^{2}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}}
-gluing
when
{\mathring{\mu}}
,
{\mathring{\lambda}}_{A}
,
\mathring{s}_{AB}
and
{{\mathring{m}}}_{AB}

run over the set of solutions of (
299
)-(
301
):

\displaystyle{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}(\pi)=-3\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\pi^{A}\mathring{\lambda}_{A}\,d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}\,,\qquad{\overset{[2]}{Q}}{}(\lambda)=-\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\lambda\mathring{\mu}\,d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}\,,

(308)

\displaystyle\ q_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}=-\frac{V}{2r}{{\mathring{m}}}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}+\alpha^{2}r\mathring{s}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}+\partial_{u}\mathring{s}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\,,

(309)

\displaystyle\ {\overset{[2]}{q}}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}=-\frac{\alpha^{2}V}{2r}\mathring{s}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}+\alpha^{2}r\partial_{u}\mathring{s}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}+\partial_{u}^{2}\mathring{s}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\,,

(310)

\displaystyle({\overset{[3,1]}{Q}}{})^{[{{\text{H}}}]}_{A}=-{3}{\alpha^{2}}\mathring{\lambda}^{[{{\text{H}}}]}_{A}+\frac{2m}{r}({\mathring{D}}^{B}{{\mathring{m}}}_{AB})^{[{{\text{H}}}]}-\frac{m}{r^{2}}({\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathring{s}_{AB})^{[{{\text{H}}}]}

(311)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+2({\mathring{D}}^{B}\partial_{u}\mathring{s}_{AB})^{[{{\text{H}}}]}\,,

\displaystyle({\overset{[3,2]}{Q}}{})^{[{{\text{H}}}]}_{A}=-{3}{\alpha^{2}}\partial_{u}\mathring{\lambda}^{[{{\text{H}}}]}_{A}+\frac{2m\alpha^{2}}{r}({\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathring{s}_{AB})^{[{{\text{H}}}]}-\frac{m}{r^{2}}({\mathring{D}}^{B}\partial_{u}\mathring{s}_{AB})^{[{{\text{H}}}]}

(312)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+2({\mathring{D}}^{B}\partial^{2}_{u}\mathring{s}_{AB})^{[{{\text{H}}}]}\,,

where
\pi
and
\lambda
satisfy, respectively,
(
57
) and (
74
).
Note that when
\alpha=0=m
, we have
V/r={{{{\varepsilon}}}}
, in which case all expressions in (
308
)-(
312
) are
r
-independent, as they should be in this case.

Keeping in mind that
{C^{2}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}}
-gluing with
m\neq 0
needs only the matching of
{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}
and
{\overset{[2]}{Q}}{}
, we have proved:

Theorem
6.1

Any
{C^{2}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}}
linearised vacuum data on
{\mycal N}_{(r_{0},r_{1}]}
can be
{C^{2}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}}
-glued to any
{C^{2}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}}
linearised vacuum data on
{\mycal N}_{[r_{2},r_{3})}
after adding to one of them a suitable field of the form (
296
).

\proof

Indeed, when
m\neq 0

we only need (cf. Table
1.1
, p.
1.1
)

\displaystyle\mathring{h}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathring{\mu}}}{r}du^{2}+\frac{{\mathring{\lambda}}_{A}(x^{C})}{r}dx^{A}du\,,

(313)

with
{\mathring{\mu}}
being a constant and
{\mathring{\lambda}}_{A}
being a combination of
\ell=1
vector harmonics satisfying
{\mathring{D}}^{A}\mathring{\lambda}_{A}=0
on
S^{2}
; with constant
{\mathring{\mu}}
and covariantly constant
{\mathring{\lambda}}_{A}
on
\mathbb{T}^{2}
; with constant
{\mathring{\mu}}
and vanishing
{\mathring{\lambda}}_{A}
on higher genus manifolds. In all cases the fields are chosen so that the radial charges

\displaystyle{\overset{[1]}{Q}}{}(\pi)=-3\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\pi^{A}\mathring{\lambda}_{A}\,d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}\,,\qquad{\overset{[2]}{Q}}{}(\lambda)=-\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\lambda\mathring{\mu}\,d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}\,,

(314)

compensate for the difference of radial charges calculated from the fields at
r_{1}
and at
r_{2}
.

When
m=0
we obtain the desired fields by choosing
{\mathring{\mu}}
and
{\mathring{\lambda}}_{A}
so that the radial charges in (
314
) compensate for the difference of the respective radial charges at
r_{1}
and
r_{2}
at
u=0
, and
by choosing

{{\mathring{m}}}_{AB}\big|_{u=0}=0=\mathring{s}_{AB}\big|_{u=0}\,.

(315)

The remaining fields vanish on
S^{2}
, in which case we are done.

Otherwise recall the obstruction
(
283
) with
p=1
:

\displaystyle{\overset{[2]}{q}}{}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}\Big|_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}

\displaystyle=\alpha^{2}\bigg[sq_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{r_{1}}+\frac{1}{2s}({{{{\varepsilon}}}}-\alpha^{2}s^{2}){h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{s}\bigg]_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}+\alpha^{2}r_{2}q_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\Big|_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}

\displaystyle=\alpha^{2}\bigg[sq_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{s}+\frac{1}{2s}({{{{\varepsilon}}}}-\alpha^{2}s^{2}){h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{s}\bigg]_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\,.

So at, say,
r=r_{2}
we can compensate
all
radial charge
deficits by choosing the remaining fields as

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\mathring{s}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{u=0}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}-q_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\Big|^{r_{2}}_{r_{1}}\\
0\ ,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\partial_{u}^{2}\mathring{s}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{u=0}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}-{\overset{[2]}{q}}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\Big|_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\\
-{\overset{[2]}{q}}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\Big|^{r_{2}}_{r_{1}}+\alpha^{2}\bigg[{sq_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{s}+\frac{1}{2s}({{{{\varepsilon}}}}-\alpha^{2}s^{2}){h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}\big|_{s}\bigg]_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}}\ ,\end{cases}

with the upper case being for
\alpha=0
and the lower case for
\alpha\neq 0
, and

\displaystyle({\mathring{D}}^{B}\partial_{u}\mathring{s}_{AB})^{[{{\text{H}}}]}\big|_{u=0}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}0\ ,&{{\mathfrak{g}}}=1\,,\\
-\frac{1}{2}{\overset{[3,1]}{Q}}{}^{[{{\text{H}}}]}_{A}\Big|^{r_{2}}_{r_{1}}\ ,&{{\mathfrak{g}}}\geq 2\,,\end{cases}

\displaystyle({\mathring{D}}^{B}\partial^{2}_{u}\mathring{s}_{AB})^{[{{\text{H}}}]}\big|_{u=0}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}0\ ,&{{\mathfrak{g}}}=1\,,\\
-\frac{1}{2}{\overset{[3,2]}{Q}}{}^{[{{\text{H}}}]}_{A}\Big|^{r_{2}}_{r_{1}}\ ,&{{\mathfrak{g}}}\geq 2\,,\end{cases}

where
\big[f(r)\big]^{r_{2}}_{r_{1}}\equiv\big[f(s)\big]^{r_{2}}_{r_{1}}\equiv f(s)\big|^{r_{2}}_{r_{1}}:=f(r_{2})-f(r_{1})
, and where we have used that
{\mathring{\lambda}}_{A}
vanishes if
{{\mathfrak{g}}}\geq 2
.

\qed

Appendix A
Constructing the
\kappa_{i}
’s

Recall that
\hat{\kappa}_{i}(s)=s^{-i}
.
We wish to construct a sequence of smooth functions
{\kappa}_{i}
compactly supported in
(r_{1},r_{2})
satisfying

\displaystyle\displaystyle\langle\kappa_{i},\hat{\kappa}_{j}\rangle\equiv\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\kappa_{i}(r)\hat{\kappa}_{j}(r)\,dr=0\quad\mbox{for $j<i$}\,,

(316)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\langle\kappa_{i},\hat{\kappa}_{i}\rangle=1\,.

(317)

This can be done as follows:
Let
{\chi}
be any smooth non-negative function supported away from neighborhoods of
{r}_{1}
and
{r}_{2}
, with integral 1. Let

{\kappa}_{i}=c_{i}{\chi}f_{i}\,,

where the
f_{i}
’s are constructed by a Gram-Schmidt orthonormalisation procedure from the family of monomials in
1/r
, namely
\{1,{r}^{-1},{r}^{-2},\ldots\}
, in the space
\mathbb{H}:=L^{2}([{r}_{1},{r}_{2}],{\chi}d{r})
,
so that
the scalar product is

\langle\phi,\psi\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}=\int_{{r}_{1}}^{{r}_{2}}(\phi\psi{\chi})(r)\,dr\,,

and the
c_{i}
’s are constants chosen so that
(
317
) holds; the possibility of doing so will be justified shortly.
Then, by construction,
f_{i}
is a polynomial of order
i
in
1/r
which is
\mathbb{H}
-orthogonal to any such polynomial of order
j<i
; this is (
316
).
As for (
317
), we note that each of the functions

{r}^{-i}
can be decomposed in the basis
\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
as
{r}^{-i}=\sum_{j=0}^{i}a_{ij}f_{j}({r})
, with
a_{ii}\neq 0
since otherwise the right-hand side would be a polynomial in
1/r
of order less than or equal to
i-1
.
This shows that

\int_{{r}_{1}}^{{r}_{2}}r^{-i}f_{i}(r){\chi}(r)\,dr=a_{ii}\neq 0\,,

so that we can indeed choose
c_{i}=1/a_{ii}
to fulfill (
317
).

Appendix B
Recursion formulae

For ease of further reference we collect here all the integral kernels appearing in (
151
)-(
152
), as needed for
{C^{2}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}}
-gluing and for various induction arguments in the rest of this Appendix:

\displaystyle\overset{(1,0,0)}{\psi}(r)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2r}\left({{\varepsilon}}-{\alpha^{2}}r^{2}{-\frac{2m}{r}}\right)\,,

(318)

\displaystyle\overset{(1,1,0)}{\psi}(r)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left({{\varepsilon}}-{\alpha^{2}}r^{2}{-\frac{2m}{r}}\right)\,,\quad\overset{(1,0,1)}{\psi}(r)=0\,,

(319)

\displaystyle\overset{(1,0)}{\psi}(s,r)

\displaystyle={{\alpha^{2}}r}\hat{\kappa}_{1}(s){-mr\hat{\kappa}_{4}(s)}\,,\quad\overset{(1,1)}{{\psi}}(s,r)=\frac{2r^{2}\hat{\kappa}_{4}(s)}{3}+\frac{\hat{\kappa}_{1}(s)}{3r}\,,

(320)

\displaystyle\overset{(2,0)}{\psi}(s,r)

\displaystyle=\frac{9m^{2}r}{2}\hat{\kappa}_{6}(s)+\frac{\alpha^{2}(2m+4r^{3}\alpha^{2})}{4r}\hat{\kappa}_{1}(s)

(321)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{8m^{2}+16mr^{3}\alpha^{2}}{16r}\hat{\kappa}_{4}(s)-\frac{3mr{{{{\varepsilon}}}}}{2}\hat{\kappa}_{5}(s)\,,

\displaystyle\overset{(2,1)}{{\psi}}(s,r)

\displaystyle=(r^{2}{{\varepsilon}}-\frac{3{m}r}{4})\hat{\kappa}_{5}(s)+\frac{(9{m}-4r{{\varepsilon}})\hat{\kappa}_{1}(s)}{12r^{3}}+\frac{{{\varepsilon}}r\hat{\kappa}_{4}(s)}{3}

(322)

\displaystyle\quad-3{m}r^{2}\hat{\kappa}_{6}(s)\,,

\displaystyle\overset{(2,2)}{{\psi}}(s,r)

\displaystyle=\frac{r^{2}\hat{\kappa}_{5}(s)}{2}-\frac{\hat{\kappa}_{1}(s)}{6r^{2}}-\frac{r\hat{\kappa}_{4}(s)}{3}\,,

(323)

\displaystyle\overset{(0,0,0)}{\chi}(r)

\displaystyle=0\,,\quad\overset{(1,1,0)}{\chi}(r)=\overset{(1,0,1)}{\chi}(r)=0\,,\quad\overset{(1,0,0)}{\chi}(r)=\frac{{{\varepsilon}}}{2r^{4}}-\frac{{\alpha^{2}}}{2r^{2}}\,,

(324)

\displaystyle\overset{(0,0)}{\chi}(s,r)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}\left(\frac{2\hat{\kappa}_{1}(s)}{r^{3}}+\hat{\kappa}_{4}(s)\right)\,,

(325)

\displaystyle\overset{(1,0)}{\chi}(s,r)

\displaystyle={-\frac{3m\hat{\kappa}_{6}(s)}{2}-\frac{m\hat{\kappa}_{4}(s)}{2r^{2}}}+\frac{{\alpha^{2}}\hat{\kappa}_{1}(s)}{2r^{2}}+\frac{{{\varepsilon}}\hat{\kappa}_{5}(s)}{2}\,,

(326)

\displaystyle\overset{(1,1)}{\chi}(s,r)

\displaystyle=\frac{\hat{\kappa}_{5}(s)}{4}-\frac{\hat{\kappa}_{1}(s)}{4r^{4}}\,,

(327)

\displaystyle\overset{(2,0)}{\chi}(s,r)

\displaystyle={m}\bigg(\frac{75{m}}{8}\hat{\kappa}_{8}(s)-\frac{77{{\varepsilon}}}{12}\hat{\kappa}_{7}(s)+\frac{3}{8}(5\alpha^{2}-\frac{2{{\varepsilon}}}{r^{2}})\hat{\kappa}_{5}(s)\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+(\frac{9{m}^{2}}{4r^{2}}+{{\varepsilon}}^{2})\hat{\kappa}_{6}(s)\,,

\displaystyle\overset{(2,1)}{\chi}(s,r)

\displaystyle=-\frac{7{m}}{4}\hat{\kappa}_{7}(s)-\frac{3{m}}{8r^{2}}\hat{\kappa}_{5}(s)+\frac{7{{\varepsilon}}}{10}\hat{\kappa}_{6}(s)

(329)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{6{m}-2r^{3}\alpha^{2}+r{{\varepsilon}}}{6r^{3}}\hat{\kappa}_{4}(s)+\frac{15{m}-80r^{3}\alpha^{2}+16r{{\varepsilon}}}{120r^{6}}\hat{\kappa}_{1}(s)\,,

\displaystyle\overset{(2,2)}{\chi}(s,r)

\displaystyle=\frac{\hat{\kappa}_{1}(s)}{15r^{5}}-\frac{\hat{\kappa}_{4}(s)}{6r^{2}}+\frac{\hat{\kappa}_{6}(s)}{10}\,.

or

\overset{(3,1)}{{\psi}}
or
\overset{(2,0)}{\chi}

\overset{(2,1)}{{\psi}}
or
\overset{(1,0)}{\chi}

\overset{(1,1)}{{\psi}}
or
\overset{(0,0)}{\chi}

\overset{(5,2)}{{\psi}}
or
\overset{(4,1)}{\chi}

\overset{(4,2)}{{\psi}}
or
\overset{(3,1)}{\chi}

\overset{(3,2)}{{\psi}}
or
\overset{(2,1)}{\chi}

\overset{(2,2)}{{\psi}}
or
\overset{(1,1)}{\chi}

\overset{(5,3)}{{\psi}}
or
\overset{(4,2)}{\chi}

\overset{(4,3)}{{\psi}}
or
\overset{(3,2)}{\chi}

\overset{(3,3)}{{\psi}}
or
\overset{(2,2)}{\chi}

\overset{(5,4)}{{\psi}}
or
\overset{(4,3)}{\chi}

\overset{(4,4)}{{\psi}}
or
\overset{(3,3)}{\chi}

\overset{(5,5)}{{\psi}}
or
\overset{(4,4)}{\chi}

Figure B.2:

The dash-dotted lines describe the contributions from the mass parameter
m
, increasing each power by 2. The dotted lines describe the contributions from the Gauss curvature
{{{{\varepsilon}}}}
, increasing each power by 1. The dashed lines arise from the cosmological constant, and are slanted to the left to visualise the fact that they decrease powers by 1.

Highest powers of
s^{-1}
in

\overset{(i,j)}{\psi}

when
