.
The dash-dotted lines describe the contributions from the mass parameter
m
, corresponding to an increase of the highest power by 2. The dotted lines describe the contributions from (
334
), increasing the power by 1.
The tree for

\overset{(i,j)}{\chi}
is identical after replacing
(i,j)
in the table by
(i-1,j-1)
.

This shows in particular that the recursion formulae (
335
) and (
336
), established with
\alpha=0
, remain valid for
\alpha{,m}\in\mathbb{R}
;
but e.g. (
338
) does not hold anymore when
m\neq 0
.

To continue, it is convenient to set

\overset{(k,-1)}{\psi}_{\ell}=0\,.

(342)

Using this notation, putting together (
334
) with (
341
)
we find the recursion formula, for
k\geq i\geq 0
and
k\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\overset{(k+1,i)}{{\psi}}(s,r)=

\displaystyle\overset{(k+1,i)}{{\psi}}_{0}(r)+\frac{\overset{(k+1,i)}{{\psi}}_{1}(r)}{s}+\frac{\overset{(k+1,i)}{{\psi}}_{4}(r)}{s^{4}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\ell=4}^{k+3}\Big[\frac{(1-\ell)}{2(2-\ell)s^{\ell}}\Big(\alpha^{2}(4-\ell){s}-\frac{(\ell-2){{\varepsilon}}}{s}+\frac{2m(1-\ell)}{s^{2}}\Big)\overset{(k,i)}{{\psi}}_{\ell}(r)

\displaystyle\phantom{+\sum_{\ell=4}^{k+3}\Big[}+\frac{(\ell-1)}{\ell(\ell-3){s^{\ell+1}}}\overset{({k},i-1)}{{\psi}}_{\ell}(r)\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\overset{(k+1,i)}{{\psi}}_{0}(r)+\frac{\overset{(k+1,i)}{{\psi}}_{1}(r)}{s}+\frac{\overset{(k+1,i)}{{\psi}}_{4}(r)}{s^{4}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\ell=4}^{k+3}(\ell-1)\bigg[\frac{\alpha^{2}(4-\ell)}{2(\ell-2)}\frac{\overset{(k,i)}{{\psi}}_{\ell}(r)}{s^{\ell-1}}-\frac{m(\ell-1)}{\ell-2}\frac{\overset{(k,i)}{{\psi}}_{\ell}(r)}{s^{\ell+2}}

\displaystyle\phantom{+\sum_{\ell=4}^{k+3}\Big[}+\Big(\frac{{{\varepsilon}}}{2}\overset{(k,i)}{{\psi}}_{\ell}(r)+\frac{1}{\ell(\ell-3)}\,\overset{({k},i-1)}{{\psi}}_{\ell}(r)\Big)\frac{1}{s^{\ell+1}}\bigg]\,,

An identical formula holds for
\chi

with

k\geq i\geq 0

after setting

\overset{(k,-1)}{\chi}_{\ell}=0\,.

(343)

One is led to:

Lemma
B.1

The integral kernels
\overset{(i,j)}{\psi}
and
\overset{(i,j)}{\chi}
are polynomials in
1/s
with coefficients depending upon
r
, with no terms
1/s^{2}
and
1/s^{3}
. Moreover

a)

When
m=0
,
the integral kernels
\overset{(i,j)}{\psi}
,

i\geq 1
,

1\leq j\leq i
,
are polynomials in
1/s
of order
i+3
,
with
\overset{(i,0)}{\psi}
of order 1.

b)

When
m=0
,
the integral kernels
\overset{(i,j)}{\chi}
,
0\leq j\leq i
, are polynomials in
1/s
of order
i+4
.

c)

When
m\neq 0
,
the integral kernels
\overset{(i,j)}{\psi}
,

i\geq 1
,

0\leq j\leq i
,
are polynomials in
1/s
of order not larger than
2i+{3}-j
, with
\overset{(i,0)}{\psi}
and
\overset{(i,1)}{\psi}
of order
2i+2
, and
\overset{(i,i)}{\psi}
of order
i+3
.

d)

When
m\neq 0
,
the integral kernels
\overset{(i,j)}{\chi}
,
0\leq j\leq i
, are polynomials in
1/s
of order not larger than
2i+4-j
, with
\overset{(i,0)}{\chi}
of order
2i+4
, and
\overset{(i,i)}{\chi}
of order
i+4
.

\proof

We summarise the arguments so far, and add some details:

1.

The functions that initialise the induction for
\partial_{u}h_{AB}
involve only
1/s
and
1/s^{4}
terms, and the functions that initialise the induction for
\partial_{u}h_{uA}
involve only
1/s
and
1/s^{5}
terms.

2.

One then applies the recursion formulae (
334
) and (
341
); cf. Figures
B.1
and
B.3
.
We note that
\ln r
- and/or
\ln s
-terms could
a priori
arise in the induction from
1/s
terms in some integrals, but the multiplicative coefficients
(\ell-1)
which appear in the second and third lines in (
334
) and (
341
) guarantee that there will be no
s^{-2}
terms in any of the integral kernels, which in turns guarantees that no logarithmic terms will occur.

3.

When
m=0
, the fact that
\overset{(i,j)}{\psi}
is of order
i+3
in
s^{-1}
follows from (
390
).

4.

Point a) together with the equality
\overset{(i,j)}{\psi}_{i+3}(s,r)=2r^{2}\,\overset{(i-1,j-1)}{\chi}_{i+3}(s,r)
(cf. (
338
)) establishes b).

5.

It follows from (
335
) that
\overset{(i,i)}{\psi}
is of order
s^{-i-3}
when
m=0
, and Figure
B.3
makes it clear that this is not affected by the non-vanishing of
m
.

6.

By following the dashed-dotted arrows in Figure
B.3
starting from the
(1,1)
entry makes it clear that

\overset{(i,1)}{\psi}
is of order
2i+2
in
s^{-1}
when
m\neq 0
. The same holds for
\overset{(i,0)}{\psi}
since the recursion formulae do not depend upon the index
j
of
\overset{(i,j)}{\psi}
, and both initialising polynomials
\overset{(1,0)}{\psi}
and
\overset{(1,1)}{\psi}
are of order 4.
In fact one checks that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\overset{(k,0)}{\chi}_{2k+4}=\frac{(-m)^{k}}{3}\frac{\bigl((2k+1)!\bigr)^{2}}{2^{3k}(k!)^{3}}\,,

(344)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\overset{(k,0)}{\psi}_{2k+2}=\frac{r(-m)^{k}}{2^{3k-1}(k-1)!}\bigg(\frac{(2k)!}{k!}\bigg)^{2}=-\frac{3m}{2r}\overset{(k,1)}{\psi}_{2k+2}\,,

(345)

which further implies

\displaystyle\overset{(k,0)}{\chi}_{2k+4}=-\frac{1}{3mr}\overset{(k+1,0)}{\psi}_{2k+4}=\frac{1}{2r^{2}}\overset{(k+1,1)}{\psi}_{2k+4}\,.

(346)

\Box

We finish this section with the following relations, needed for (
414
):

Lemma
B.2

For
k\geq 2
we have

\displaystyle\overset{(k-1,0)}{\chi}_{2k+2}\overset{(k,0)}{\psi}_{2k+1}

\displaystyle=\overset{(k-1,0)}{\chi}_{2k+1}\overset{(k,0)}{\psi}_{2k+2}\,,

(347)

\displaystyle\overset{(k-1,0)}{\chi}_{2k+2}\overset{(k,1)}{\psi}_{2k+1}

\displaystyle=\overset{(k-1,1)}{\chi}_{2k+1}\overset{(k,0)}{\psi}_{2k+2}+\overset{(k-1,0)}{\chi}_{2k+1}\overset{(k,1)}{\psi}_{2k+2}\,,

(348)

\displaystyle\overset{(k-1,0)}{\chi}_{2k+2}\overset{(k,2)}{\psi}_{2k+1}

\displaystyle=\overset{(k-1,1)}{\chi}_{2k+1}\overset{(k,1)}{\psi}_{2k+2}\,.

(349)

\proof

We start by noting the
following recursion formulae, which can be read off (
342
)-(
343
), for
k\geq 1
,
k\geq i\geq 0
, and
n\geq 5
:

\displaystyle\overset{(k,i)}{\chi}_{n}={{m}}_{n}\overset{(k-1,i)}{\chi}_{n-2}+{{{{\varepsilon}}}}_{n}\overset{(k-1,i)}{\chi}_{n-1}+{{\alpha}}_{n}\overset{(k-1,i)}{\chi}_{n+1}+{{\iota}}_{n}\overset{(k-1,i-1)}{\chi}_{n-1}\,,

\displaystyle\overset{(k,i)}{\psi}_{n}={{m}}_{n}\overset{(k-1,i)}{\psi}_{n-2}+{{{{\varepsilon}}}}_{n}\overset{(k-1,i)}{\psi}_{n-1}+{{\alpha}}_{n}\overset{(k-1,i)}{\psi}_{n+1}+{{\iota}}_{n}\overset{(k-1,i-1)}{\psi}_{n-1}\,,

where
{{m}}_{n}
arises from the mass
m
,
{{{{\varepsilon}}}}_{n}
from the Gauss curvature
{{\varepsilon}}
of
{\mathring{\gamma}}
,
{{\alpha}}_{n}
from the cosmological constant encoded in
\alpha
, and
{{\iota}}_{n}
is associated with the term containing a shift in
i
:

\displaystyle{{m}}_{n}

\displaystyle=-m\frac{(n-3)^{2}}{n-4}\,,\quad{{{{\varepsilon}}}}_{n}={{\varepsilon}}\frac{n}{2}\,,\quad{{\alpha}}_{n}=-\alpha^{2}\frac{n(n-3)}{2(n-1)}\,,

\displaystyle{{\iota}}_{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{n-2}{(n-1)(n-4)}\,.

By Lemma
B.1
, the coefficients
\overset{(i,j)}{\chi}_{\ell}
vanish for
\ell+j>2i+4
, and the coefficients
\overset{(i,j)}{\psi}_{\ell}
vanish for
\ell+j>2i+3
. Thus, for
k\geq 2
we can write

\displaystyle\displaystyle\overset{(k,0)}{\psi}_{2k+2}={{m}}_{2k+2}\,\overset{(k-1,0)}{\psi}_{2k}\,,\qquad\overset{(k,1)}{\psi}_{2k+2}={{m}}_{2k+2}\,\overset{(k-1,1)}{\psi}_{2k}\,,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\overset{(k,i)}{\psi}_{2k+1}={{m}}_{2k+1}\overset{(k-1,i)}{\psi}_{2k-1}+{{{{\varepsilon}}}}_{2k+1}\,\overset{(k-1,i)}{\psi}_{2k}+{{\alpha}}_{2k+1}\overset{(k-1,i)}{\psi}_{2k+2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+{{\iota}}_{2k+1}\overset{(k-1,i-1)}{\psi}_{2k}\,,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\overset{(k,2)}{\psi}_{2k+1}={{m}}_{2k+1}\overset{(k-1,2)}{\psi}_{2k-1}+\underbrace{{{{{\varepsilon}}}}_{2k+1}\,\overset{(k-1,2)}{\psi}_{2k}+{{\alpha}}_{2k+1}\overset{(k-1,2)}{\psi}_{2k+2}}_{=0}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+{{\iota}}_{2k+1}\overset{(k-1,1)}{\psi}_{2k}\,,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\overset{(k,1)}{\psi}_{2k+1}={{m}}_{2k+1}\overset{(k-1,1)}{\psi}_{2k-1}+{{{{\varepsilon}}}}_{2k+1}\,\overset{(k-1,1)}{\psi}_{2k}+{{\alpha}}_{2k+1}\underbrace{\overset{(k-1,1)}{\psi}_{2k+2}}_{=0}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+{{\iota}}_{2k+1}\overset{(k-1,0)}{\psi}_{2k}\,,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\overset{(k,0)}{\psi}_{2k+1}={{m}}_{2k+1}\overset{(k-1,0)}{\psi}_{2k-1}+{{{{\varepsilon}}}}_{2k+1}\,\overset{(k-1,0)}{\psi}_{2k}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+\underbrace{{{\alpha}}_{2k+1}\overset{(k-1,0)}{\psi}_{2k+2}+{{\iota}}_{2k+1}\overset{(k-1,-1)}{\psi}_{2k}}_{=0}\,.

Similarly for
k\geq 3
we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\overset{(k-1,0)}{\chi}_{2k+2}={{m}}_{2k+2}\,\overset{(k-2,0)}{\chi}_{2k}\,,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\overset{(k-1,1)}{\chi}_{2k+1}={{m}}_{2k+1}\overset{(k-2,1)}{\chi}_{2k-1}+\underbrace{{{{{\varepsilon}}}}_{2k+1}\,\overset{(k-2,1)}{\chi}_{2k}+{{\alpha}}_{2k+1}\overset{(k-2,1)}{\chi}_{2k+2}}_{=0}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+{{\iota}}_{2k+1}\overset{(k-2,0)}{\chi}_{2k}\,,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\overset{(k-1,0)}{\chi}_{2k+1}={{m}}_{2k+1}\overset{(k-2,0)}{\chi}_{2k-1}+{{{{\varepsilon}}}}_{2k+1}\,\overset{(k-2,0)}{\chi}_{2k}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+\underbrace{{{\alpha}}_{2k+1}\overset{(k-2,0)}{\chi}_{2k+2}+{{\iota}}_{2k+1}\overset{(k-2,-1)}{\chi}_{2k}}_{=0}\,.

We now check that (
347
)-(
349
) hold with
k=2
:

\displaystyle\underbrace{\overset{(1,0)}{\chi}_{6}}_{-\frac{3m}{2}\ \text{by \eqref{25XI22.3}};}\underbrace{\overset{(2,0)}{\psi}_{5}}_{-\frac{3mr{{{{\varepsilon}}}}}{2},\ \text{by \eqref{25XI22.10}};}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\overset{(1,0)}{\chi}_{5}}_{\frac{{{\varepsilon}}}{2}\ \text{by \eqref{25XI22.3}};}\underbrace{\overset{(2,0)}{\psi}_{6}}_{\frac{9m^{2}r}{2}\ \text{by \eqref{25XI22.10}};}\,,

\displaystyle\underbrace{\overset{(1,0)}{\chi}_{6}}_{-\frac{3m}{2}\ \text{by \eqref{25XI22.3}};}\underbrace{\overset{(2,1)}{\psi}_{5}}_{r^{2}{{\varepsilon}}-\frac{3mr}{4}\ \text{by \eqref{25XI22.15}};}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\overset{(1,1)}{\chi}_{5}}_{\frac{1}{4}\ \text{by \eqref{25XI22.6}};}\underbrace{\overset{(2,0)}{\psi}_{6}}_{\frac{9m^{2}r}{2}\ \text{by \eqref{25XI22.10}};}

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\overset{(1,0)}{\chi}_{5}}_{\frac{{{\varepsilon}}}{2}\ \text{by \eqref{25XI22.3}};}\underbrace{\overset{(2,1)}{\psi}_{6}}_{-3mr^{2}\ \text{by \eqref{25XI22.15}};}\,,

\displaystyle\underbrace{\overset{(1,0)}{\chi}_{6}}_{{-\frac{3m}{2}}\ \text{by \eqref{25XI22.3}};}\underbrace{\overset{(2,2)}{\psi}_{5}}_{{\frac{r^{2}}{2}\ \text{by \eqref{25XI22.15b}};}}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\overset{(1,1)}{\chi}_{5}}_{{\frac{1}{4}}\ \text{by \eqref{25XI22.6}};}\underbrace{\overset{(2,1)}{\psi}_{6}}_{{-3mr^{2}\ \text{by \eqref{25XI22.15}};}}\,.

To continue, let
k\geq 3
and assume that (
347
) holds with
k
replaced by
k-1
, then:

\displaystyle\overset{(k-1,0)}{\chi}_{2k+2}\overset{(k,0)}{\psi}_{2k+1}-\overset{(k-1,0)}{\chi}_{2k+1}\overset{(k,0)}{\psi}_{2k+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{{m}}_{2k+2}\,\overset{(k-2,0)}{\chi}_{2k}\big({{m}}_{2k+1}\overset{(k-1,0)}{\psi}_{2k-1}+\cancel{{{{{\varepsilon}}}}_{2k+1}\,\overset{(k-1,0)}{\psi}_{2k}}\big)

\displaystyle-\big({{m}}_{2k+1}\overset{(k-2,0)}{\chi}_{2k-1}+\cancel{{{{{\varepsilon}}}}_{2k+1}\,\overset{(k-2,0)}{\chi}_{2k}}\big){{m}}_{2k+2}\,\overset{(k-1,0)}{\psi}_{2k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{{m}}_{2k+2}\,{{m}}_{2k+1}\,\big(\overset{(k-2,0)}{\chi}_{2k}\overset{(k-1,0)}{\psi}_{2k-1}-\overset{(k-2,0)}{\chi}_{2k-1}\,\overset{(k-1,0)}{\psi}_{2k}\big)

\displaystyle=0\,.

Next, we assume that (
348
) holds with
k
replaced by
k-1
. Then

\displaystyle\overset{(k-1,0)}{\chi}_{2k+2}\overset{(k,1)}{\psi}_{2k+1}-\overset{(k-1,1)}{\chi}_{2k+1}\overset{(k,0)}{\psi}_{2k+2}-\overset{(k-1,0)}{\chi}_{2k+1}\overset{(k,1)}{\psi}_{2k+2}

\displaystyle={{m}}_{2k+2}\,\overset{(k-2,0)}{\chi}_{2k}\overset{(k,1)}{\psi}_{2k+1}-\overset{(k-1,1)}{\chi}_{2k+1}{{m}}_{2k+2}\,\overset{(k-1,0)}{\psi}_{2k}

\displaystyle\quad-\overset{(k-1,0)}{\chi}_{2k+1}{{m}}_{2k+2}\,\overset{(k-1,1)}{\psi}_{2k}

\displaystyle={{m}}_{2k+2}\Big[\overset{(k-2,0)}{\chi}_{2k}\Big({{m}}_{2k+1}\overset{(k-1,1)}{\psi}_{2k-1}+\xcancel{{{{{\varepsilon}}}}_{2k+1}\,\overset{(k-1,1)}{\psi}_{2k}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\cancel{{{\iota}}_{2k+1}\overset{(k-1,0)}{\psi}_{2k}}\big)

\displaystyle\phantom{{{m}}_{2k+2}\Big[}-\Big({{m}}_{2k+1}\overset{(k-2,1)}{\chi}_{2k-1}+\cancel{{{\iota}}_{2k+1}\overset{(k-2,0)}{\chi}_{2k}}\Big)\overset{(k-1,0)}{\psi}_{2k}

\displaystyle\phantom{{{m}}_{2k+2}\Big[}-\Big({{m}}_{2k+1}\overset{(k-2,0)}{\chi}_{2k-1}+\xcancel{{{{{\varepsilon}}}}_{2k+1}\,\overset{(k-2,0)}{\chi}_{2k}}\Big)\overset{(k-1,1)}{\psi}_{2k}\Big]

\displaystyle={{m}}_{2k+2}{{m}}_{2k+1}\Big[\overset{(k-2,0)}{\chi}_{2k}\overset{(k-1,1)}{\psi}_{2k-1}-\overset{(k-2,1)}{\chi}_{2k-1}\overset{(k-1,0)}{\psi}_{2k}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\overset{(k-2,0)}{\chi}_{2k-1}\overset{(k-1,1)}{\psi}_{2k}\Big]

\displaystyle=0\,.

Finally, suppose that (
349
) holds with
k
replaced by
k-1
. Then

\displaystyle\overset{(k-1,0)}{\chi}_{2k+2}\overset{(k,2)}{\psi}_{2k+1}-\overset{(k-1,1)}{\chi}_{2k+1}\overset{(k,1)}{\psi}_{2k+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{{m}}_{2k+2}\overset{(k-2,0)}{\chi}_{2k}\,\big({{m}}_{2k+1}\overset{(k-1,2)}{\psi}_{2k-1}+\cancel{{{\iota}}_{2k+1}\overset{(k-1,1)}{\psi}_{2k}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad-\big({{m}}_{2k+1}\overset{(k-2,1)}{\chi}_{2k-1}+\cancel{{{m}}_{2k+2}\overset{(k-2,0)}{\chi}_{2k}}\big){{\iota}}_{2k+1}\overset{(k-1,1)}{\psi}_{2k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{{m}}_{2k+2}{{m}}_{2k+1}\big(\overset{(k-2,0)}{\chi}_{2k}\overset{(k-1,2)}{\psi}_{2k-1}-\overset{(k-2,1)}{\chi}_{2k-1}\overset{(k-1,1)}{\psi}_{2k}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\,.

The validity of (
347
)-(
349
) follows thus by induction.

\qed

Appendix C
Operators on
{\mathbf{S}}

The aim of this appendix is to analyse the mapping properties of several operators acting on tensor fields defined on a compact orientable two-dimensional manifold
({{}^{{2}}M}\equiv{\mathbf{S}},{\mathring{\gamma}})
with constant Gauss curvature
{{{{\varepsilon}}}}\in\{0,\pm 1\}
.

C.1
Vector and tensor spherical harmonics

For integers
\ell\geq 1
,
-\ell\leq m\leq\ell
, let
Y^{(\ell m)}
be the standard spherical harmonics on the unit sphere. Following the notations and conventions of
[
2
,
20
]
,
we define the
vector spherical harmonics
, as well as
trace-free symmetric
2
-tensor spherical harmonics

on
S^{2}
as:

1.

For
\ell\geq 1
,
-\ell\leq m\leq\ell
, define the vector fields

\displaystyle E_{A}^{(\ell m)}:=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\ell(\ell+1)}}{\mathring{D}}_{A}Y^{(\ell m)}\,,\ H_{A}^{(\ell m)}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\ell(\ell+1)}}\epsilon_{AB}{\mathring{D}}^{B}Y^{(\ell m)}\,,

(350)

where
\epsilon_{AB}
denote the volume two-form of
S^{2}
.

2.

For
\ell\geq 2
,
-\ell\leq m\leq\ell
, define the trace-free symmetric
2
-tensors

\displaystyle\psi^{(\ell m)}_{AB}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}\ell(\ell+1)-1}}C(E^{(\ell m)})_{AB}\,,

(351)

\displaystyle\phi_{AB}^{(\ell m)}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}\ell(\ell+1)-1}}C(H^{(\ell m)})_{AB}\,,

(352)

where the operator
C(\xi)_{AB}=TS({\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi_{B})
of (
38
)
corresponds to the operator
-\not{\mathcal{D}}_{2}^{*}

of
[
2
,
20
]
.

Let us summarise the properties of these
tensor harmonics, as needed in the main text. More details and proofs can be found in
[
20
]
, see also
[
17
]
.

Lemma
C.1

The following holds.

1.

On
S^{2}
,
L^{2}
-integrable functions
f
, vector fields
\xi
and trace-free symmetric
2
-tensors
\varphi
can be decomposed as

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell\geq 0}\sum_{-\ell\leq m\leq\ell}f^{\ell m}Y^{(\ell m)}\,,

\displaystyle\xi_{A}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell\geq 1}\sum_{-\ell\leq m\leq\ell}\xi^{(\ell m)}_{E}E^{(\ell m)}_{A}+\xi^{(\ell m)}_{H}H^{(\ell m)}_{A}\,,

\displaystyle\varphi_{AB}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell\geq 2}\sum_{-\ell\leq m\leq\ell}\varphi^{(\ell m)}_{\psi}\psi^{(\ell m)}_{AB}+\varphi^{(\ell m)}_{\phi}\phi^{(\ell m)}_{AB}\,,

where

\displaystyle f^{(\ell m)}

\displaystyle:=\int_{S^{2}}fY^{(\ell m)}d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}\,,

\displaystyle\xi^{(\ell m)}_{E}

\displaystyle:=\int_{S^{2}}\xi^{A}E_{A}^{(\ell m)}\,,\qquad

\displaystyle\xi_{H}^{(\ell m)}

\displaystyle:=\int_{S^{2}}\xi^{A}H^{(\ell m)}_{A}d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}\,,

\displaystyle\varphi_{\psi}^{(\ell m)}

\displaystyle:=\int_{S^{2}}\varphi^{AB}\psi_{AB}^{(\ell m)}\,,\qquad

\displaystyle\varphi_{\phi}^{(\ell m)}

\displaystyle:=\int_{S^{2}}\varphi^{AB}\phi^{(\ell m)}_{AB}d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}\,.

2.

It holds that for
\ell\geq 2
,

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{A}\psi_{AB}^{(\ell m)}=\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}\ell(\ell+1)-1}\,E_{B}^{(\ell m)}\,,

(353)

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{A}\phi_{AB}^{(\ell m)}=\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}\ell(\ell+1)-1}\,H_{B}^{(\ell m)}\,.

(354)

3.

The space of conformal Killing vector fields on
S^{2}
is spanned by
E_{A}^{(1m)}
and
H_{A}^{(1m)}
.

C.2
The conformal Killing operator

Consider the conformal Killing operator on a closed
{{2}}
-dimensional Riemannian manifold
({{}^{{2}}M},{{\mathring{\gamma}}})
:

\xi^{A}\mapsto{\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi_{B}+{\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi_{A}-{\mathring{D}}^{C}\xi_{C}{{\mathring{\gamma}}}_{AB}\equiv 2C(\xi)_{AB}\,.

(355)

We have

Proposition
C.2

The conformal Killing operator on two dimensional manifolds is elliptic, with

1.

six dimensional kernel and no cokernel on
S^{2}
;

2.

two dimensional kernel and cokernel on
\mathbb{T}^{2}
;

3.

no kernel and
6({{\mathfrak{g}}}-1)
dimensional cokernel on manifolds of genus
{{\mathfrak{g}}}\geq 2
.

\proof

We first show that
C
is elliptic. For this, let
0\neq k\in T^{*}({{}^{{2}}M})
and let
\sigma(k)
be the symbol of
C
, with kernel determined by the equation

\displaystyle\big(\sigma(k)\big)_{AB}\equiv\frac{1}{2}\big(k_{A}\xi_{B}+k_{B}\xi_{A}-k^{C}\xi_{C}{\mathring{\gamma}}_{AB}\big)=0\,.

(356)

Contracting with
k^{A}k^{B}

one obtains

k^{A}k_{A}k^{C}\xi_{C}=0\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad k^{C}\xi_{C}=0\,,

(357)

Equation (
356
) becomes now

\displaystyle k_{A}\xi_{B}+k_{B}\xi_{A}=0\,.

(358)

Contracting with
k^{A}
one concludes that

\displaystyle k^{A}k_{A}\xi_{B}=0\,.

(359)

Hence
\xi_{B}=0
, and ellipticity of
C
follows.

Concerning the kernel in point 1.,
we start by noting that the equation

{\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi_{B}+{\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi_{A}-{\mathring{D}}^{C}\xi_{C}{{\mathring{\gamma}}}_{AB}=0

(360)

is conformally invariant. Hence it suffices to analyse it on the unit round sphere. Therefore, by Lemma
C.1
, its solution are of the form

\xi_{A}={\mathring{D}}_{A}\varphi+\epsilon_{AB}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\psi\,,

where
\varphi
and
\psi
are linear combinations of
\ell=1
spherical harmonics. The
\varphi
-solutions are in one-to-one correspondence with the three generators of boosts of four-dimensional Minkowski space-time, while the
\psi
-solutions correspond to rotations.

The statements about the kernel in points 2. and 3. follow from Proposition
C.3
which we are about to prove.

The statements about the cokernels follow from

C^{\dagger}=-{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}

where
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!
is the divergence operator on two-symmetric trace-free tensors,

({\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}h)_{A}:={\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\,,

(361)

together with the results in Section
C.3
below.

\Box

Recall that we use the symbol
CKV
to denote the space of conformal Killing vectors, while
TT
denotes the space of trace-free divergence-free symmetric two-tensors, and orthogonality is defined in
L^{2}
. Then:

Proposition
C.3

1.

On
\mathbb{T}^{2}
all conformal Killing vectors are covariantly constant, hence Killing.

2.

There are no nontrivial Killing vectors or conformal Killing vectors on higher genus two dimensional manifolds.

3.

\text{im}({\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}C)={{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}
.

4.

For any vector field
\xi
we have
C(\xi)^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}=0
.

\proof

1. and 2.:
Taking the divergence of (
360
) and commuting derivatives
leads to

{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi_{B}+{\mathring{R}}_{BC}\xi^{C}=0\,.

(362)

Multiplying by
\xi^{B}
and integrating over
{{}^{{2}}M}
one finds

\int(|{\mathring{D}}\xi|^{2}-{\mathring{R}}_{BC}\xi^{B}\xi^{C})=0\,.

(363)

If
{\mathring{R}}_{BC}\leq 0
we find that
\xi
is covariantly constant, vanishing if
{\mathring{R}}_{BC}<0
.

3.
Let
\eta
be
L^{2}
-orthogonal to the image of
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}C
, thus for any vector field
\xi
we have

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\eta^{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}({\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi_{B}+{\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi_{A}-{\mathring{D}}^{C}\xi_{C}{{\mathring{\gamma}}}_{AB})d\mu_{{{\mathring{\gamma}}}}

\displaystyle=2\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\eta^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\big(\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}({\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi_{B})\big)d\mu_{{{\mathring{\gamma}}}}

\displaystyle=-2\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\eta^{A}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}({\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi_{B})d\mu_{{{\mathring{\gamma}}}}=-2\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}({\mathring{D}}^{B}\eta^{A})\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}({\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi_{B})d\mu_{{{\mathring{\gamma}}}}\,.

Letting
\xi=\eta
we conclude that
\eta
is a conformal Killing vector.

4. The field
C(\xi)^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}
is obtained by
L^{2}
-projecting
C(\xi)
on
TT
. As such, for any
h\in{{\text{TT}}}
we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}h^{AB}C(\xi)_{AB}\,d\mu_{{{\mathring{\gamma}}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}h^{AB}\big(\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}({\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi_{B})\big)\,d\mu_{{{\mathring{\gamma}}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}(h^{AB}){\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi_{B}\,d\mu_{{{\mathring{\gamma}}}}=\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}h^{AB}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi_{B}\,d\mu_{{{\mathring{\gamma}}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\underbrace{{\mathring{D}}_{A}h^{AB}}_{0}\xi_{B}\,d\mu_{{{\mathring{\gamma}}}}=0\,.

Hence
C(\xi)^{[{{\text{TT}}}]}=0
.

\qed

C.3
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!

We denote by
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(1)}}\,}\!
the divergence operator on vector fields:

{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(1)}}\,}\xi:={\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi^{A}\,.

(364)

and by
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!
that on two-symmetric trace-free tensors,

({\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}h)_{A}:={\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\,.

(365)

As is well-known,
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!
is conformally covariant in all dimensions. In particular, in dimension two if
g_{AB}=e^{\varphi}\bar{g}_{AB}
then

D_{A}h^{AB}=e^{-2\varphi}\bar{D}_{A}(e^{2\varphi}h^{AB})\,,

(366)

where
D
is the Levi-Civita connection of
g
and
\bar{D}
that of
\bar{g}
.
It follows that it suffices to understand the kernel for metrics of constant Gauss curvature.

As already pointed out, on a two-dimensional closed negatively curved manifold of genus
{{\mathfrak{g}}}\geq 2
,
the operator
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!
has a
6({{\mathfrak{g}}}-1)
-dimensional kernel; it has no kernel on
S^{2}
; on a flat torus
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!
has a two-dimensional kernel consisting of covariantly constant fields (cf., e.g.,
[
10
]
Theorem 8.2
and the paragraph that follows or
[
11
, Theorem 6.1 and Corollary 6.1]
).

We claim that:

Lemma
C.4

Consider a two-dimensional Riemannian manifold
({{}^{{2}}M},{\mathring{\gamma}})
. Then the operator
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!
acting on symmetric traceless tensors is elliptic, and it holds that

\text{im}\,{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}={{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}\,.

In particular if
{\mathring{R}}_{BC}<0
, the operator
{}{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}{}
is surjective.

\proof

We start with ellipticity. For this, let
0\neq k\in T^{*}({{}^{{2}}M})
and let
\sigma(k)
be the symbol of
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!
, with kernel determined by the equation

\displaystyle\big(\sigma(k)h\big)_{A}\equiv k^{C}h_{AC}=0\,.

(367)

In an orthonormal frame in which
k^{2}=0
this is equivalent to

\displaystyle h_{11}=h_{12}=0\,.

(368)

For symmetric and traceless tensors
h_{AB}
this is the same as
h_{AB}=0
. So
\sigma(k)
has trivial kernel for
k\neq 0
, which is the definition of ellipticity.

Next, let
\xi
be
L^{2}
-orthogonal to the image of
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!
, then for all smooth symmetric traceless tensors
h
we have

0=\int\xi^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}=-\int{\mathring{D}}^{B}\xi^{A}h_{AB}=-\int\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}({\mathring{D}}^{B}\xi^{A})h_{AB}\,.

(369)

This shows that
\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}({\mathring{D}}^{B}\xi^{A})=0
, hence
\xi^{A}
is a conformal vector field.

Since no such fields exist when the Ricci tensor is negative by Proposition
C.3
, surjectivity for such metrics follows.

\Box

C.4
\widehat{\operatorname{{L}}}
and
\operatorname{{L}}

To continue, we wish to analyse the operators

\widehat{\operatorname{{L}}}=-{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}C\operatorname{{L}}\,,\quad\operatorname{{L}}=({\mathring{D}}{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(1)}}\,}-{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}C+{{\varepsilon}})\,;

(370)

recall that
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(1)}}\,}\xi={\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi^{A}
,
({\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}h)_{A}={\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}
, and that
{{\varepsilon}}\in\{0,\pm 1\}
is the Gauss curvature of
{\mathring{\gamma}}
.

We consider first the operator
\xi\mapsto{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}C(\xi)
. One finds

\big({\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}C(\xi)\big)_{A}=\frac{1}{2}({\Delta_{{{\mathring{\gamma}}}}}+{{\varepsilon}})\xi_{A}\,,

(371)

which is elliptic, self-adjoint,
with kernel and cokernel spanned by conformal Killing vectors.

Next, we turn our attention to
\operatorname{{L}}
:

\displaystyle\phantom{xxx}\operatorname{{L}}(\xi)_{A}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}^{C}\xi_{C}}+\frac{1}{2}\big(\underbrace{{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}^{C}\xi_{C}-{\mathring{D}}^{C}({\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi_{C}}_{-\mathring{R}^{C}{}_{A}\xi_{C}}+{\mathring{D}}_{C}\xi_{A})\big)+{{\varepsilon}}\xi_{A}

(372)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}^{C}\xi_{C}+\frac{1}{2}\big(-{\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}+{{\varepsilon}}\big)\xi_{A}\,.

One readily checks that
\operatorname{{L}}
is also elliptic and self-adjoint.

Applying
{\mathring{D}}^{A}
to (
372
), commuting derivatives, and using

{\mathring{R}}_{AB}={{\varepsilon}}{{\mathring{\gamma}}}_{AB}

(373)

one finds that the kernel of
\operatorname{{L}}
consists of vector fields satisfying

\frac{1}{2}{\Delta_{{{\mathring{\gamma}}}}}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi^{A}=0\,,

(374)

hence
{\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi^{A}=c
for some constant
c
. Integrating this last equality over
{{}^{{2}}M}
shows that
c=0
.
It now follows that the kernel of
\widehat{\operatorname{{L}}}
consists of vector fields satisfying

\big(-{\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}+{{\varepsilon}}\big)\xi_{A}=0\,,\qquad{\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi^{A}=0\,.

(375)

Recall the Hodge decomposition: on a compact two dimensional oriented manifold every one-form can be decomposed as

\xi_{A}={\mathring{D}}_{A}\psi+\epsilon_{AB}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\phi+r_{A}\,,

(376)

where
r_{A}
is a harmonic one-form, i.e. a covector field satisfying

{\mathring{D}}^{A}r_{A}=0=\epsilon^{AB}{\mathring{D}}_{A}r_{B}=(-{\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}+{{\varepsilon}})r_{A}\,.

(377)

On
S^{2}
the forms
r_{A}
vanish identically, and on manifolds with genus
{{\mathfrak{g}}}
the space of
r_{A}
’s is
2{{\mathfrak{g}}}
-dimensional; cf., e.g.,
[
8
, Theorems 19.11 and 19.14]
or
[
9
, Theorem 18.7]
.

From the second equation in (
375
) together with (
376
)-(
377
)
we find that the Laplacian of
\psi
vanishes, hence
\psi
is constant, and the first equation in (
375
) gives

\epsilon^{AB}{\mathring{D}}_{B}{\Delta_{{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}}\phi=0\,.

(378)

It readily follows that
\phi
is also constant, hence
\xi_{A}=r_{A}
, and we conclude that:

Lemma
C.5

The operator
\operatorname{{L}}
is elliptic, self-adjoint, with kernel and cokernel consisting of one-forms
r_{A}
satisfying (
377
), hence of dimension equal to twice the genus of the compact, oriented, two-dimensional manifold.

We are ready now to pass to the proof of:

Proposition
C.6

The operator
\widehat{\operatorname{{L}}}
is elliptic, self-adjoint, with

\ker\widehat{\operatorname{{L}}}=\coker\widehat{\operatorname{{L}}}={{\text{CKV}}}+{{\text{H}}}\,.

In particular:

1.

on
S^{2}
and on
T^{2}
we have
\ker\widehat{\operatorname{{L}}}=\coker\widehat{\operatorname{{L}}}={{\text{CKV}}}
;

2.

on two-dimensional compact orientable manifolds of genus
{{\mathfrak{g}}}\geq 2
both the kernel and cokernel of
\widehat{\operatorname{{L}}}
are spanned by the
2{{\mathfrak{g}}}
-dimensional space of harmonic 1-forms.

\proof

We first check that
\operatorname{{L}}
and
-{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}C
commute. In view of (
371
)-(
372
) it suffices to check the identity

({\Delta_{{{\mathring{\gamma}}}}}+{{\varepsilon}}){\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}^{C}\xi_{C}={\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}^{C}({\Delta_{{{\mathring{\gamma}}}}}+{{\varepsilon}})\xi_{C}\,,

(379)

which follows from a straightforward commutation of derivatives. This shows that

\widehat{\operatorname{{L}}}
is the composition of two commuting self-adjoint elliptic operators, hence elliptic and self-adjoint.

On
S^{2}
the operator
\operatorname{{L}}
is an isomorphism by Lemma
C.5
, hence the cokernel of
\widehat{\operatorname{{L}}}
is determined by that of
-{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}C
. The claim on the kernel follows by duality.

It should be clear that in manifestly flat coordinates on
\mathbb{T}^{2}
the kernels of both
\operatorname{{L}}
and
-{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}C
consist of covectors
\xi_{A}
with constant entries, which span the space of conformal Killing vectors on
\mathbb{T}^{2}
. Self-adjointness implies the result for the cokernel.

In the higher genus case the operator
-{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}C
is an isomorphism, so that the kernel of
\widehat{\operatorname{{L}}}
coincides with the kernel of
\operatorname{{L}}
, as given by Lemma
C.5
. One concludes as before.

\qed

C.5
P

Consider the operator

\displaystyle Ph_{AB}:=\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}^{C}h_{BC}]\,.

(380)

of (
98
). where
h
is symmetric and
{\mathring{\gamma}}
-traceless.

We have:

Proposition
C.7

The operator
P
is elliptic, self-adjoint and negative, with

1.

six-dimensional cokernel and kernel on
S^{2}
;

2.

two-dimensional kernel and cokernel on
\mathbb{T}^{2}
;

3.

6({{\mathfrak{g}}}-1)
-dimensional cokernel and kernel on manifolds of genus
{{\mathfrak{g}}}\geq 2
.

\proof

Note that

P=C\circ{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}

(381)

is a composition of elliptic operators, hence is elliptic.
Using

{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!\!^{\dagger}=-C\,,\quad C^{\dagger}=-{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\,,

(382)

we have

P=-{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!\!^{\dagger}\circ{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\,,

(383)

from which self-adjointness follows.

Finally, we have

\int h^{AB}Ph_{AB}=-\int h\,{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!\!^{\dagger}\circ{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}h=-\int|{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}h|^{2}\leq 0\,,

(384)

hence all eigenvalues of
P
are negative, and
Ph=0
implies

{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}h=0
.

\qed

C.5.1
S^{2}

As already discussed in Section
C.1
, it follows from
[
2
,
20
]
that on
S^{2}
we can write symmetric trace-free
2
-tensors
\varphi_{AB}
as

\varphi_{AB}=\sum_{\ell\geq 2}\sum_{-\ell\leq m\leq\ell}\varphi^{(\ell m)}_{\psi}\psi^{(\ell m)}_{AB}+\varphi^{(\ell m)}_{\phi}\phi^{(\ell m)}_{AB}\,.

(385)

It follows from (
351
)-(
352
) and (
353
)-(
354
)
that
the operator
P
of (
153
), namely

P\varphi_{AB}=TS[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}^{C}\varphi_{BC}]\equiv C({\mathring{D}}^{C}\varphi_{CD})_{AB}\,,

(386)

acts on
\varphi_{AB}
as

\displaystyle P

\displaystyle\varphi_{AB}=\sum_{\ell\geq 2}\sum_{-\ell\leq m\leq\ell}\varphi^{(\ell m)}_{\psi}C({\mathring{D}}^{B}\psi^{(\ell m)}_{AB})+\varphi^{(\ell m)}_{\phi}C({\mathring{D}}^{B}\phi^{(\ell m)}_{AB})

(387)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell\geq 2}\sum_{-\ell\leq m\leq\ell}\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}\ell(\ell+1)-1}\left(\varphi^{(\ell m)}_{\psi}C(E^{(\ell m)})_{AB}+\varphi^{(\ell m)}_{\phi}C(H^{(\ell m)}_{AB})\right)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\ell\geq 2}\sum_{-\ell\leq m\leq\ell}\underbrace{\left(\frac{1}{2}\ell(\ell+1)-1\right)}_{>0\text{ for }\ell\geq 2}\left(\varphi^{(\ell m)}_{\psi}\psi^{(\ell m)}_{AB}+\varphi^{(\ell m)}_{\phi}\phi^{(\ell m)}_{AB}\right)\,.

In particular the operator
P
is self-adjoint and has trivial kernel on
S^{2}
. On the other hand the operator
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}(P+2)
, which appears in (
4.3.1
) with
p=1
and
m=0
,
acts according to

\displaystyle{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}

\displaystyle(P+2)(\varphi)_{B}:={\mathring{D}}^{A}(P+2)\varphi_{AB}

(388)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\geq 2\\
|m|\leq\ell\end{subarray}}\left(\tfrac{\ell(\ell+1)}{2}-1-2\right){\mathring{D}}^{A}\left(\varphi^{(\ell m)}_{\psi}\psi^{(\ell m)}_{AB}+\varphi^{(\ell m)}_{\phi}\phi^{(\ell m)}_{AB}\right)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\geq 2\\
|m|\leq\ell\end{subarray}}\underbrace{\left(\tfrac{\ell(\ell+1)}{2}-3\right)\sqrt{\tfrac{\ell(\ell+1)}{2}-1}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=0\text{ for }\ell=1,2\\
>0\text{ for }\ell>2\end{subarray}}\left(\varphi^{(\ell m)}_{\psi}E^{(\ell m)}_{B}+\varphi^{(\ell m)}_{\phi}H^{(\ell m)}_{B}\right)\,.

It follows that
the
L^{2}
-orthogonal

\big(\text{im}({\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}(P+2))\big)^{\perp}
of

\text{im}({\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}(P+2))

is spanned by conformal Killing vectors together with spherical harmonic vector fields with
\ell=2
. Subsequently, for any covector field
X_{A}\in L^{2}
the equation

{\mathring{D}}^{B}\left(P+2\right)\varphi_{AB}-\xi_{A}^{[\leq 2]}=X_{A}

(389)

admits a unique solution
with a symmetric traceless
2
-tensor
\varphi_{AB}
and a covector field
\xi_{A}^{[\leq 2]}
.

For a
{C^{2}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}}
gluing we need the operator

{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}(P^{2}+7{{\varepsilon}}P+10{{\varepsilon}})\,,

as determined from the coefficients of
\hat{\kappa}_{6}
in the formulae (
330
) for
\overset{(2,i)}{\chi}
.
On
S^{2}
, a calculation similar to that in (
388
) shows that its kernel consists of spherical harmonic tensors with
\ell=1,2,3
, which results in a cokernel spanned on spherical harmonic vectors with
\ell=1,2,3
.

C.5.2
Polynomials in
P

In this section we assume that
m=0
.

For
C^{k}
-gluing, the operator
\sum_{i=0}^{k}\overset{(k,i)}{\chi}_{k+4}P^{i}
appearing in (
4.3.1
) is of the form

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{k}\overset{(k,i)}{\chi}_{k+4}(r_{2})P^{i}={\hat{c}_{k}}\prod_{i=1}^{k}(P+{{\varepsilon}}a_{i})\,,\qquad a_{i}=\frac{1}{2}i(3+i)\,,

(390)

where

{\hat{c}_{k}}=\frac{1}{k!(k+3)}\,.

This can be verified by induction.

Indeed, when
k=1
this follows from (
326
)-(
327
) with
\hat{c}_{1}=1/4
. Using the recursion formula (
334
),
a straightforward calculation shows that the
\hat{\kappa}_{k+5}
component of the
(k+1)
-order coefficient are given by

\overset{(k+1,i)}{\chi}_{k+5}={c_{k}}\times\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}{{\varepsilon}}a_{k+1}\overset{(k,0)}{\chi}_{k+4}\,,&\hbox{$i=0$}\,,\\[5.0pt]
{{\varepsilon}}a_{k+1}\overset{(k,i)}{\chi}_{k+4}+\overset{(k,i-1)}{\chi}_{k+4}\,,&\hbox{$1\leq i\leq k$}\,,\\[5.0pt]
\overset{(k,k)}{\chi}_{k+4}\,,&\hbox{$i=k+1$}\,,\end{array}\right.

(391)

with
{c_{k}}=\frac{k+3}{(k+4)(k+1)}
. Therefore, assuming (
390
), the operator at order
k+1
is actually
r_{2}
-independent and reads,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{k+1}\overset{(k,i)}{\chi}_{k+5}(r_{2})P^{i}

\displaystyle={c_{k}}\sum_{i=0}^{k}\left(\overset{(k,i)}{\chi}_{k+4}P^{i+1}+{{\varepsilon}}a_{k+1}\overset{(k,i)}{\chi}_{k+4}P^{i}\right)

\displaystyle={c_{k}}\sum_{i=0}^{k}\big(\overset{(k,i)}{\chi}_{k+4}P^{i}\big)(P+{{\varepsilon}}a_{k+1})

\displaystyle={c_{k}}\hat{c}_{k}(P+{{\varepsilon}}a_{k+1})\prod_{i=1}^{k}(P+{{\varepsilon}}a_{i})

\displaystyle={\hat{c}_{k+1}}\prod_{i=1}^{k+1}(P+{{\varepsilon}}a_{i})\,,\qquad\text{with}\ {\hat{c}_{k+1}}={c_{k}}\hat{c}_{k}\,.

It thus follows from (
387
) that, on
S^{2}
, spherical harmonic vector fields with mode
\ell\geq 0
satisfying

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{k}\left(-\frac{1}{2}\ell(\ell+1)+1+a_{i}\right)=\frac{1}{2}\prod_{i=1}^{k}(1+i-\ell)(2+i+\ell)

(392)

belong to
\ker\left(\sum_{i=0}^{k}\overset{(k,i)}{\chi}_{k+4}P^{i}\right)
. The corresponding values of
\ell
are
\ell=2,...,k+1
.

For the remaining topologies, each of the operators

P+{{{{\varepsilon}}}}a_{i}

appearing in (
390
) is negative.
On
\mathbb{T}^{2}
its kernel, when acting on traceless tensors, is two-dimensional, consisting of covariantly constant tensors. Hence, in the toroidal case, the kernel of the left-hand side of (
390
) is also two-dimensional, which can be seen e.g. by a Fourier-series decomposition.

On higher genus manifolds
P+{{{{\varepsilon}}}}a_{i}
is strictly negative and therefore has no kernel. Hence so does the left-hand side of (
390
).

Appendix D
A trace identity

The aim of this appendix is to prove the following curious consequence of Bianchi identities:

\displaystyle r^{-1}\gamma^{AB}\delta G_{AB}=-\frac{1}{2}\gamma^{AB}\partial_{r}\delta R_{AB}+{\mathring{D}}^{A}\delta G_{rA}\,,

(393)

when
\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\beta=0
(i.e.,
\partial_{u}^{i}\delta G_{rr}=0
) for
i=0,1
.

For this, we start by noting that the operator
g^{AB}R_{AB}
is related to that appearing in (
69
), which can be seen as follows: From the definition (
48
) of the Einstein tensor
G_{\mu\nu}
and the Bondi parametrisation of the metric (
10
) we have

\displaystyle G_{ur}=\frac{1}{2}e^{2\beta}g^{rr}G_{rr}-U^{A}G_{rA}+\frac{1}{2}e^{2\beta}g^{AB}R_{AB}\,.

(394)

Now, from the linearisation of (
394
), when
\delta\beta=0
,
G_{rr}=0
, and
\partial_{u}^{i}\delta G_{rr}=0
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}^{AB}\delta R_{AB}

\displaystyle=r^{2}\delta G_{ur}\quad\implies\quad\frac{1}{2}{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}^{AB}\partial_{r}\delta R_{AB}=2r\delta G_{ur}+r^{2}\partial_{r}\delta G_{ur}\,,

(395)

and hence the identity (
393
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{A}\delta G_{rA}-\frac{1}{r}{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}^{AB}\delta G_{AB}=2r\delta G_{ur}+r^{2}\partial_{r}\delta G_{ur}\,.

(396)

Meanwhile, it follows from the divergence identity (
123
) with
\nu=r
that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{|g|}}\partial_{\mu}(\sqrt{|g|}\mycal E^{\mu}{}_{r})+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}(g^{\mu\rho})\mycal E_{\mu\rho}\,.

(397)

The linearisation of (
397
) with
\partial_{u}\delta G_{rr}=0
gives,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\partial_{r}(r^{2}\delta G_{ur})+\frac{1}{r^{2}}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\delta G_{rA}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\left(\frac{1}{r^{2}}{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}^{AB}\right)\delta G_{AB}\,,

(398)

and hence,

\displaystyle 2r\delta G_{ur}+r^{2}\partial_{r}\delta G_{ur}

\displaystyle={\mathring{D}}^{A}\delta G_{rA}-\frac{1}{r}{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}^{AB}\delta G_{AB}\,,

(399)

which agrees with (
396
).

Appendix E
Conserved charges for
\partial^{i}_{u}{\check{h}}_{uA}

The aim of this appendix is to present an alternative way to obtain the obstructions for gluing of
\partial^{i}_{u}h_{uA}
,
i=0,1
, in terms of gauge-dependent radial charges, as suggested to us by S. Czimek. We assume in what follows that

m=0\,.

Similar calculations can be done for
m\neq 0
and for higher derivatives, but we have not pursued these ideas any further.

E.1
{\check{h}}_{uA}

Recall (
104
):

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\bigg(3{\check{h}}_{uA}+r\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{uA}-r^{-3}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\bigg)=r^{-4}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\,.

(400)

In particular we obtain a collection of radially-conserved charges

\partial_{r}\underbrace{\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\pi^{A}\bigg(3{\check{h}}_{uA}+r\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{uA}\bigg)d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}}_{=:{\overset{[4,0]}{Q}}{}(\pi^{A})}=0\,,

(401)

where the
\pi^{A}
’s are conformal Killing vectors of
({\mathbf{S}},{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}})
.
Keeping in mind our assumption that
m=0
, under gauge transformations we have

3{\check{h}}_{uA}+r\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{uA}\mapsto 3{\check{h}}_{uA}+r\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{uA}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}{L_{1}}(\xi^{u})_{A}+3(\partial_{u}\xi_{A}+\alpha^{2}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi^{u})\,.

(402)

Hence the gauge-dependent radial charge
{\overset{[4,0]}{Q}}{}
transforms as,

{\overset{[4,0]}{Q}}{}(\pi^{A})\mapsto{\overset{[4,0]}{Q}}{}(\pi^{A})+3\int\pi^{A}\big(\partial_{u}\xi_{A}+\alpha^{2}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi^{u}\big)d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}\,,

(403)

since the
{L_{1}}
-gauge term in (
402
) has zero projection onto the
\pi^{A}
’s.

E.2
\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{uA}

Now, we hope to obtain a conserved radial charge involving
\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{uA}
. For this, take
\partial_{u}
of (
400
):

\partial_{r}\bigg(3\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{uA}+r\partial_{r}\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{uA}\bigg)=\frac{1}{r}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\partial_{r}\left(\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{AB}\right)\,.

(404)

We can then achieve the goal by rewriting the RHS into terms of the form
\partial_{r}(\cdot)\,+
(terms involving only
\partial_{r}^{i}h_{AB})
. For this, recall (
91
) with
\delta\beta=0
:

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\Big[r\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{AB}-\frac{1}{2}V\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{AB}-\frac{1}{2r}V{\check{h}}_{AB}-r\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\big[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\check{h}}_{uB}\big]\Big]

(405)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}(V/r){\check{h}}_{AB}-\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\big[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\check{h}}_{uB}\big]\,.

We wish to combine the above two equations by taking RHS of (
404
)
+

Ar^{c}\,\times{\mathring{D}}^{B}
(
405
) for some constants
A,c
such that all
the
\partial_{u}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\check{h}}_{AB}
terms collect into the form
B\partial_{r}(r^{a}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{AB})
, for some constants
B,a
. By a straightforward comparison of coefficients, we find that this is possible with
A=-\frac{1}{2}=-B
and
c=-2
,
a=-1
. Indeed, we have,

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\bigg(3\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{uA}

\displaystyle+r\partial_{r}\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{uA}\bigg)=\frac{1}{r}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\partial_{r}\left(\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{AB}\right)-\frac{1}{2r^{2}}\partial_{r}\big(r\partial_{u}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\check{h}}_{AB}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2r^{2}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\partial_{r}\Big[\frac{1}{2}V\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{AB}+\frac{1}{2r}V{\check{h}}_{AB}+r\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\big[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\check{h}}_{uB}\big]\Big]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{4r^{2}}\partial_{r}(V/r){\mathring{D}}^{B}{\check{h}}_{AB}+\frac{1}{2r^{2}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\big[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\check{h}}_{uB}\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{1}{r}\partial_{u}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\check{h}}_{AB}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2r^{2}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\partial_{r}\Big[\frac{1}{2}V\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{AB}+\frac{1}{2r}V{\check{h}}_{AB}+r\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\big[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\check{h}}_{uB}\big]\Big]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{4r^{2}}\partial_{r}(V/r){\mathring{D}}^{B}{\check{h}}_{AB}+\frac{1}{2r^{2}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\big[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\check{h}}_{uB}\big]\,.

We rewrite this as

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\bigg(3\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{uA}

\displaystyle+r\partial_{r}\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{uA}-\frac{1}{2r}\partial_{u}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\check{h}}_{AB}\bigg)

(406)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\quad\frac{1}{2r^{2}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\partial_{r}\Big[\frac{V}{2}\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{AB}+\frac{V}{2r}{\check{h}}_{AB}\Big]-\frac{1}{4r^{2}}\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{V}{r}\bigg){\mathring{D}}^{B}{\check{h}}_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2r^{2}}\partial_{r}\Big(r{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\big[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\check{h}}_{uB}\big]\Big)+\frac{1}{2r^{2}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\big[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\check{h}}_{uB}\big]\,.

Next, we perform a similar trick, by taking RHS of (
406
)
+\,D\,r^{d}\times{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}[
LHS of (
400
)
]
to collect all the

{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\check{h}}_{uA}]
terms into the form

\tilde{A}\partial_{r}\big(r^{\tilde{a}}\partial_{r}(r^{\tilde{b}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\check{h}}_{uA}])\big)

for some constants
D,d,\tilde{A},\tilde{a},\tilde{b}
. By power matching
and comparing coefficients,
we get
d=-1
and
D=-3/8=\tilde{A}=-1/\tilde{b}=1/\tilde{a}
. Explicitly, as a first step we write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2r^{2}}\partial_{r}\Big(r

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\big[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\check{h}}_{uB}\big]\Big)+\frac{1}{2r^{2}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\big[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\check{h}}_{uB}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{3}{8r}\underbrace{\partial_{r}\bigg(3{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\check{h}}_{uB}]+r\partial_{r}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\check{h}}_{uB}]\bigg)}_{=\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}\left({\mathring{D}}^{B}P{\check{h}}_{AB}\right)\text{ by \eqref{17I23.2}}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{3}{8}\partial_{r}\big(r^{-8/3}\partial_{r}(r^{8/3}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\check{h}}_{uB}])\big)\,.

Substituting this into (
406
) then gives,

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\bigg(3\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{uA}

\displaystyle+r\partial_{r}\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{uA}-\frac{1}{2r}\partial_{u}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\check{h}}_{AB}\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\quad\frac{1}{2r^{2}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\partial_{r}\Big[\frac{V}{2}\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{AB}+\frac{V}{2r}{\check{h}}_{AB}\Big]-\frac{1}{4r^{2}}\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{V}{r}\bigg){\mathring{D}}^{B}{\check{h}}_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{3}{8}\partial_{r}\big(r^{-\frac{8}{3}}\partial_{r}(r^{\frac{8}{3}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\check{h}}_{uB}])\big)+\frac{3}{8r^{2}}\partial_{r}\left({\mathring{D}}^{B}P{\check{h}}_{AB}\right)\,,

which we rewrite as

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\bigg(3\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{uA}

\displaystyle+r\partial_{r}\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{uA}-\frac{1}{2r}\partial_{u}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\check{h}}_{AB}+\frac{3}{8}r^{-\frac{8}{3}}\partial_{r}\big(r^{\frac{8}{3}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\check{h}}_{uB}]\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2r^{2}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\partial_{r}\Big[\frac{V}{2}\partial_{r}{\check{h}}_{AB}+\frac{V}{2r}{\check{h}}_{AB}\Big]-\frac{1}{4r^{2}}\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{V}{r}\bigg){\mathring{D}}^{B}{\check{h}}_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{3}{8r^{2}}\partial_{r}\left({\mathring{D}}^{B}P{\check{h}}_{AB}\right)

\displaystyle=\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{V}{4r^{5}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}+\frac{V}{4r^{4}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\partial_{r}h_{AB}+\frac{3}{8r^{4}}Ph_{AB}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg(\frac{9V}{4r^{6}}-\frac{3\partial_{r}V}{4r^{5}}\bigg){\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}+\frac{3}{4r^{5}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}Ph_{AB}\,,

or equivalently,

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\bigg(3\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{uA}+r\partial_{r}\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{uA}-\frac{1}{2r}\partial_{u}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\check{h}}_{AB}+\frac{3}{8}r^{-\frac{8}{3}}\partial_{r}\big(r^{\frac{8}{3}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\check{h}}_{uB}]\big)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{V}{4r^{5}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}-\frac{V}{4r^{4}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\partial_{r}h_{AB}-\frac{3}{8r^{4}}Ph_{AB}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{3}{4r^{5}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\bigg(\underbrace{\frac{3V}{r}-\partial_{r}V}_{=2{{\varepsilon}}}+P\bigg)h_{AB}\,.

(407)

Let us denote by,

\displaystyle{\overset{[1]}{q}}{}_{A}:=

\displaystyle 3\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{uA}+r\partial_{r}\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{uA}-\frac{1}{2r}\partial_{u}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\check{h}}_{AB}+\frac{3}{8}r^{-\frac{8}{3}}\partial_{r}\big(r^{\frac{8}{3}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\check{h}}_{uB}]\big)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{V}{4r^{5}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}-\frac{V}{4r^{4}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\partial_{r}h_{AB}-\frac{3}{8r^{4}}Ph_{AB}\,.

(408)

Let
\mu_{A}\in\coker({\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}(P+2{{\varepsilon}}))
. Equation (
407
) gives

\displaystyle\partial_{r}{\overset{[5]}{Q}}{}(\mu^{A})=0,

(409)

where

\displaystyle{\overset{[5]}{Q}}{}(\mu^{A})

\displaystyle:=\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\mu^{A}{\overset{[1]}{q}}{}_{A}d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}\,.

(410)

We have thus found a gauge-dependent radial charge involving
\partial_{u}{\check{h}}_{uA}
. From the analysis in Appendix
C.5.2
, on
S^{2}
,
\coker({\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}(P+2{{\varepsilon}}))
is the
16
-dimensional space of
\ell=1
and
\ell=2
spherical harmonic vectors; on
\mathbb{T}^{2}
,
\coker({\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}(P+2{{\varepsilon}}))
is the two dimensional space of covariantly constant vectors; on negatively curved manifolds of higher genus,
\coker({\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}(P+2{{\varepsilon}}))
is trivial. Note that
\coker({\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}(P+2{{\varepsilon}}))
corresponds exactly to the space of obstructions for solving (
4.3.1
) with
p=1
(compare also (
388
)).

Under gauge transformations,
{\overset{[1]}{q}}{}_{A}
transforms as

\displaystyle{\overset{[1]}{q}}{}_{A}

\displaystyle\mapsto{\overset{[1]}{q}}{}_{A}-\frac{{{\varepsilon}}}{r^{3}}{L_{1}}(\xi^{u})_{A}+\frac{3\alpha^{2}}{2}({\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{B}+{\mathring{D}}^{B}C(\xi)_{AB})

\displaystyle\quad+3\partial_{u}^{2}\xi_{A}-\frac{3}{4r^{2}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}(2{{\varepsilon}}+P)C(\xi)_{AB}\,.

Note that the
r
-dependent gauge terms in this equation vanish upon projection onto
\mu^{A}
. Hence, the charge
{\overset{[5]}{Q}}{}
transforms as,

\displaystyle{\overset{[5]}{Q}}{}(\mu^{A})\mapsto{\overset{[5]}{Q}}{}(\mu^{A})+3\int_{{\mathbf{S}}}\mu^{A}\bigg(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{2}({\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{B}+{\mathring{D}}^{B}C(\xi)_{AB})+\partial_{u}^{2}\xi_{A}\bigg)d\mu_{{\mathring{{\gamma}}}}\,.

(411)

Appendix F
An identity

One way of ensuring continuity of
\partial_{u}^{{p}}{\check{h}}_{uA}
and

\partial_{u}^{p}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}
at
r_{2}
is through the fields
\{{\overset{[2p+4]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}_{AB},\partial_{u}^{p+1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{C}^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]}\}
and the gauge field
\partial_{u}^{p}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{C}^{[{{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}]}
respectively.
This turns out not to be convenient from the perspective of nonlinear theory, as it involves losses of derivatives, which is avoided by the argument presented in the main body of the paper. We present this alternative calculation here as it involves some unexpected identities which might be useful for further applications.

We thus revisit (
229
).
For the sake of the induction here we assume that the fields

{\overset{[\ell]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}_{AB}
for
\ell\leq 2p+2
are known
and collect them, together with
-\partial_{u}^{p}{\check{h}}_{uA}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}
, into a new term
\overset{({p})}{\hat{\tilde{X}}}_{A}
, allowing us to rewrite
the
L^{2}
-projections on
CKV
and
{{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}
of
(
229
) respectively as

\displaystyle\partial_{u}^{p+1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]}

\displaystyle=\overset{({p})}{\hat{\tilde{X}}}{}^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]}_{A}\,,

(412)

\displaystyle-\overset{({p},0)}{\chi}_{2p+4}(r_{2})

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\overset{[2p+4]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}_{AB}=-\partial_{u}^{{p}+1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}^{[{{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}]}_{A}+\overset{({p})}{\hat{\tilde{X}}}{{}^{[{{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}]}_{A}}

(413)

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathring{D}}^{B}(\overset{({p},0)}{\chi}_{2p+3}(r_{2})+\overset{({p},1)}{\chi}_{2p+3}(r_{2})P){\overset{[2p+3]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}_{AB}\,.

An argument identical to that below (
214
) shows that,
both on
S^{2}
and
\mathbb{T}^{2}
,
(
412
) determines
\partial_{u}^{p+1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]}
uniquely in terms of
\overset{({p})}{\hat{\tilde{X}}}{{}^{[{{\text{CKV}}}]}_{A}}
while (
413
) determines
{\overset{[2p+4]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}
uniquely in terms of
\overset{({p})}{\hat{\tilde{X}}}{{}^{[{{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}]}_{A}}
,
{\overset{[2p+3]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}_{AB}
and
\partial_{u}^{p+1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}]}
; the fields
{\overset{[2p+3]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}_{AB}
and
\partial_{u}^{p+1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}]}
remain free to use for
other gluing equations. On negatively curved sections with higher genus, (
413
) determines
{\overset{[2p+4]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}_{AB}
in terms of
\overset{({p})}{\hat{\tilde{X}}}_{A}
,
{\overset{[2p+3]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}_{AB}
and
\partial_{u}^{p+1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}^{[{{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}]}=\partial_{u}^{p+1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}
, with the fields
{\overset{[2p+3]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}_{AB}
and
\partial_{u}^{p+1}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{A}

remaining free.

Next, in order to take into account the dependence of
{\overset{[2p+2]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}
and
{\overset{[2p+1]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}
upon
\partial_{u}^{p}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{C}^{[{{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}]}
,
we consider the equation obtained by acting with
{\operatorname{\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,}\!
on (
285
).
There occur some miraculous cancellations, which are likely to have some simple origin:

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{A}

\displaystyle\partial^{p}_{u}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}|_{\tilde{{\mathbf{S}}}_{2}}={\mathring{D}}^{A}\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\Psi}}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}_{AB}(r_{2},x^{A})-2r_{2}^{2}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}\partial_{u}^{p}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}_{B}^{[{{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}]}]

(414)

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathring{D}}^{A}(\overset{({p},0)}{\psi}_{2p+2}(r_{2})+\overset{({p},1)}{\psi}_{2p+2}(r_{2})P){\overset{[2p+2]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathring{D}}^{A}(\overset{({p},0)}{\psi}_{2p+1}(r_{2})+\overset{({p},1)}{\psi}_{2p+1}(r_{2})P+\overset{({p},2)}{\psi}_{2p+1}(r_{2})P^{2}){\overset{[2p+1]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}_{AB}

\displaystyle={\mathring{D}}^{A}\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\Psi}}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}_{AB}(r_{2},x^{A})-2r_{2}^{2}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}\partial_{u}^{p}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}_{B}^{[{{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}]}]

\displaystyle\quad+\overset{({p},0)}{\psi}_{2p+2}/\overset{({p-1},0)}{\chi}_{2p+2}\,\partial_{u}^{p}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{B}^{[{{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}]}

\displaystyle\quad+\overset{({p},1)}{\psi}_{2p+2}/\overset{({p-1},0)}{\chi}_{2p+2}\,{\mathring{D}}^{A}C(\partial_{u}^{p}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}^{[{{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}]})_{AB}

\displaystyle={\mathring{D}}^{A}\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\Psi}}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}_{AB}(r_{2},x^{A})-3mr_{2}\partial_{u}^{p}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{B}^{[{{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}]}\,,

where in the second equality we made use of the expression for
{\overset{[2p+2]}{\hat{\varphi}}}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}_{AB}
from (
413
) at order
(p-1)
,
while the last equality uses (
346
)-(
349
), Appendix
B
.
Thus, continuity of
\partial_{u}^{p}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}
can be achieved by solving (
414
) for
\partial_{u}^{p}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{C}^{[{{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}]}
:

\displaystyle 3mr_{2}\partial_{u}^{p}{\overset{(2)}{{\xi}}}{}{}_{B}^{[{{\text{CKV}^{\perp}}}]}

\displaystyle=-{\mathring{D}}^{A}\partial^{p}_{u}{h}_{AB}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}|_{\tilde{{\mathbf{S}}}_{2}}+{\mathring{D}}^{A}\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\Psi}}{}^{[{{\text{TT}}}^{\perp}]}_{AB}(r_{2},x^{A})\,.

(415)

In fact, Lemma
B.2
, p.
B.2
shows that we have the factorisation

\displaystyle\overset{({p-1},0)}{\chi}_{2p+2}

\displaystyle(r_{2})\big(\overset{({p},0)}{\psi}_{2p+1}(r_{2})+\overset{({p},1)}{\psi}_{2p+1}(r_{2})P+\overset{({p},2)}{\psi}_{2p+1}(r_{2})P^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip-327.20668pt=

\displaystyle\big(\overset{({p},0)}{\psi}_{2p+2}(r_{2})+\overset{({p},1)}{\psi}_{2p+2}(r_{2})P\big)\big(\overset{({p-1},0)}{\chi}_{2p+1}(r_{2})+\overset{({p-1},1)}{\chi}_{2p+1}(r_{2})P\big)\,.
